Part One: The Fallen

PROLOGUE

Rocks Stones bottles and fire bombs were being hurled through the Crisp Night air along with rapid gunfire as the 1972 election came to an end, raining in Michael Manley the new supreme prime minister of Jamaica. The crowd cheered as bullets echoed through the dark night sky while tires bullets and cars burned in downtown Kingston. Off of the shadows of the building. Mike McCormick and his crew of cohorts held out their young hands as the dreadlocked man with one eye placed the crumbled $50.00 bill in each of their bloodied gasoline stained filthy little hands for a job well done for firebombing the Jamaica labor party, (JLP) ballot boxes. Preventing them from casting their votes clearing the way for Manley's "People's National Party" (PNP) to come out victorious as Jamaicans lead party exalted prime minister.
Shortly thereafter young Mike found himself being dragged down the streets of Kingston by his mother's hand which were being lead by two of Jamaica's most vicious gunmen giving them safe passage through both of the areas most deadliest political party lines "PNP" (JLB). Trina was informed of the Kingston McGregor gully area which had taken its name from the drainage canal that ran through its veins connecting to its heart lines along the Western flank of Wareika Hill.
The gully has stayed away from the political warfare of the island until Manley set his sights on the area funneling money into the area for a ministry of works to clean up and rebuild the gully. Too many complaints about young Mike's activity participating in the political carnage forced Trina to make a great escape from the island to New York City with young Mike in tow.
Fast forward to the 1980 election which would mark it's bloody stain in Jamaica's history as the bloodiest election of all time with Seaga rising as Jamaica's new prime minister outing Manley and his PNP Party catapulting the (JLP) to the top of the food chain on the island forcing PNP members and Affiliates to migrate to America many to settle in Miami and New York City.
CHAPTER ONE
THE COME-UP

Damn, I wonder if Duke is home," he said quietly to himself, leaving the basement and preparing to go to his neighbor, Greg's house. He needed to get a ride over to the city. He and Greg got along well and they regularly lent a helping hand to one another when it was needed.
Luckily Greg was home with his girl Keenya, and when Mike explained what he needed done, Greg easily agreed to assist him. The three walked out to Greg's charcoal gray Pontiac Grand Prix and Greg handed the car keys to Keenya so that she could do the driving. The trip wasn't very long because as soon as they reached the New York City end of the George Washington Bridge, Mike caught sight of one of his partners and asked to be let out there. He slipped Greg a twenty, thanked him for the ride and exited the car.
"Yo, Bob!" Mike exclaimed, stepping quickly down the sidewalk to catch up.
Bob was Mike's soon-to-be baby mother's older brother. He and Bob had hit it off from the beginning and remained close. In the past they had partnered up to commit various crimes from assault, to drug dealing and robbery, on up to murder.
As Bob turned to see who was calling his name, he eased his hand to his lower back where he usually kept a 38" Special tucked in his waistline.
That's when he realized he had left his gun at home. He wasn't simply paranoid; he knew to be on his P's and Q's in the streets because of the life he led. When he realized it was Mike he exhaled heavily, breathing out the tension that had quickly overcome him moments before he recognized his partner.
"Yo, son wus’up!" He said as he gave Mike a pound, their hands making a loud clapping sound as they met. They proceeded walking down Broadway and chatting about Bob's sister Kim and how she was due any day to have Mike's baby. Bob told Mike that Kim needed some money to buy a crib, food and baby supplies.
"Tell her I'll come through and hit her off with something ." Mike responded casually. Bob told him it was all good and they continued on in brief silence as Bob contemplated his plans for that night. He knew Mike would be down to participate in a good money making move so he looked over at Mike and spoke quietly to be certain that no one could overhear him.
"Whatcha got going on tonight kid?"
"Not too much of nothing. I'm just tryna reach for some coke and lay back, ya’ know what I mean?"
"Yeah, I'm diggin’ it." Bob said, moving closer to Mike. "I think I can help you out, homeboy."
Mike looked over at his partner and studied his face. Experience with Bob had taught Mike that "help" was Bob's personal description for some type of drama he had sat back and connived with. Within minutes he found out that his suspicions were right on the money.
"Oh, yeah?" He asked Bob while looking him in his face not wanting to miss a word of his response. "What kind of help did you get? You know I'm tryna get on." Mike finished.
Bob smiled big. His partner was about to pull through for him again. He knew he could count on Mike to be there for him. In the past Mike had always been game to make moves even when he didn't really want to, and Bob was there for him likewise to show his appreciation and love.
"Check this out, right... I got a smooth lick setup for tonight. I'm talkin’ at least twenty bricks and a hun’ed-thousand cash. You down with it?"
Mike wasn't low on money. He had just put in work out in New Jersey the night before. All he wanted to do now was get more crack and pick up a gold nameplate for another one of his baby mother's sisters. Her birthday was coming up and he had promised her that she would get the gift. He had planned a nice quiet evening at his grandmother's house but it was evident that his evening would be everything but quiet. He refused to let his partner down.
"Is it Trump tight?" Mike asked, wanting confirmation that Bob had studied their intended mark thoroughly.
"I wouldn't fuck wit’ it any other way." Bob replied with the confidence of a well-experienced car salesman.
"You got a gun?" Mike asked since he always kept his with him.
"Naw." Bob answered. "But one of my boys in the projects will hook me up. Cool?"
"Yeah, I'm game." Mike said, hailing a cab as it rolled by. "We can handle this tonight."
"A’ight, bet." Bob responded as he followed Mike into the cab.
Mike and Bob went to the Polo Grounds Projects on 8th Avenue to get an extra gun. Once they were properly strapped up they immediately made their way up the hill, back to Washington Heights, toward the targeted coke spot. Mike sat back in the cab relaxing and vibing off of the Marvin Gaye track playing at low volume in the cab's tape deck. Bob rocked his head to the music of Gaye's hit "Let's get it on," and the cabdriver rocked his head likewise while tapping his fingers on the steering wheel along with the rhythm. While attempting to release all excess thoughts from his mind so that he could concentrate on the robbery about to go down, Mike had no control over the rush of flashbacks that took over his entire frame of mind while Marvin Gaye filled the cab’s passenger cabin. The deeper the song got the more Mike's thoughts slipped into replays of incidents that contributed to him becoming the scandalous person he had become. While looking out the window at the raindrops, he could feel the flutters in his stomach. This was not caused by fear but by the heat of the moment. There had been many such moments before this, and there was bound to be more after this. To Mike it was all just a part of his survival.
"The last time was hectic as a muthafucka," he thought to himself, periodically glancing over at his partner. "We both could've gotten our asses killed. I wonder if we'll make it out alive tonight. I know I am off on some foul shit but now I got babies coming and I need to raise them up. I hope this nigga got everything worked out right. Shit, I hope we both don't fuck around and get smoked like we almost did the last time. Damn."
"Yo, nigga!" Bob interrupted Mike's trance. "You alright, homey? You all zoned out and shit."
"Yeah, I'm ight,” Mike said, coming back to the reality at hand. "I was just thinking about something’."
"Well squash that for a few minutes, my nigga, we're here. Let’s go handle this lick, kid."
"Ight, lets roll," Mike said as he paid the driver and slid out of the cab. "Yo, we ain't leavin’ unless we get it all. Cash, drugs plus we smokin’ them kids, feel me?!" Mike commented coldly to Bob who was anxious to get busy.
"You got that, son. Shit, I ain't come all the way over here to leave empty handed or half paid. We're gonna break these muthafuckas," Bob responded in an equally chilling tone.
"Ight," Mike said, pulling out a black Sig Sauer P226 9-millimeter pistol and cocking it. Bob brandished the 38. Special he’d picked up from the projects, then the two made their way to the coke spot, which was on the heavily trafficked block of 160th Street between Amsterdam Avenue and Broadway.
Bob knew the people so he knocked four times and a heavy Spanish accent called out.
"Yo, who is it!?" the unseen Latino called out before opening the door. "It's me, Bob!" Bob answered as if today was just a normal day. The door swung open and two Spanish dudes said, speaking in near perfect unison "Mi amigo! Enter, enter!" They had been counting money waiting for their re-up. They knew Bob so they trusted him. Not even a full minute after they entered the spot and Mike looked around quickly to observe all entrances and exits, he yanked up his pistol, pointing it at the Spanish dudes.
"A'ight," Mike said loudly. "You muthafuckas know what it is! Where the fuck is the dope at,
The two Spanish dudes were scared to death and started begging for their lives to be spared claiming they have no coke. All the while, Mike is putting all their money where the lining should be, inside his Army fatigue jacket. He had removed the lining from the jacket especially for robberies.
"We got no coke, we got no coke!" one of the Spanish dudes cried out. The other followed up, "All we have is the eighty-thousand dollars you just took, Pa-Pa. We was waiting for coke to come, I swear we have none just let us live. Please!"
"A’ight, fuck it," Bob said looking over at Mike. "We got more than we expected, son, let's roll out!"
"Nah, fuck that!" Mike replied. "They got dope and if they don't run it they gettin’ smoked tonight!"
The Spanish kid in front of Mike swallowed hard and began to sweat as his eyes bulged out. "No, no, we got no coke, Pa-Pa I swear it on my kids! We got no coke!"
Mike raised his arm high and smacked the other kid with his pistol.
"Ahh, shit!" The kid cried. "Shut the fuck up, bitch!" Mike growled. Both of the victims reclined deeper into the ragged couch they were sitting on. They were holding their arms over their faces attempting to brace themselves in anticipation of more blows. They both were speaking at once sticking to their story that they had no drugs to give.
"Get the fuck up!" Mike said to them both. "We goin’ up on the roof and if y’all don't do some talkin', both of y’all gettin' smoked, you understand?!"
"Si, si, we understand," one of them responded. They both got up and walked ahead of Mike and Bob to the roof. Entering the roof, Mike made his way over to the corner and said, "Now, who's gonna be the first to die, muthafuckas?!"
One of them began to weep and beg, "Please, mi amigo, please, we have no cocaine. We really don't have none, just let us live, please." Mike looked him squarely in the face.
"So that's how you want it?" he asked, and then he shot three bullets into his terrified victim’s face. The impact of the bullets ripped through the man's cheek and tore off half of the back of his head. The large bloody piece of scalp flung from his head like a cork popping out of a shaken bottle of cheap champagne. The hairy piece of flesh extracted from this once living man landed a few inches away from his twitching hand and it appeared from looking at the grotesque scene that the dead man was actually reaching out for this useless piece of meat and skull. The other captive appeared to be on the verge of a stroke, looking down at his for damn sure dead friend.
"Oh shit, man!" he moaned loudly through his own tears.
Mike wasn't moved at all. He just wanted the coke if it was there and as far as he could tell it wasn't his wickedness that killed the kid laying on the roof, it was his victim’s own lack of ability to follow simple directions that got him smoked. The other kid knew he was next to die and he was frantically trying to think fast of a way out of this predicament.
"Blow this muthafucka," Mike said to Bob who had the skinny shaking man at gunpoint, "…and don't get no blood on you, Son."
"Bet," Bob replied. "No, no!" the man pleaded as profuse sweat mixed with his tears streamed down his pale face. "I give you ‘pedico’ now, just don't shoot me!
Just then there was noise and the sound of footsteps coming up the stairs. Bob ducked down as three shadowy figures came out of the building’s roof entrance. Mike snatched up the terrified man by the neck using him as a human shield. A hail of gunshots began to ring out on the roof. All the men except Mike and his hostage were diving, rolling, and firing their weapons, trying hard to kill one another.
Mike's hostage got hit in the left side and his body went limp but Mike still held him up and continued shooting. Then Mike got hit in his left side, inches from his heart. The impact of the gunshot pushed him back some and made it hard for him to hold his hostage steady as he aimed his pistol in the direction where the gunshot came from. He hit the large Hispanic man, who had shot him, in the chest and at the same time his hostage got hit in his right side. His hostage was bleeding from the mouth as his eyes rolled back in his head and his convulsing body began to slip from Mike’s grip. Mike snapped his neck letting him fall all the way down then he crawled over to where Bob was laying on the roof trying to reload his revolver.
The two remaining men made their way closer as Mike fired his weapon at them trying to cover Bob. He was beginning to worry because he was losing a lot of blood from the bullet he took in his side and he knew he didn't have many bullets left in his own weapon.
"Yo son, I'm hit. Hurry up and reload that shit, I'm bleeding bad!"
"We gotta get these fools and get outta here before the po’ come," Bob answered, pushing the last bullet into the revolver. One of the men came within ten feet of where he and Mike lay and started firing again. Bob fired back and Mike shot twice hitting the first man low in the stomach. He looked over at Bob because he wanted to let him know that if they were going to leave alive the time was then. What he saw was his partner laid face down on the roof with blood spurting out of the back of his head.
Mike had no time to think. He knew there was at least one more man on the roof who would kill him so he crawled over to the edge of the roof. He tried to stand up but he was weak and as he got up, more gunshots came from behind him. A bullet hit him dead in the ass and the force of the bullet along with the surprise of being hit again caused Mike to tumble over the side of the roof. His body landed on the canopy of a storefront and then rolled into a fruit stand, which broke his fall. The combination of blood loss and the impact of falling from the roof caused Mike to pass out.
There was a large crowd gathered around as the two police officers who responded to the dispatcher's call were trying to get answers from Mike who was only partially conscious. It took much effort for Mike to speak so he just raised his arm pointing out towards the open doorway.
"The building," he whispered weakly.
Observing that Mike was bleeding badly and appeared to be shot, the officer talking to him called out to his partner.
"This man is bleeding pretty bad, I think he's been shot. I need you to stay down here with him and call for backup. He pointed at that building right there so I'm going in.”
"Be careful in there," the other officer responded.
The first officer took his service revolver, a shiny, black 38. Police Special, from its holster and ran toward the open doorway Mike had pointed to. After calling the incident in, the second officer came back to the fruit stand where Mike still lay. Mike was unconscious, and appeared to be dead. The officer was apparently more concerned with his partner than he was about this young, bloody, black creature who was ruining someone's fruits and vegetables with his animal blood. He placed his thumb and index finger against Mike's throat checking for vital signs even though he was already almost certain that Mike was dead. He didn't feel a pulse so he stepped back and seemed to weigh his options in his mind. "I can either stay out here wasting time watching this dead nigger," he said to himself, “Or I can go back my partner up until the others get here to scrape this black bastard out of this fruit stand.”
Twenty seconds later the second officer was running towards the open doorway after his partner. This poor nigger was dead so it was senseless to watch him and risk his partner being harmed by whoever killed this thing. He was black and he was dead. He had served his purpose in life. Nothing.

CHAPTER TWO
THE BIG ESCAPE

 Bleeding like a punctured pig and feeling weaker than a crack baby, Mike languished in the produce, suffering from the two crucial gunshots he received. His thoughts were slightly hazy from the blood loss so he couldn't remember exactly what had happened. All he knew was that he could not let the police take him into custody. He looked around and noticed the large crowd and the deserted police squad car on the curb. He also heard the sounds of approaching squad cars coming from the distance. He knew he had to get away from the scene however he could because if he was caught there in his position he would definitely be taken away and locked up for a very long time, if not forever.
He was in excruciating pain as he moved to the edge of the stand, careful to avoid putting pressure on his wounded side. He was weak on his feet but still able to stumble out to the corner. Mike leaned up against the building at the corner to gather some strength. Clutching his side he stared out to the middle of the street attempting to cross to the other side. His legs went out from under him as he passed out in the middle of the street. The last thing Mike saw before falling unconscious was a huge N.Y.C. transit bus screeching hard on its brakes to avoid slamming into him. Before his mind went completely blank and before the bus came to a halt, expecting to be hit, Mike wondered to himself if he would go to heaven or hell. "Wherever you send me, Lord, if it's hellfire, I deserve it, if it's the golden streets of heaven I surely appreciate it. Either way just forgive me for all the shit I done down here," Mike continued to think to himself with the bus still coming toward him, then, suddenly, he was unconscious again. Mike came to, in front of the emergency room being thrown out of a cab by a cabdriver he didn't recognize. He passed out again and when he came back to consciousness he was on a stretcher with doctors sticking long wooden Q-tips in his bullet holes to see if the bullets were still inside of him while he was laying on his back. One doctor looked at the other and said, “He's not gonna make it. There's just nothing we can do for him. If we try to cut the bullets out we'll kill him, and if we leave them in there, they'll kill him on their own. All we can do is put the clothes we cut off him in a bag and tag it for the morgue."
"Okay, Doctor Swayne," the other doctor said, "What about this bloody jacket he was wearing?"
"Put it in the bag with his personal belongings." "It's quite heavy." "That's because he bled a lot and that jacket absorbed it all. If no one claims the body, the bag will have to be burned." They pushed the stretcher in the corner of the room. Doctor Swayne turned to the other doctor, "Don't forget to check on Ms. Collins with the burns, okay?"
"Okay, Doctor Swayne."
Mike was slowly drifting out of consciousness again. All he could see was blackness. Over and over again, the doctor's words played themselves in his mind..."He's not going to make it...don't forget to check on Ms. Collins with the burns...he's not going to make it."
Mike’s spirit and his will to live were too strong and he refused to just lay there and die as the doctors had said he would. With a surprising burst of strength he lifted himself from the stretcher and walked into the emergency room with blood dried up on his body. He looked like a victim in a horror film and everybody in the waiting room began to scream and move away so he couldn't touch them and some were heard making retching sounds, followed by the sound of their stomach contents slapping the floor. The doctors rushed to him and took him to a room upstairs on the fifteenth floor. They were still refusing to remove the bullets from his body because they were sure that he wouldn't live to see the next sunrise. They had no idea if Mike would survive.
A familiar voice brought Mike closer to consciousness. The voice belonged to his baby's mother, Samone. He had gone out to get a gift for her sister before bumping into Bob. He used all of his strength to sit up so that he could talk to her.
"How did you know I was here?" Mike asked her. She wiped tears from her eyes before she spoke. "The hospital called us just now from a receipt in your pocket. Matter of fact I don't see how they read the number with all that blood on the receipt, and neither could the doctor.” Samone’s mother was with her also and Mike watched her looking down at him with tears in her eyes. There was something bothering Mike and he couldn't help speaking up.
"Did they find Bob? Is he going to make it?'" he asked the woman in front of him. They both looked down at the floor remaining silent. Right then and there Mike knew what time it was and made up his mind about what he had to do. Real niggas represent real niggas to the fullest extent, till death do us part.
Samone and her mother were still silent when a nurse walked into the room holding a mirror. The nurse handed the mirror over to Mike who took it and looked at himself observing his sickeningly ashen face. He looked like a corpse. The horrid sight caused him to faint.
Frantically, the nurse ran around the stretcher where Mike and checked him for a pulse.
"Is he going to be okay?!" Samone's mother asked as tears streamed down her face, "Please don't let him die!"
"Where the fuck am I?!" he blurted out, surprised and slightly confused by the scene around him.
"Just relax before you hurt yourself more. You're at the hospital eight blocks from where you got shot, now just relax," the nurse said to him.
"Stand me up!” Mike said out loud, as he leaned up, ready to get off the bed. Everybody in the room looked at each other with disbelief in their eyes. Samone spoke up. “The doctors said that you won’t live through this and even if you do you won't be able to walk again baby," she whispered in Mike’s ear. He exploded.
"FUCK THEM DOCTORS! I GOT THINGS TO DO! STAND ME THE FUCK UP NOW!!" he erupted in frustration.
"Okay, okay!" Samone reasoned, "Baby, just calm down." The nurse, Samone, and her mother helped Mike out of bed and with their assistance he stood up. "Let me the fuck up..." he yelled at them and they obeyed him. From all the pressure on his legs Mike passed out. His eyes rolled back in his head. They all grabbed him before he could hit the floor, and then they put him back on his bed.
When Mike came to, everyone in the room was crying. He became furious, "Get the fuck out!" he snapped. "All of y’all!"
Samone and her mother left but the nurse stayed with him. Samone had given Mike a small package containing 5 grams of cocaine. The nurse saw the transaction and knew what it was because she used it herself at times.
She watched as Mike put the coke in his bullet holes to numb the pain. "Can I have some?" she asked.
"Yeah, if you help me get out of here," he said. "What do you want me to do?" "Get me a wheelchair and a cab and just help me the fuck up out of here.” “Why do you want to leave here so bad? You can't walk.” "Yo, I just gotta leave, now are you going to help me or not? One way or the other I'm gettin' outta here." "Okay, I hear you," she said, "Just don't hurt yourself more, doing it." When the nurse left the room Mike laid back thinking to himself. He knew he had to leave the hospital. Here he was only eight blocks away from the spot where he had murdered several people. It would be a matter of time before their people found out what happened and what hospital he was in. This would surely be the first one they would check.
The nurse came back pushing a wheelchair.
"Come on. I'm going to help you up, there's a cab down in the lot. He said he was here to see somebody anyway so we don’t have to rush since he's not going anywhere yet," she said. The nurse did as much as she could to clean Mike up so they wouldn't draw too much attention as they exited. With great effort he got into the wheelchair and they made their way to the cab. The nurse helped him into the back of the cab and told Mike that he could keep the wheelchair since she had stolen it from the supply room anyway.
"You can have this plus more if you stay with me for a whole day and help me learn to walk again."
"But you have a bullet in each leg, your ass and your back!" the nurse said to him with her eyes wide. "And you will never walk again. What can I do?"
"Yo, fuck what that fake-ass doctor said! I ain't tryna hear none of that bullshit!"
"Do I still get the coke if you don't get to walk in twenty four hours?"
"Yeah, you got that comin’, a'ight," he stated. Then off they went to the Bronx.

CHAPTER THREE
THE HERB GATES, THE CAB DRIVER & THE NURSE

The cab rolled down Garden Street in the South Bronx. Mike sat up a little observing the surroundings of this familiar area. This was his stomping grounds and place of business. There was a big deserted and unkempt lot that was turned into a makeshift playground for the kids in the area. All of the equipment used to make this playground was trash donated by the scrap yard from nearby Hunt's Point, an industrial section filled with warehouses and prostitutes. There were tire swings, tire obstacle courses, a sliding board supported by tires, and even a tire tree house. This was as good as it got for these poor kids and they loved their little playground because it was all that they had. This pathetic place of fun and games for children was located directly across the street from an old abandoned building. This building looked as if it had been hit by a bomb and would collapse at any minute.
All of its windows were shattered and boarded over. Pieces of the building's walls were missing in big slabs and the concrete that once made up those missing sections were scattered all around the outside of the building.
"Stop right here," Mike said to the cabdriver. "At this building?" "Yeah," Mike spat back, preparing to get out of the cab. "Help me up," he commanded the nurse who was sitting quietly beside him in the back seat. The cab driver got out of the car and helped the nurse give Mike support as he struggled to stand up on his weakened legs. They helped him into the old building and up the stairs to an apartment that surprised both of them so much that they nearly let Mike drop to the floor from their astonishment.
The decor inside this spacious apartment was worth more than the building itself. The floor was covered from wall to wall with plush, wine red carpeting. The sitting chair, love seat, and couch were all made of butter soft leather. There was a sixty inch projector screen dominating one wall and a state of the art high-tech entertainment center filled with audio equipment covering another. Embedded into the wall behind the leather couch was a huge two hundred and fifty gallon fish aquarium filled with healthy, exotic, tropical fish, including several piranhas. From a first look, a person would wonder how the other fish survived in the same aquarium as the piranhas. But a closer look revealed that the huge aquarium actually had a large, oval shaped tank made of Plexiglas inside of it. With this effect, the piranhas could swim all around the other fish inside of the monstrous tank, and the non-carnivorous fish could do likewise and not be eaten. To the left of the aquarium was a gun rack. It was stocked with high-powered assault rifles and handguns with silencers, and was at arm’s reach from the couch.
"Take me over to the sofa," Mike said, regaining the attention of his company. They complied. Mike sat down on the couch and winced from the pain that raced up his back from the bullet wound in his ass.
"Yo," he called out to the nurse. "Reach behind this couch, there's a bag back there, get it for me."
"Okay." She replied. She bent over and reached behind the couch retrieving a black and red Adidas gym bag.
The cab driver stood near the sitting chair looking at the bag in the nurse's hand as she handed it to Mike. As he unzipped it the cab driver stated that he had to leave.
"How much do you make in a day? Mike asked him, pulling a stack of money out of the gym bag.
"About five hundred," the cabdriver responded.
"I'll give you a thousand for twenty four hours plus a bonus if you take me downtown back to Harlem on Monday night. Is that cool?"
"Hell yeah! That's cool!" the driver answered.
Looking back and forth from one man to the other, the nurse spoke up. "What am I going to get?!" She asked, not wanting to impose or sound greedy, but at the same time not wanting to be left out.
Let me give you the same as long as you help me the best you can and don't be up in here faking, know what I mean?"
"Yeah, I hear you. I'll do what I can, whatever you need me to do.”
It was Saturday night, just twelve hours after Mike's unfortunate ordeal at the coke spot where his number one partner lost his life. Mike's head was spinning and all he could think of was how Bob looked lying face down on the roof bleeding. He had lost many people close to him but none were as close to him as Bob. He made a silent promise to himself and to his dead partner that his death would be avenged. Not in his entire life had Mike ever broken a promise.
The weekend seemed to go on forever. Mike showed the nurse how badly he wanted to walk again by remaining persistent and following all of her orders. Instead of congratulating or babying Mike when he first stood up on his own late Saturday night, the nurse took on the role of a sexy drill sergeant, telling him the sky's the limit, and giving him more challenging demands.
She had gotten on Mike's nerves with her loud orders and instructions but he knew that she meant well and was only helping him to do what her superiors had said he would never be able to do again. Observing his strength caused the nurse to develop feelings for Mike, and noticing the rapid rate in which he was progressing she began believing he could be walking again by Monday.
She had continuously redressed his wounds and filled them with cocaine in between exercises. By late Monday afternoon Mike was walking with a harsh limp. But he was walking.
He was walking slumped over and taking short painful looking steps. But at least the steps were taken on Mike’s own steam.
Over the weekend Mike told the nurse and the cabdriver a little about himself and why he lived his life the way he did. Even though it was only a little bit of information that he shared it was more than enough to give them some insight into the mind of the battered person before them. They were both equally shocked to learn how young Mike was, his heritage, and his little more than off- the-beaten path views.
When Monday night finally came Mike was exhausted from the extensive physical therapy he had endured over the weekend. He’d been asleep on the couch taking a short nap when he heard a knock at the door. Even though he heard the knocking, he didn't call out to ask who was at the door. Instead he got up from the couch and limped to the door, his awkward footsteps were muffled by the thickness of the carpet. Getting close up on the door, he looked through the peephole as the knocking continued. It was the cabdriver. Mike opened up the door to let him in and at the same time the nurse came from the doorway of the back bedroom where she had slept for the weekend. She was wearing only a large t-shirt and from a glimpse at her chest the cabdriver saw that she had no bra on. Seeing her dressed this way and coming from the bedroom, the cabdriver wondered to himself what kind of day Mike and the nurse had had so far. "Would she go that far?" he thought to himself, walking into the apartment and closing the door behind him. His thoughts were changed as he watched Mike limping across the floor back to the couch. He knew sex was impossible for any man in such a condition.
He had knocked on the door because the doorbell was only used for Mike's marijuana customers. When a customer would ring the doorbell they would know whether or not the spot was open depending on the result of pushing the button. If the doorbell sounded that meant it was open. If no sound followed a push of the button, they knew it was closed.
The cabdriver stood leaning against the wall beside the large projector tossing his keys in the air and catching them. He didn't bother sitting down because Mike had previously informed him that once he arrived they would immediately prepare to leave because Mike had pressing business to take care of. The nurse went back into the room and got dressed. Mike was on the couch counting a small stack of money.
“Are you ready for your bonus?" he asked the cabdriver.
"Definitely!" The cabdriver responded with a big smile on his face.
"All I want you to do is drive me downtown to these muthafuckas' coke spot. If po-po stops us comin’ or goin’, just tell them I kidnapped you and held you at gunpoint and forced you to drive me downtown," Mike said.
By now he and the cab driver had somewhat of a bond and Mike sensed that the cab driver's motives for helping him went beyond merely making money. He seemed to have personal reasons for wanting to see Mike make it, but Mike couldn’t determine whether it was awe, or pity. Either way, Mike, who believed himself to be good at judging character, sensed that the cab driver was down to do whatever. When the nurse returned to the living room fully dressed, Mike gave her a small stack of money.
"Thank you," she said. “Lord knows I need it. They don't pay me much of shit at that damn hospital."
"There you go, homeboy," Mike said, throwing a tightly wound, rubber banded knot of money to the cab driver who quickly snatched it out of the air with one hand.
"Good lookin’ out, man," the cabdriver responded, placing the money in his jacket pocket.
"You got that. Thank y'all for lookin' out for me like y'all did," Mike said looking from one of his guests to the other.
Mike went over to the gun rack retrieving an AK-47 assault rifle, a chrome 9-millimeter pistol, and five extra clips of ammunition.
"Time to roll, my friend," he said to the cabdriver.
They left the nurse and went out to the cab. Both men were wearing black hooded jackets. Mike sat in the back of the cab just as he had done a few days earlier on his way to the robbery, the only difference was today it was drizzling and Bob was gone.
"Wait right here," Mike said to the cab driver as he limped out of the cab. His limp wasn't as awkward as it had been earlier mainly due to the high level of adrenaline now pumping through him. He unconsciously began to pray.
"Look over me, Bob, my nigga," Mike said toting the AK-47 in one hand approaching the door of the cocaine spot owned by the Spanish men who had killed Bob during the robbery. "Watch me get down homey, this one's for you," he added, speaking quietly to his dead partner.
He knocked on the door and waited for a response.
"Just a minute!" came a sweet female voice. Seconds later the door opened partially and was stopped by a door chain from the inside.
"Hi," said the confused looking young Spanish woman. "You lookin' for Poppi? You should know he doesn't serve here.
If you want something you have to go to his place across town," she continued, while peeking through the narrow opening.
Mike looked at her as she spoke and nearly started laughing. He had seen it all in his line of survival but this one took the cake. His mind had always caught on to things fast and it was evident that this bitch had him pegged as a crackhead just looking for a hit or two. Thinking of his appearance while she continued talking made him realize that if the tables were turned, he would be thinking the same thing. Here he was all thin and slumped over, wearing ragged clothes. He decided to use this to his advantage.
"N-N-NO!" he stuttered with an expression on his face that made him look as if he was on the verge of tears. "I just talked to Poppi at the payphone."
He paused briefly, looking frustrated and worried. "He told me to come here and get something straight from him."
The Spanish woman looked at him as if he was nothing more than an agitating insect. Poppi had taken her off the streets where she lived as a runaway, and he had taught her that she was better than crackheads because they were weak. "Look at this motherfucker," she thought to herself, watching Mike ramble on with his stuttered speech. "He probably walked all the way over here for a fuckin’ ten dollar rock. I hate crackheads."
Mike could see she was lost in her thoughts by the look in her eyes. She was looking in his direction but appeared to be staring through him. He took advantage of the split second her mind slipped and raised the assault rifle up, pointing it at her. The Spanish woman was so surprised that her eyes threatened to pop out of her head. Confused and horrified the woman’s only response was, "What the fuck? What do you want? What are you doing? Don't you know Poppi will kill you?"
"Bitch, shut the fuck up and open the door unless you want to be the one gettin’ killed tonight!" he whispered harshly to the scared woman. "If you scream or close this door, I'll shoot through it and blow ya’ fuckin’ head off your goddamn shoulders. Is that what you want?"
"No please, no!" she said, trembling violently and beginning to cry. "I open the door just don't hurt me or Poppi please!"
"What the fuck's goin’ on, Valerie?" came a deep husky voice just as she opened the door and Mike burst into the room brandishing his rifle. He aimed the rifle at Poppi because he knew better than to aim it at Valerie. He knew that if Poppi was anything like him, he could care less about his woman's life being threatened or taken if his own could be spared. "I know you muthafucka!" Poppi said loudly pointing at Mike. "You're one of those maricóns who robbed my fuckin’ spot three days ago!"
"Yeah, you faggot muthafucka," Mike said as his heart raced with anxiety. "You should've killed me too. Now I'ma make sure you wish you did."
"Fuck you!" Poppi said with fear slowly overcoming him. “Please Poppi just give him some shit so he can leave!" "Yeah, Poppi!" Mike said mockingly. “Just give me some shit so I can leave!" There was a wicked smile plastered across Mike’s face. "Alright, alright. Look Valerie, go into the freezer and get all I got, and bring it out here. The twenty bricks and the money, just go get it baby, everything will be cool."
Poppi said, never taking his eyes away from Mike. "Okay," Valerie said crying hysterically. "Don't try nothin’ slick, bitch!" Mike said to Valerie. "If you get cute, I won't hesitate to kill your man and you." She believed him. She truly believed she couldn't live without Poppi, and she had no intentions of doing anything that could get him killed. She went into the kitchen right around the corner and began making trips back and forth bringing package after package into the living room. By the time she was finished there were thirty-one packages full of money and cocaine stacked up in the middle of the living room. When she was done she went to Poppi and threw herself into his arms. She then looked back at Mike.
"That's all we got, now would you please leave us alone?! We don't want any trouble!"
Mike cracked a smile that had "fuck you" written all over it. "Oh well, baby, or should I say, Valerie, you definitely got trouble! You see? Your man here killed my homeboy and I think he owes him an apology. Understand what I'm sayin? I want him to apologize to my boy for taking his life and if you don't move yourself off his bitch-ass I'm gonna send you there with him so you can watch it firsthand! Now move!"
Just then Poppi grabbed Valerie in a tight bear hug holding her between him and Mike. He wasn't going to let this maniac come into his home and kill him without a fight.
Poppi was a pretty big man and even with Valerie pinned against his body, parts of him were quite exposed.
Mike reached inside his coat and pulled out the nine millimeter he had brought with him. There was a silencer attached to it and the clip was full of hollow point rounds. Mike cocked it after dropping the rifle and then aimed the semi-automatic at Poppi’s exposed head. Just as Poppi went to duck down behind Valerie, Mike pulled the trigger on his weapon twice and skillfully shot him in the left side of his forehead. Valerie began to scream as the big man fell dead to the floor.
"You bastard!" She screeched through tears. "You killed him!"
Mike was unfazed by the woman’s dramatic display of emotion. "Aaaw, bitch," he said to her, "That muthafucka ain't give a fuck about you. He almost got you killed wit’ him. Now shut the fuck up before I buss one in yo ass, fool!"
She just fell to the floor crying softly, holding the big man's head in her lap and rocking from side to side.
Back in the abandoned building he called home for the past two years, Mike laid on the couch in the living room getting his bullet wounds redressed while the cabdriver stood close by holding two large bags.
"Where do you want me to put these?" he asked.
Mike, preoccupied with tending to his still tender injuries, motioned toward the bedroom.
"Cool." The cabdriver did as told.
Mike had successfully avenged Bob's death by murdering the man who had killed him. He also killed the man's woman before taking the drugs and money, because he didn't want his “favor” of leaving her alive to be a favor that would come back to haunt him. So he blew her heart out of her chest as she cried over her dead man. Now he was back at his apartment with the cab driver who had helped him move all of the drugs and money out of Poppi's apartment, and the nurse who had been there all the time they were gone. She was worried about Mike while he was out, so when he returned alive with no additional injuries, she couldn't help but to hug him.
Now she was in front of him packing his bullet wounds with cocaine again. One by one she put her fingers in her mouth to moisten her tips before placing them into the bag of cocaine that was sitting on the floor. She then placed her cocaine-covered finger inside of Mike's wounds to numb away his discomfort. Mike's heart began to beat rapidly as the coke filtered-into his bloodstream. He snorted some of the blow, which made him really high. After the cab driver finished moving the big bags of drugs and money into the back bedroom, he plopped down on a leather chair. He didn’t use drugs so he just looked on as Mike and the-nurse sucked up lines of coke after all of his wounds were redressed. The cabdriver just sat there wondering what could be on Mike's mind. "How the fuck can somebody turnout like this?" he wondered to himself. "This is one crazy muthafucka!"
The cabby admired Mike because he seemed to be absolutely fearless. Drifting off into his own past, the cabby found himself thinking about his father. Mike reminded him of his father in many ways. As he became more comfortable he recalled the night that his father had died.
_____________________________________________
Barely in his teens he witnessed his father get killed while in the process of robbing a liquor store. He was peering through the window when he saw his father with a gun pointed at the head of the store owner. While the storeowner was going into the cash register to take out the money, his father looked out the window making eye contact with his son who should've been waiting in the car as he was told. The little boy was surprised when he witnessed, firsthand, his father in the act of doing what he had done all of his life to survive. He was even more surprised to see the store owner raise up a huge revolver just like the one he had recently seen in a Clint Eastwood movie, and pull the trigger, blowing off half of his father’s face, right there in front of him.
The cabdriver remembered standing there, shocked, hurt, and screaming as tears flowed down his face. He was unable to move as the only man he had ever called his hero laid dead on the floor of the liquor store.
Ever since that hot summer night, he blamed himself for his father's death. He felt in his heart that his father wouldn't have shot or hurt the storeowner, and that his father would still be alive if he would've stayed in the car instead of going to look through the window. Most of all the cab driver blamed the store owner for taking his father's life in cold blood. After the incident, the cab driver would wait until closing time and sneak down to the liquor store and peek through the window at the store owner with hatred in his heart and blood in his eyes. He would silently vow to kill this old man in revenge for his father.
His father used to spend a lot of time with him doing all the things any good father would do with their children. They did everything together, whether it was homework, playing sports at the park with the other neighborhood kids, or just riding around in the car listening to music and visiting his father's friends. They lived in the suburbs off the proceeds of his father's "jobs" but his father always took him into the hood where he himself was raised.
After his father’s death, the boy and his mother were forced to move back into the ghetto because his mother couldn’t afford to keep up the mortgage on their beautiful home. They lost practically everything they had. Many nights the boy thought to himself before he went to sleep, "Damn, if only I would have stayed in the car." With his father dead he was forced to fend for himself and bear the weight of guilt for his father's death.
_____________________________________________
Snapping back into his present reality, the cabby thought aloud, "If I only had the heart like this nigga, Mike."
"Yo, what the fuck you over there mumblin’ about?" Mike asked him, obviously stoned.
"Nothin’ much," he responded, "Just thinking out loud." To the cabdriver, Mike seemed to have no thoughts of the drama they had just been involved in at Poppi’s home. This puzzled him because since the whole thing went down it was all he could think about.
During the revenge/robbery the cabby had been parked near the curb when he saw Mike limping from the well-known Hispanic man's home. Mike had a big bag with him, and he dragged it into the cab with him.
"There's one more bag in there, go get it!" he yelled to the cabdriver. "It's in the front room, you can't miss it.” When the cab driver entered the home the scene was as sickening as the day his father had been killed. There was blood everywhere and a big piece of brain was stuck to the wall behind the two dead bodies that were lying on the floor. He remembered nearly fainting when he looked at the female victim with her mouth and eyes wide open. He had grabbed the heavy bag, lifting it as if it weighed nothing and raced back to the cab.
"Oh my God!" he said. "We gotta get the fuck up outta here!"
The tires peeled rubber off onto the street and sirens were blaring in the background along with the sounds of helicopter propellers as they sped away from the scene. He could have sworn he heard Mike whisper, "That's one for you, Bob. Rest in peace, my nigga!" as the cab rolled on.
Now, here Mike sat in this lavishly decorated apartment, inside an abandoned building, in one of the worst areas in the South Bronx, and it was as if nothing had happened at all. The nurse finished dressing Mike's wounds and snorting coke with him. Now she was just sitting there on the floor in front of the couch Mike was stretched out on. He touched her on the shoulder.
"Go into the kitchen and bring back three glasses and a bottle of Moet."
"Okay," she responded smoothly as she set out to grant his request. Just like the cab driver, the nurse admired Mike because of the sense of power he possessed and the way he always seemed to be in control of things. She and the cabdriver both knew that Mike possessed riches and respect that they only wished they had, and that he had to depend on and answer to no one but himself, so they did all they could to please him. What neither of the two realized or knew was that Mike quietly admired them both just as much as they admired him, simply because they lived a life that gave them the privilege of not having to respond to all of their feelings, thoughts, and emotions in a physically violent manner. Plus they both had told Mike that they still lived with their mothers.
Mike really loved his mother and it hurt him constantly that he couldn't and didn't know how to get in touch with her. He laid there on the leather sofa, in his coked up condition and wondered why his life had turned out the way it did–even though all the answers were trapped deep in his mind and memory.
The cocaine was doing its job, so he couldn't feel the pain in his legs anymore.
After the nurse returned, she handed the cabdriver a glass of Moet and poured some for herself and Mike, then she went over to the couch, fluffed his pillows and began to give him a massage. As she worked on his shoulders, Mike began to relax, and as he relaxed, his childhood briefly came to his mind.
_____________________________________________
The best memories that Mike had of his childhood were of him growing up in Jamaica. He had lived with his grandparents, his brothers, sisters and some cousins. There were six children living in an old store that was converted into a bungalow ten feet by fifteen feet. Mike grew up with his mother's side of the family. Jamaican families are typically tight knit, and theirs was no exception.
He was the youngest out of all the kids so he had always sought attention. All in all, there was nothing but love and support shared from one family member to another.
Mike's grandmother was the town's baker and a mentor to most everyone. She baked all sorts of things for weddings, birthdays and get-togethers. Everyone really loved her just as much as they loved her baking. Mike’s family lived across the street from the police station. Their little home had a big wall around it that everyone would sit on to watch when the police arrested someone and brought them to the station. The police would tie the criminals' hands with rope being that poverty prevented the police station from being able to supply all the officers with handcuffs. Arrests were usually the talk of the town. The police would walk the rowdy criminals through the town beating them the whole trip to the station.
As the town's people walked along watching, the taunting crowds would get bigger and bigger. This attention from the town's people usually caused the criminal to become tough and try to resist being taken in, and it also caused the police to beat them worse. Mike was only a baby but he would sit in the dirt playing by himself while everybody raced to get a seat on the wall to view the arrests. They would taunt, cheer, and yell, giving big ups to the criminals and shouting curse words at the cops. When it was all over everybody would return to the back of the house where Mike was still playing in the dirty yard by himself. They would begin speaking about all the people who got arrested and those who had left the island to go to America and make a better life for themselves. At the young age of four Mike began to hear legendary stories of people who had left the island and became successful.
He paid special attention to these stories because of the ongoing attention they received, even in their absence, and he wanted to grow up to be just like the stars of those tales. Mike would daydream about how much money he would make when he was old enough to leave the island and how successful he would become so that he could one day become one of the legends and be spoken of with the same regard as the others who had found their way overseas.
Some people would think that having dreams and plans of becoming successful and rich is something good for a four-year-old boy. They might have it in their minds that it’s healthy to have older people around such a small child, giving him inspirational stories of others who had become successful in life so that he would want to do the same. That's until they hear the subjects of these stories.
Once people left the island, most of them fell victim to the many vices that plagued the U.S. So the stories the locals would sit around and proudly tell were true tales of those who had left for America and ended up breaking the law to survive and get paid. The locals would speak of these people in such a positive way that almost every child listening wanted to grow up and break the law themselves. They wanted to follow in the footsteps of their hometown "heroes" who went overseas and started robbing, selling drugs, and even murdering as their daily means of survival. Virtually no one ever talked about those who went to America and became doctors, lawyers, or owners of legal businesses. "Maybe that's why I turned out like I did," Mike thought to himself as he rested on his expensive leather sofa, receiving a massage from the nurse.
At the age of six Mike met his brother who was living with his grandmother on his father's side of the family, out in the country part of Jamaica. In this part of the island there was no running water except for the creeks and streams. There were no toilets or lights in the houses. The streets were all dirt roads if you wish to call them roads at all. Mike would never forget the day when he first met his older brother.
"This is your older brother, Kenny," Mike's grandmother told him as he stood looking at the other boy in front of him. Kenny was a year older than Mike, but growing up in the city, Mike was exposed to much more than Kenny had been. So Mike knew how to think his way into or out of any kind of situation, whereas Kenny didn't have a clue about a lot of things. Mike was a lot more exposed from living right across the street from the police station and all the stories he had heard growing up at his grandmother's house in the city. Mike and Kenny became close right away and they made promises to each other that from that day on they would look out for and take care of each other no matter what. They stuck by each other and held firm to their promise.
At some point, their mother had sent them some toy guns with holsters that made short popping sounds with every pull of the trigger. The two boys used to play with the other kids in town using them as victims of robberies. One day they walked up the hill past the police station, made a right turn, and walked past the patty shop owned by a Chinese family. They had a plan. Many nights Mike would have to walk up the hill to go pick up groceries from the Chinese store. Next to the Chinese store was a beauty salon. Mike and Kenny took their guns from the holsters and ran into the beauty salon. All of the ladies inside had been talking away gossiping about whomever they disliked and when the two boys barged into the shop the ladies were all surprised.
"Stick'em up high where I can see' em'!" Mike said loudly with his face twisted up into a serious frown.
"Yeah!" Kenny screeched. "Stick 'em up high where we can see' em!"
The women thought that this had to be some type of joke, so they started to laugh at the two little promising robbers standing before them brandishing their ridiculous toy pistols.
"What the BLOOD CLOT yall laughing' at?" Mike yelled furiously. "We ain't playing', we want to see some money in our hands NOW!! You see this gun?" He said, raising up his pistol. "I'll use it, now move it!"
"Yeah!" Kenny followed up. "Move it!"
Hearing these two youngsters yelling and cursing made all of the women mad except for one woman.
"Listen, babies," she said with the sweetness of a favorite aunt. "We ain't got bunch of nothing around here so we can't give much, but I'll tell y'all how to really get something' if y'all want it."
She came from behind her station and sat the two young brothers down. "If y'all want a lot of money, y'all got to get yourselves some real pistols and go over to the U.S. where all the money is.
Y'all ain't supposed to rob the people who changed your shitty pampers just a few years ago, you hear?" she said and then broke out laughing at the whole thing.
"Here," she said, regaining control of herself and reaching into her purse to retrieve two quarters, "this is for your efforts." She dropped a quarter in each boy’s hand.
"Man," Mike said with an exasperated expression on his face. "What the hell are we supposed to do with quarters?" "I don't know," the woman said looking from one boy to the other, "But I do know that I'm going to take y'all home and let your grandmother know about y'all’s little stick up." She knew the boys' grandmother very well. She also had grandchildren of her own and didn't want to see them doing things like these two boys had done that night, trying to rob her salon.
She followed through with her threat, but their grandmother wasn't home. Someone made mention that she was at the police station across the street. On occasion his grandmother would bake muffins and even cakes for the good officers and they always complimented her baking. The owner of the salon dragged Mike across the unpaved road, and began telling his grandmother about what had happened. Mike stood off to the side, playing with his toy gun. The ladies glanced in his direction just as he was pointing the gun at the police station's windows and pulling the trigger repeatedly.
Seeing him do this was fuel for the vicious ass whipping he had coming from his grandmother, especially after hearing the entire story of his mock robbery. She promised to beat Kenny's little ass too, and when she got home she proved to be a woman of her word. That incident became the talk of the town and remained so, for as long as Mike could remember.
No one ever spoke of the good things Mike had done like getting good grades, helping his grandmother around the house, and cleaning.
This made Mike believe that it was better to do wrong, and to get praise and attention, than it was to do right and disappear amongst sheep. That night Mike and Kenny had promised each other that together they would grow up to be praised the only way they knew how. _____________________________________________
"You want some more coke, baby?" the nurse asked Mike, snapping him out of his trance.
“No, baby. I'm cool," he replied. They were all tired and neither the nurse nor the cab driver showed any signs of wanting to leave. Mike knew they wanted their money but he also needed them to stay longer to help him. He just didn't know how to go about asking them because he had never in his life been so helpless that he had to ask for help. Now he needed help more than anything and he wondered if his guests had seen through his superficial attitude of being in control. Somehow he was going to bring up the subject tomorrow. For now he was too tired and too high to discuss much of anything so he told them that they could sleep wherever they felt comfortable and he went to the back bedroom, locked the door, and fell into a deep sleep.

CHAPTER FOUR
THE NURSE AND THE CAB DRIVER OPENS UP ABOUT THEIR LIVES

The sun had come up and sharp beams of light sliced through the sparse spaces in the heavy cover Mike had placed over the windows. He raised a hand to protect his eyes as he rose up using the wall to support himself. He limped to the bathroom and used one hand to support himself on the sink and the other to turn the hot water on, then splashing it on his face. He sat down  on the toilet seat and opened a Ziploc bag that was fat with cocaine. Placing his finger in his mouth, he used its moistness to gather some of the blow and then he placed it into his bullet wounds. Most of them were healing and scabbed while others were still partially open from constant movement. He made his way back to the living room, pausing to overcome the momentary rush he felt from the cocaine entering his bloodstream. He plugged the doorbell's buzzer into the wall and limped over to the loveseat across from the sofa where the nurse was still sleeping. The cab driver was snoring loudly in the large plush chair.
Mike watched them sleep for a short time before his obligation for the day rushed his mind, and began tearing at his heart.
"How can I tell that woman some shit like this?" he asked himself quietly while shaking his head. He wondered how he was going to tell Bob's mother that her son had been killed just a few days ago. Plus he wondered how she would react to the fact that the child being developed inside of her only daughter's womb was his. Mike and Kim had kept their relationship as much on the hush as they could because they knew Ms. Johnson didn't approve of Mike at all because of the way he carried himself. Only Bob knew about their relationship and they knew that he could be counted on to keep it a secret. Even though he wasn't monogamous, Mike really did have strong feelings for Kim. It would be very difficult to bring all of this to Ms. Johnson’s attention at once but Mike knew that it was his responsibility to do so. He was the one who had her daughter pregnant and he was the one who was with her son when he was murdered, so he was the one who had to make Ms. Johnson aware of these things. He sort of hoped that she already knew about Bob's death. These thoughts were quickly broken by the remembrance of the fact that Bob had never had any form of identification and it was not possible for the hospital to notify his family of his death. There was no way around it. Mike had his work cut out for him.
His thoughts were interrupted by the loud buzzing of the doorbell. He limped over to the door.
"What ya want?!" he asked, knowing what was going on.
"Three nickel bags, and a trey bag," the unseen customer said from the other side of the door.
Dropping the keyhole's cylinder, which was attached to a few heavy rubber bands and wrapped around a nail in the door, Mike made the transaction. The customer stuck a twenty-dollar bill through the hole in the door where the cylinder should be. Mike took the money and pushed out the three nickel bags of cheeba and the one three dollar bag the customer had asked for. Then he put two dollars through the hole.
This arrangement of the keyhole cylinder was essentially perfect. The rubber bands holding the cylinder were connected to a long four by eight inch thick piece of wood that was screwed across the front door. The door was also covered by a half inch thick piece of steel. This was done so that if anyone shot at the apartment door, the bullet would ricochet and possibly hit them or get lodged into the door, but under no circumstances would any small or common weapon harm anyone in the apartment through the door.
"Much love," the unseen customer said after taking her purchase. "I came here over the weekend but you were closed, is everything alright?" the female voice spoke, sounding concerned. "I thought Five-0 knocked y'all off," she continued in a beautiful Spanish accent.
"Thanks for the concern," Mike replied to her, "...and have a nice day." He pulled the cylinder back into its proper place and wrapped the rubber bands around the nail pulling it back in place.
On his way back to the couch, Mike noticed that his two guests were both awake and looking at him astounded.
"Well I'll be damned," said the nurse. "This is a weed spot ain't it?"
"Hell naw it ain't no petty ass weed spot. A weed spot is known for pushing cheap homegrown bush. I don't deal with nothin' but the best shit, Thai Stick, Hawaiian bud, RedHead Sess etc. etc., nothin' but the best bud.
"That shit with the door is real slick," the nurse said, obviously impressed.
"I got some things I gotta do today." Mike said to them both, "So I have to get a move on. How much do I owe y'all?"
"We were talking last night," The cabdriver answered. "And we decided that we want to stay around and help you out. Can we roll with you or what?" He was smiling and so was the nurse who was staring at Mike a little too long.
They had ridded Mike of one more burden he had to deal with. Deep inside he was ecstatic, knowing he would have the help he needed but of course he had an image to protect so he couldn't show his true feelings openly.
"I ain't got no problem with that," he said with a passive expression on his face. "But, y'all steal anything from me and I'll have to kill you, understand?"
They both laughed even though they knew he was dead serious. "Yeah, we understand," the nurse replied, placing her hands in the back pockets of her tight jeans.
Mike knew that he was too weak to take care of himself so his decision to keep the nurse and the cabdriver close by was easy to make. At that moment Mike made up his mind to stop smoking coke, which he had been doing for the past two years. "After all, I'll be thirteen years old in less than two months," he thought to himself. "Plus I already have two babies on the way."
The nurse walked over to the couch where he was sitting. "You okay?" she asked in a low voice.
"I'm aight," Mike responded. "I just need to get dressed and go take care of shit." "Come on," she said, "I'll help you."
They went into the bedroom and Mike sat down on the edge of the big bed.
"I feel like wearing black," he said to the nurse. "Go in the closet and see what you can find."
She went into the large closet noticing that it was stocked with expensive, top of the line gear. She came back from the closet handing Mike some clothes with the tags still on them. The pants were made from imported wool and the sweater was a cashmere wool blend. There was a down filled black vest and a new pair of Timberland boots. The tags showed that the clothes were purchased at AJ Lester's on 125th between 7th and 8th Avenue in Harlem.
She helped him get dressed. "You look good, baby," she said to him, looking him over. He didn't pay much attention to her statement. Instead he made his way over to the large mirror to observe for himself.
"Look in the top drawer over there and get me a chain to put on," he said to her. She went over to the chest of drawers, reached into the top drawer and brought back a thick flat platinum Cuban link necklace that had a lion's head medallion flooded with diamonds dangling from it.
He was fully dressed and back on his way to the front room when the door's buzzer sounded again. The cab driver was standing up looking at Mike confusedly. "Come on," Mike said to them both. "I'll show y'all what to do." He went to the solid door and dropped the cylinder. For future use, the nurse and the cabdriver paid close attention to everything that Mike did.
"Yo, wus'up?" Mike said looking through the peephole seeing that there were about ten people lined up in the dimly lit hallway on the fourth floor of the abandoned building. This hallway only had one sixty-watt bulb suspended from the ceiling by an extension cord coming through a hole in the ceiling.
"Gimme ten nicks," a male voice said, and a fifty-dollar bill was pushed through the hole in the block of wood that was screwed to the door. Mike went and got some of his stash. When he came back he began to serve his customers. As each one got what they came for they quickly made their way down the rickety stars. As he sold the weed he showed the nurse and the cabdriver what he was doing. "This is a nickel ($5) bag, this is a dime ($10) bag, and this is a trey ($3) bag.
He made sure that they could easily distinguish each bag before sliding the product through the hole. He had put three bags in place on the door, each holding smaller bags ready to be sold individually. One contained dime bags, another contained nickel bags and the last contained trey bags. He continued teaching his new associates the basics of his operation.
"If anybody wants a half-ounce just take eight nickel bags and dump them in one of the Ziploc bags that’s on the kitchen table next to the scale. Double it if they want an ounce and don't forget to fluff it up and be careful sticking it through the hole. And never open the door for nobody except me. And only open it for me if I'm by myself, never open it if somebody is behind me, it could get us all killed."
"A'ight, cool," the cabdriver responded. "We can handle it."
When all the customers had been served, Mike told the cab driver to pull the cylinder back in the door, and then he went over to the couch and reached under one of the cushions pulling out a 9-millimeter pistol and three extra clips placing them inside of his waistline.
"Hey!" He called the nurse. "Check this out, I'm about to go over to my baby's mother's crib. She ain't got the baby yet but that's what we call our pregnant woman, and women who have our kids.
"I know that," the nurse said, laughingly.
"Anyway," Mike said, "I need you to stay here and work the door. You got that?"
"Mm-hmm, I got it," she said. "Just be careful." Without thinking, the nurse walked quickly over to Mike and hugged him tight. She kissed him softly on the cheek, "Please promise me you'll be careful, baby."
Mike didn't know how to react to affection due to the fact that he had never gotten any unless it was from his mother or his grandmother, all of which seemed like an entire lifetime ago.
"Yeah, whatever," he said. His mind was elsewhere. Before he lived with his grandmother, he had been with his mother. He remembered how she used to kiss him at times to lift his spirits. Right before he went to bed or before he went outside to play in the yard, she would always show her love to him with a comforting hug and kiss on the cheek. Even before letting him go inside the building where he went to preschool or before she went off to work, she would always find a way to show her son that he was important. Her job was housecleaning. She used to work her fingers to the bone cleaning white people's homes for little pay. Sometimes they would have to go without lights because the bill couldn't be paid but she still worked hard and tried her best to raise him. At times when he would see her sitting down in their little shack of a home, exhausted from her day's work, little Mike would perch himself up on her lap and promise her that he would grow up and make a lot of money so that she wouldn't have to clean white people's houses anymore. She would smile, kiss him and say, "Take care, little man." She had told him that the last day he saw her, she was crying and he was crying too, not knowing or understanding why he wasn't going to be with her. "I can't take care of you, son. My mother loves you and she'll take good care of you," she had said. "I want you to always know that as soon as I can, I will be back to get you. And never forget that I love you, baby. I love you with all my heart, you hear?"
Then she went down the front walkway to board a plane to America. "I love you," Mike found himself saying softly. He stepped away from the nurse who was smiling and blushing.
"What you want me to do?" the cab driver asked Mike, bringing him back to reality.
"You can drive me over to my girl’s crib," Mike said. Then he showed them both how to open the door, and how to barricade the door back in place. He looked through the peephole seeing that no one was in the hallway.
"We'll be back a little later," he said to the nurse, then he and the cab driver left the apartment.
The nurse closed the door and barricaded it in place just the way Mike had demonstrated. Then she went back and sat on the sofa with her mind on Mike.
"He said he loves me," she said to herself softly smiling.
She had no idea that the words Mike had inadvertently spoken were not in any way directed toward her so she sat there feeling good inside. "I think he knows I want him," she thought privately.
She was twenty-two years old and the only reason she had become a nurse was because of her ailing mother who had nearly died three years earlier. Before she got herself together, the nurse was going nowhere fast. She had been rebellious, careless, and abusive to herself through the drugs she used on a regular basis and the foul company she kept around her. Her mother had given birth to her at the age of sixteen and her mother was wild so, naturally, the nurse grew up to be wild as well. Her mother continued to live recklessly until the day she went to the emergency room feeling nauseated only to be diagnosed at the age of thirty-four, with sickle cell anemia. That's when she was forced to slow down and concentrate on her painful illness. This disease also changed the way the nurse had been living. She was on the verge of dropping out of school and destined to become a victim of the vicious inner-city streets until the day she came home to find her mother crying and in pain. She loved her mother and when she learned that she could lose her, she promised that day to dedicate her time to helping her mother get well again. She stayed home tending to her mother when she wasn't in school. After she graduated from high school she took a college correspondence course in nursing. Her mother's sickness continued to progress making her weak and thirteen months after being diagnosed, she went into cardiopulmonary arrest, nearly dying at the young age of thirty-five. Dealing with the stress from her mother's sickness, the nurse came close to falling apart and going back into the destructive life she had led before.
On her twenty-first birthday, a hotel maid who just happened to go into the wrong room found the nurse lying unconscious. The nurse had to be rushed to the hospital where her stomach was pumped clear of the bad dope she had consumed. Had she lain on that floor any longer than she did, her heart would've burst. This is what was told to her by the doctors who had tended to her. At that moment, she thought of her mother and reminded herself that dying young was something her mother wouldn't want for her. From then she began getting her life together. She made it a priority to go back to the hotel she had been found dying in so that she could thank the maid who had saved her life.
During her mother's sickness she obtained her nursing certificate. A short time later she was able to get a job at Presbyterian Hospital. She used her small paychecks to take care of her mother and to maintain their tiny apartment. When it came down to dealing with men, the nurse had specific tastes and she never deviated from them. Since she was from the hood she had a particular liking for men who were also from the hood. She had never involved herself with weak men or square men, or men from the suburbs whom she believed thought they were somehow better than everyone else. She liked hustlers because they had heart and were strong enough to protect her if the situation called for it. While working at the hospital, several doctors had come onto her and acted as if she was out of line when she kindly turned them down time after time.
All they saw was a poor, young, good-looking woman, and because of their lifestyles they figured that they were entitled to her. They flashed their rings, watches, clothes, and cars before her, thinking that she would fall for them because of the money they had. She still turned them down because money wasn't an issue to her to the point where she would give herself to a lame just because he had a few dollars. The way she saw it was simple; if she needed money it wasn't a hard thing for her to get it through the hood, so no man could entice her with material things that she could get on her own if she needed to. She looked for strength and security in men, and even though Mike was young she was attracted to him because he possessed the things she wanted in a man.
The nurse was tired so she rested her bare feet on the couch and laid back. Mike was still on her mind and she felt no shame from the heat that flowed through her thighs and stomach while she thought of him. "I love you too, baby," she found herself saying with a smile on her face, moments before she drifted off to sleep.

CHAPTER FIVE
HOW THE HERB GATES RUN. THE LION IN THE ZOO & THE BIG CONVERSATION

Driving down Prospect Avenue in the Bronx, cutting across to Fordham Road and making a right turn, the cab passed the Bronx Zoo.
"Pull over here," Mike said to the cabdriver. "To the zoo?" The cab driver asked with a snicker. "Just park the car and chill!" Mike said looking seriously. "I'll be back in about five minutes. You can get out if you want." When the car was parked, Mike got out and eased down to the section where the lion cage was located in the zoo. The cabdriver came upon a large rock and sat down on it. He enjoyed the fresh air and observed the people who were milling around him.
Standing in front of the lion cage, Mike took a deep breath and looked around to see if anyone could see him. Satisfied that he didn't seem to be being watched Mike lowered himself down to his knees. Inside the cage, the huge lion paced from side to side only taking his eyes off of Mike when turning. After a few seconds of pacing, the lion went to the front of the cage. Its large head was only inches from Mike’s and there was a low vibrating sound coming from its throat. The lion laid down on its stomach facing Mike and bobbed its head as if acknowledging Mike and deeming him welcome.
Mike looked into the eyes of the beast and took a quick second to gather his composure. Clearing his mind, Mike began to speak.
"O' Mighty King of the jungle. Please forgive me for my sins. I know that man is not supposed to kill as you do O' Mighty One. But getting-by in my own jungle is a bitch and survival is something only accomplished by the strong. Life is hard on us all in the ghetto because we have nothing but an obligation to kill or be killed. I know that we are supposed to kill only when it's necessary for food, or when our own lives are threatened but this is a cruel world. There is no code of honor, nor any respect. My people are at the bottom of the food chain, even below dogs. This is the way we are treated, so to get to the top we have to fight hard and even kill.
“We all have one blood, one goal, and one purpose. Survival! By any means necessary. I have killed before and I know that I will kill again and I ask you now for forgiveness." Mike finished and then rose to his feet. As he left, the lion looked on, and then let loose a ferocious roar.
Back inside the cab the two men were on their way to Mike's girl’s crib. The cabdriver sat silently driving for a while before he spoke.
"Yo," he said to Mike who was relaxing in the backseat. "Don't get upset or think I'm prying into your personal business, but what was that all about back there at the zoo?"
