Mike sat up straight in the back seat. "You really tryin' to hear it?" he asked. "Yeah, I'm sorta curious." "Okay. You see," Mike started. "I never told this to nobody, but I half-ass like you, so I'm gon’ let you in on something." He paused shortly then continued, "The last time I saw my mom I was three, and she took me to the zoo down in Jamaica where she lived. She told me that the lion represents the spirits of our forefathers. She told me that just as the lion is the king of his jungle, so is man the king of his own." He paused again, and took a breath before continuing. "I can hear it like she is right here sitting next to me. She said, 'Son everyone has the ability and right to kill for survival, just as the lion kills his prey to eat and his enemy to survive, so shall man do the same. Don't ever be ashamed of anything you have to do. You have the ability to live, kill and survive. You, son, are the keeper of your destiny. No man can judge you. Only God can judge you. Just ask the Lord for forgiveness when in doubt of anything you do. Whatever you do in life Mommy will stand by you no matter where you are or where I am. That's for all eternity!’"
He paused again, looking at the cabdriver. "I don't want to bore you," he said.
"Go on, finish up, this is interesting as hell," the cab driver said, clearly blown away from what he had heard so far.
Mike continued. "Mommy said, ‘Son, if you ever need strength, courage, or heart, go to the lion and the lion will give you all that you need’."
If there was ever a time when Mike needed strength, courage, and heart it was now. He still had to face his baby's mother and worst of all her mother. He had the responsibility to be the bearer of bad news.
"I might as well get this shit done and over with," he said leaning back in his seat as the cab closed in on his destination.

CHAPTER SIX
BREAKING THE NEWS TO BOB'S MOTHER

Bob and Mike had had many good times together. Bob was sixteen years old and he had love for Mike because although he was three years younger, Mike was a natural born hustler and hardcore from his flesh down to his bones. They both had the same outlook on success. They had both known older men who were prime examples of the saying that "good guys lose with pride, but they still lose." Neither of the two youngsters wanted to grow up losers, so they lived out their lives as bad guys so that they could start off winning and take it from there.
Mike remembered how proud Bob used to look when they brought money and other things back to share amongst the younger children around their way who were also poor. There were days that he and Bob would stop the ice cream man's truck, buy everything that he had and then give it all to the neighborhood kids. The kids loved Mike and Bob because on a typical day the ice cream man wouldn't even bother playing the music in his truck while driving down their strip because he knew the kids' parents couldn’t afford to spend money on his non-essential products. They were too poor to waste even the spare change that they may have had. Bob was looked up to most by all the kids because he was the older of the two hustlers.
Bob was a slender built boy with a full face, and his eyes always seemed to be squinting from too much sun. His skin was light brown and his head was covered in thick, tightly curled lochs of hair. He could look as innocently as a choirboy one second, and as dangerously as a junkyard dog the next. Bob held his section down with a firm fist and made sure that none of the children from around his way were picked on or pushed around. Bob had love for the neighborhood kids, and he wanted them to have the same things he had, even if they had to get them the way he did. To the kids in his hood, Bob was "The Man." To Kim, Bob was a big brother. To Ms. Johnson Bob was an only son. Now Mike found himself stepping out of a cab, on his way to break the news to Kim and Ms. Johnson that their big brother and only son was dead.
On the way over in the cab, the song "Secret Lovers" was playing and Mike thought to himself, “That's what me and Kim have been.” But he knew that now their secret had to be released, and as he and the cabdriver crossed the street Mike wondered how badly Ms. Johnson would take it all. All of the neighborhood bums, winos, prostitutes, and small-time drug dealers moved aside as Mike and the cabdriver cut through the crowd, down the walkway and towards the elevators. Everybody in the hood knew Mike from the herb gates (weed spot) across the street, and for his reputation for popping his pistols and stomping dudes out if they posed a threat to his money or merely got in his way.
When the elevator doors opened, Mike and the cab driver got on it. Mike reached over to the button panel and pressed the one for the 21st floor.
"I see everybody stepped out of your way when you came through. You seem to be pretty well respected around here," the cabdriver commented, attempting to make conversation to fill the silence.
"Yeah." Mike responded. "They know not to fuck with me. Anybody who ever fucked with me ain't around to speak on it, you understand?" The cabdriver just chuckled to himself as the elevator stopped and the doors slid open.
He was still amazed at the heart this youngster had, or the heart he didn't have at all.
"So this is where your girl stays?" the cab driver asked, looking around the dreary hallway.
"Yeah, this is it." Mike said quietly.
"How do you two get along, if you don't mind me asking?" he asked Mike. He wondered if Mike treated his lady the same way that he treated other people when she rubbed him the wrong way. He wondered if Mike was physically abusive to her.
"We pretty cool," Mike answered, detecting a hint of suspicion in the cab driver's question. "She trips at times but I know how to punish her when she acts-a-fool. My mom taught me the way to punish a woman is through her emotions. Taking a woman on an emotional roller coaster ride and mentally beating the shit out of her is more effective than giving her a physical ass kicking. Shit, most of these broads really like getting hit in the mouth. They act like you don't care about them unless you beat the hell out of them to prove it when they start clowning. I ain't got no time for that shit so I punish mine with her emotions.”
"That's pretty deep," the cab driver said, impressed with Mike having this knowledge on dealing with defiant women so early in life.
Walking up to the apartment, Mike took one more look down the hall before reaching for his gun and using its barrel to knock on the door. He kept his finger on the trigger just in case anyone may have peeped him coming into the building and thought they would slide up on him to avenge somebody who he may have killed. "Yo, who's there?" Kim's voice came from inside of the apartment. "It's me, baby. Open up." The door opened and Mike stepped in with the cabdriver on his heels. Kim closed the door behind them but before they could even take a seat Kim's mother busted out of the back room. She was hysterical and breathing hard. She looked into Mike's eyes and screamed loudly, "WHERE THE FUCK IS MY SON?!!"

CHAPTER SEVEN
KIMS & BOB’S MOTHER, MISS JOHNSON AIRS OUT MIKE ABOUT HIS LIFE

Lost in his thoughts, Mike stood motionless trying to find a way to answer Ms. Johnson's question. He had planned to sit her down and make sure that she was calm before he told her what he had to say. But she was already raving so he knew that his news would be even harder to get across. He still couldn't believe that Bob was dead himself, so he knew making his mother understand it would be close to impossible.
"I said, where the fuck is my son!" Ms. Johnson exclaimed loudly, her eyes as wide as they could go. “He doesn't do shit but run around with fools like you. Now where the hell is he? I ain't seen his black ass since Friday and he knows I need money for the rent this week."
Mike just looked down at the floor. He needed strength and heart badly. He worked up his nerve and then he looked up into the woman's eyes.
"He's dead, Ms. Johnson," he said, not taking his eyes away from hers.
"What the fuck?" Ms. Johnson said and began breathing harder. She started to cry hysterically while her whole body shook violently.
"No! No! God, no!" she screamed and rushed at Mike pounding him all over his face and his already aching body. "He was all I had, you bastard!" she cried. "I know he was with you! I know he was with you!" She continued fighting him. Mike instinctively reached back for his pistol, but brought his hand back when he remembered it was his dead partner's mother who was attacking him.
Kim stood shaking and crying hard. She loved her big brother with all her heart.
The cabdriver just stood in place perplexed by the scene before him.
Kim went over to grab her mother and get her off of Mike. She was unsuccessful and only caused them all to fall to the floor. All three were crying and holding on to each other down on the floor. Mike was the first to make it out of the pile, staggering to his feet wiping his teary eyes.
The cab driver helped him lift Ms. Johnson from the floor. Kim got up and went to the bathroom to get cold towels for them all, while Mike walked Ms. Johnson over to the couch and sat down beside her.
When Kim came back she handed her mother and Mike a towel each, she kept one for herself and then sat down on the other side of her mother.
"How did it happen?" Ms. Johnson managed to ask Mike who was holding one of her hands in both of his.
Mike recanted most of the incident of Bob's death, leaving out parts that were best left untold.
"Where's his body?" Ms. Johnson asked through tears, wiping her face with the damp towel.
"It's at the Presbyterian Hospital on 168th and Broadway in Manhattan. That's the same hospital I was in."
"Ms. Johnson…" Mike said after a short while of no words being spoken. "I got something else to tell you that you might not like."
"What can be worse than what you already told me? My child is dead. What's worse than that?"
"I don't think what I'm about to tell you is bad at all. Matter of fact, Kim and I both think it's pretty good news."
"And just what the hell does Kim have to do with what you got to say to me?"
"Ms. Johnson, you already know that Kim is pregnant and all, but I think it's only right for you to know that the baby on the way is mine. I wanted to tell you sooner but Kim thought it was best if you didn't know until the baby got here and I, as a man, had to respect my woman's wishes.”
Ms. Johnson's eyes got as big as paper plates. "As a man? As a man you had to respect your woman's wishes?" she ranted steaming with anger, snatching her hand away from Mike. "Little boy, who the fuck do you think you're foolin'? What the hell do you know about being a goddamn man? You just now getting a few hairs on your damn dick and you come into my home after you got my son killed and tell me you are the one who got my daughter pregnant? Who the fuck do you think you are? You ain't shit but a little devil. Jamaican, punk muthafucka! That's all you are!" She continued standing up and looking down on Mike as a gesture to let him know that she was an adult and no matter what he thought, he was only a child, whether he liked it or not. Your mother should've closed her fuckin' legs when she was in labor and snapped your damn neck and done the world a fuckin' favor! I hate all you damned Jamaicans! All y'all do is swim your hateful asses over here and ruin people with your drugs and evil ways! Now here you are over here bringin’ a fuckin' monster into the world through the only child that I have left. Me having a filthy rotten Jamaican grandchild is what you think of as good goddamn news? GET THE HELL OUT OF MY HOUSE, YOU LITTLE PIECE OF SHIT!! And DON'T bring your trifling little narrow ass back around here. Now GET OUT!!" She snapped and ran towards the door past the cabdriver. "And take your buddy with you too!” She said while snatching the door open.
Mike felt just like what she had called him, "a little piece of shit." He got up and left the apartment, taking the cab driver with him as he was ordered.
Kim felt terrible. She got up and ran to her bedroom crying. She slammed the door shut behind her. Her mother was yelling at her even through the closed door. "You're only fourteen years old and you already done fucked up your life you little tramp!"
Kim pressed her head into her pillow and wept herself to sleep.
Back in the living room, Ms. Johnson sat back down on the sofa and placed her head in her trembling hands.
"Oh, Bob, baby. I'm so sorry for raising you here. I'm so sorry."

CHAPTER EIGHT
THE NURSE FINDS OUT MIKE’S AGE AND THE CAB DRIVER PUTS MIKES IN HIS PLACE

Back at the apartment Mike sat back on the leather sofa. The nurse was relaxing on the loveseat while the cab driver was sitting in the chair. They were all quietly listening to the stereo system, which was playing Heatwave’s "Always and Forever." As the song filled the room, Mike's thoughts were preoccupied. His mind was on his mother. He was deeply hurt by the things that Kim's mother had said to him and he privately longed for his own mother. He planned to go to his grandmother's house the next day to have her contact his father and see if he had heard anything from his mother. He knew that it was probably a long shot, but he was quite desperate and couldn't think of any other avenue. At that moment, he felt that to at least hear his mother's voice would erase all the unease he felt from Ms. Johnson's hatefully spoken words.
The next morning Mike and the cab driver got up early, took showers, got dressed, and prepared to go to New Jersey.
Before they got the chance to leave, the nurse rolled over on the sofa awakened by the noise made by the two men moving about. She sat up wiping her eyes.
"Can you stop off on Fordham Road and pick up some more clothes for me and some personal things before y'all go on your runs?" she asked after yawning.
"You two can roll over there right quick. I'm not in a big rush. I'll be here when y'all get back," Mike answered then reached into his jacket pocket pulling out a thousand dollars. He gave five hundred dollars to each of them.
While driving to Fordham Road the nurse sat in the front with the cabdriver. They were both quiet and the cabdriver accurately assumed that the nurse's mind was on Mike. He had noticed that look in her eyes every time she spoke to Mike. His assumption was confirmed the minute the nurse broke the silence with a question.
"What happened when you and Mike left yesterday?" she asked, looking over at the cabdriver.
"What makes you ask that? Is something wrong?"
"No, nothing’s wrong. It's just that when y'all came back Mike seemed to be really upset and out of it. I just figured that something had happened.”
The cabdriver didn't directly answer her question because he felt that it wasn't his place to discuss Mike's business. The whole calamity had replayed itself in his mind over and over and he had no aim or intent of going into it with the nurse.
"Can you believe he's only thirteen fuckin' years old?" he said shaking his head.
"Who?" The nurse said. "Mike!" The cabdriver responded. "He's only thirteen!"
"Get the fuck out of here!" the nurse said with surprise in her wide eyes. "I knew he looked young, but I figured that he was at least nineteen. Damn, he's so mature to be only thirteen! What the hell made him so hard? That shit is wild!"
"Naw." The cab driver stated loudly, “He's wild!"
Pulling up to the stores on Fordham Road, the cab driver looked around for a place to park. There were a lot of stores on Fordham Road and the entire strip was usually packed with people. Today was no exception. The street was very busy and the sidewalks were congested with shoppers coming and going. There seemed to be no available place to park so the cab driver circled back around the block.
On his second spin, he spotted a red Nissan Maxima pulling out into the street, opening up a parking spot. He took advantage of the opportunity to park before anyone could beat him to the newly available space. Once parked, he and the nurse got out of the car.
"I'll meet you back here in thirty minutes, cool?" he said to the nurse.
"Okay," she said and made her way onto the sidewalk and into one of the stores. When they got back to the apartment Mike still looked upset. Knowing that he was only thirteen and had so much to deal with at that young age, the nurse's heart went out to him. "He'll be eighteen in only five years," she thought to herself, already mentally justifying her attraction to the youngster. He was more mature and responsible than most of the men she knew who were twice his age, and she wanted him. The nurse had it in her mind that she could be the one to be with him and show him that he could use his fast mind somewhere other than the streets. "I can do it," she thought with a smile. "Five years from now he'll be a real man. I'll see to it."
Seeing him so down fed her urge to do what she did next. She walked over to him and grabbed him, holding him tightly in her arms as if he was a long lost brother to her. She kissed him softly on the lips and whispered in his ear. "Don't feel bad, baby. I'll never leave you," she said, "I got your back."
Mike was caught off guard, but in his mind he had seen and heard it all before. "She sees me suffering and now she think I'm gon’ to let her get close. This broke coke-head just wants my money," he thought while she was still clutching him in her arms. He began to have feelings that he didn't recognize. His heart was beating a little faster and there was a funny feeling inside of his stomach. Somehow he felt his arms holding on to the nurse tighter. Then he went off.
"Bitch, are you crazy?!" he yelled at her. "Get the fuck off me!"
He pushed her away even though she continued to reach out for him. She was hurt and confused and it was evident from the glassy look in her eyes.
"Baby, don't be like that. I just want to help you. I just want to protect you. What's so wrong with that?" she said, finally backing up and looking into Mike's eyes.
He reached into his waistline and pulled out his pistol. "Bitch!" he said through clenched teeth, holding up his gun, "This right here is the only protection I need. And if you don't squash whatever you're up to, I'll give you some of this shit. You understand?! Huh?!"
He watched her turn and run towards the sofa falling on it and burying her face into the cushioning, sobbing loudly as her body shook.
"Fuckin' women!" Mike thought to himself. "Been giving me problems all my damn life. Now here this bitch comes tryna use me. If she makes me have to fuck her up, I will."
He looked over to the cab driver who had a look of disapproval in his eyes. The cabdriver was a good man and Mike liked that. He also liked the fact that the cab driver was not afraid of him. He knew that Mike was a young fellow but none of that mattered to him as he stood there frowning and staring into Mike's eyes.
"Look man," he said. "We went out of our way to help you out. I lost my job and she probably lost hers too. But if this is how you gonna treat us, we can bounce up out of here if that's what you want." He pointed to the nurse who was still crying on the sofa. "She cares about you for real, man. She ain't got nothin' as it is and you want to carry her like she ain’t shit just because she wants to help you?"
He shook his head, "It ain't right, man. It ain't right and I don't appreciate that shit."
Mike was stunned. He had played the role all this time they were with him knowing that he needed their help. He had never been checked before by anyone who wasn't related to him, at least not without tragic repercussions following. He knew that the cab driver was right and he knew that if he ran them away, it would be difficult to get around. He trusted the cab driver's judgment.
"Ah," he said to the cabdriver. "You know that shit is just hard for me right now.
"Yeah, it's cool," the cab driver said, "But it's not really me you should be saying all that to." He nodded his head in the nurse's direction.
He said, "Give her a break, man."
Mike wasn't used to apologizing about anything whether he was wrong or not, but he knew that he had to straighten things out. It would be flat out stupid for him to burn his own bridges. He walked over to the sofa and sat down on the edge of it. He rubbed the nurse’s arm to get her attention. "He be buggin’ out sometimes, you know what I mean? He won't allow me to eat meat up in the spot. Like every day my girl Kim may bring me some fried rice and chicken from the Chinese restaurant down the street from our spot, or she might cook some meat herself, and I gotta eat all the shit up before four o'clock in the afternoon when Ron gets back, because if he comes and finds it up in the spot, he hits the fuckin' roof. So bust-this-shit-out, son, I be sittin' there with my girl smashin' all types of meat and five minutes before Ron comes home, I be at the window facing the back alley throwing a ton of fuckin' bones out there," Mike laughed as he recalled it.
"What's so funny about that?" Tommy asked.
"It's funny because of the rats!" He laughed louder. "These huge, fat ass rats, be out there every fuckin' day at the same time waitin' for me to toss that shit out there.
I mean, you got to see these fuckin' things, kid! They're big as cats and they be out there standing' on their hind legs knowing it's feeding time and shit. They be movin' all around and lookin' up to the window like 'feed us homey, feed us! We hungry as shit out here'. Everybody in the hood know about them big ass things, kid. They got reps for fuckin' up dogs and everything! They don't fuck wit’ me, like how they attack everybody and everything in the hood! It's like I'm their king or some shit." Mike laughed more. As he sat there laughing about the rats where he lived in New York, Tommy just sat there looking at him with a mixture of pity and awe. "Un-fuckin-believable," he thought to himself shaking his head and watching his little cousin carry on.
"Yo…" he said, regaining Mike's undivided attention. "Don't you ever get scared or lonely up there in the Bronx living in that old ass abandoned building by yourself all the time?"
"Whaaat!" Mike said, still grinning. "Shit, son, I love it! I love to be alone, especially helping my big brother get paid. Hell, I'm gone inherit that shit one day. I got to put my work in," he said suddenly looking very serious.
“No doubt, son, no doubt." Mike finished.
Tommy shook his head, and then looked Mike in his eyes. "Baby cousin, you need to do somethin' better for yourself. You need to get down with this Rasta thing, man, for real. See, you've been brainwashed up there in the big city but Jah will help you and protect you from all that, you just need to get Jah in your heart. You see what I'm gettin' at?" he asked.
Mike heard the words but they went in one ear and out of the other, completely unregistered. "I don't walk the streets without my pistol, son, that's all the protection I need. My gun ain't failed me yet." He paused. "Speaking of guns, let me get the one you're supposed to have for me."
"You're crazy as shit, man," Tommy said, taking a revolver from the inside of his jacket and handing it to Mike.
"What the fuck am I supposed to do with this? I ain't tryin' out for no western flick! You ain't got no automatics up in here?"
"Yeah, but I ain't giving one of them to you to throw away. You can use that. You shouldn't have no drama down here, hell, you'll be back on the plane at nine forty five anyway."
While Mike still had the gun out in the open, Tommy's wife came into the room from upstairs and saw him with it.
"Tommy he's just a fuckin' baby! Why does he have a gun?" she asked, looking at Mike.
"He can handle his own baby. He's cool." Tommy responded.
"Well he can handle his own in somebody else's house because I want him out of mine, right now. I don't care if he is your family, he gotta get outta here. I don't want my kids meeting no gun totin' baby." Then she turned and went back upstairs angry at what she had seen.
"Yo, you gon’ let her clown me like that?" Mike asked, finding his cousin's wife's anger funny.
"Man, let's just go," Tommy said as he pulled out his car keys. "We got to hurry up and get to the connect's house. We're late already. Come on."
Mike followed him outside to his maroon Volvo. Seconds later they were on their way to the house where Mike would be picking up the marijuana.
Tommy dropped Mike off at the connect's expensive home in South Miami. Mike had seen big homes such as this around his grandmother's neighborhood in New Jersey but he had never been inside of a home this big unless he was robbing or burglarizing it. When Tommy saw Mike being invited into the home, he pulled the BMW out of the driveway and left.
Mike introduced himself to the big hairy Columbian who had let him in.
"Yo, I'm Mike, I'm down here to make a pick up," he said with no expression on his young face.
The Columbian shook his hand, "My name is Juan, but all who know me call me Juaney. Good to meet you Mike."
They walked into a huge living room. "Make yourself comfortable," his host said while making his way over to a large designer sofa. He pulled a cloth from the top of a glass box and Mike could see different objects in the box.
"You base-free?" He asked Mike after noticing that he was being watched.
"You mean freebase?" Mike asked.
"Yeah, you get high off this stuff? It's the best high on the fuckin' planet."
Mike had never smoked freebase before, but he didn't want the Columbian to think that he wasn't hip to what it was all about. In the streets of New York freebase was considered to be a rich man's drug because the people who used it were usually well-paid drug dealers, successful musicians, or affluent people who lived in the suburban areas of New Jersey.
"Yeah," Mike said to the Columbian. "I use it when I can, you know what I mean?"
"Well, here my friend, help yourself to this," Juaney replied, handing a pipe over to Mike who took it and took his first puff. Inhaling the chemicals of the drug caused Mike's eleven-year old heart to race. His eyes opened wide and stayed that way. He began to sweat. Exhaling a thick cloud of smoke Mike felt as if he was in a completely different world. There was a tingly feeling creeping around in his lower abdomen.
This feeling was a feeling that Mike remembered experiencing when he had cum while fucking Kim for the first time, earlier that year.
"Damn," he said. "This shit is off the chain, yo!" Then he took another hit from the pipe.
Juaney knew that this was Mike's first time smoking freebase because this sample of the dope was from a batch that was noticeably weaker than the batches he usually shipped up to his spots in New York. If Mike had used freebase in New York before, more than likely it would've been Juaney's drugs he had used, and if he had used Juaney's drugs he would have immediately noticed the weakness in what he was smoking now, compared to what he had smoked before. He took notice of Mike's willingness to adjust and adapt when he agreed to smoke the freebase even though he had never done it before. Juaney took it as if Mike had done these things so that Juaney himself wouldn't feel uncomfortable doing it in front of him. This was partly correct, but what Juaney didn't know was that, ultimately, it was curiosity that made Mike go as far as he had.
Mike smoked a whole rock by himself, loving what he was feeling. The high he experienced was unexplainable. While he did so, Juaney told him how Ron and Tommy were Rastas, which was why they dejected selling cocaine and chose to sell ganja instead. He told Mike about all of his successful spots in New York City on 158th Street, between Broadway and Amsterdam Avenue in Manhattan, and who he should go see up there if he knew any customers who wanted to use coke but had no access to it.
"You know?" Mike said to Juaney, leaning forward to make sure that he would be heard. "There's a big market for freebase up in New Jersey."
"What makes you say that?" Juaney asked him.
"Well I check out license plates and notice out-of-towners real quick. And a lot of people from Jersey come over to New York City to get freebase."
"Oh yeah?" Juaney said. "I'll keep that in mind, Mike." After conversing a while longer, Juaney gave Mike the package he had come for, then he drove him to the airport.
Mike returned to New York City with a mission. He wanted to get high and have his people in New Jersey help him satisfy his thirst and hunger for this new capital God in his life. Everyone would gather into his grandmother's basement and he would introduce them to the "new" God one by one. When the drugs ran out, Mike showed them how to get money robbing the rich white people's homes in the suburbs of New Jersey, taking any and everything that wasn't screwed into the fucking floor. He brought all of these valuables back to Juaney's coke spot in Manhattan on 158th Street and sold them for coke. He had traded television sets, stereos, expensive mink coats, VCRs, jewelry, cameras, and even cars for cocaine. He took all of the coke to his grandmother's basement and cooked it up into a freebase, and filtered it as much as he could all over New Jersey. He also continued to smoke it. Cooking the drugs were simple but none of his workers knew how to cook it and Mike made sure not to show them. He would buy all of the things needed to cook the coke from the head shop that sold all kinds of paraphernalia. He would take a pinch of baking soda and a half of a gram of coke and add a drop of water to the mixture in a small glass bottle. He would hold the bottle with steel tongs from his grandmother's kitchen because the cooking process made the bottle very hot. He would shake the bottle in a circular motion until the contents inside of it turned into a liquid. Then he would add a little bit of ice water and shake the bottle more until the contents turned into a little ball at the bottom of the bottle.
Once the impurities were cooked off, the ball got hard and then it was ready to be sold, smoked and loved. Juaney had taught him all of this in Miami, and he used his newfound skill to become a strong hustler and also to support the new habit he had developed, smoking freebase. His workers were content anyway because they knew that Mike cooked his freebase correctly without running the gimmicks and schemes they would go through if they went to an actual base house to get their drugs cooked. The house workers had many different ways of swindling their clients out of their drugs. One scheme the base houses were popular for was intentionally failing to cook the contents in the bottle all the way. In this scheme, only half of the coke placed in the bottle would come back in base form, the rest remained at the bottom of the bottle. They would give the pinched batch to their clients, and then they would return later and cook the rest of the coke that remained in the bottle. They would either smoke it themselves or sell it.
The only good thing in the base houses were the women. There were always eager women who were willing to satisfy any sexual request for the opportunity to smoke base.
A person could fuck all night with the drug in their system even if it was only a small rock that they had consumed.
This drug was so addictive that once a person was hooked on it, they would do or give anything to get it. Hooked wives sold their husband's jewelry and cars for the drug. Hooked husbands gave their entire paychecks to dealers for the drug. Young kids barely in their teens robbed, and stole things to sell for the drug.
Some of the dealers were crude and had bad habits of not charging baseheads money for the drug. Instead they offered the addicts a chance to have the drug without paying money on, provided that they were willing to perform some sort of degrading act. One such dealer was well known for making women have vaginal and oral sex with his Doberman Pinscher in return for the drug and they did it because they felt that the drug was far more valuable than their pride. Another dealer had a large fish tank full of piranhas and if addicts came without money, he would place a ten dollar bill inside one of those clear plastic bubble-shaped little containers that you see at supermarkets inside vending machines with prizes inside for children. He would tie a rock to the container and drop it inside of the fish tank. He would then tell the addict that if he or she could reach into the deep tank and retrieve the container, they could have the ten dollars inside of it. One by one they would all reach into the tank receiving bites from the piranhas. They would be made to roll up their sleeves if they were long, before they were allowed to reach into the tank, so when they pulled their arm out there would be bloody bite marks all over them. The fish would go into a frenzy trying to bite as much flesh as their mouths could hold from these pitiful addicts' arms.
Mike was in a base house one night and a filthy, dusty, and smelly woman came into the house holding a small baby boy. When she left the house she was smiling even though tears were streaming down her cheeks. One thing in particular that Mike noticed when she left was the fact that she had left without her baby. The spot was owned by a Dominican whose wife was barren, as Mike later learned. He found out that the dirty woman was once the owner of her own successful car dealership and had let freebasing cocaine lead her from wealth to homelessness. She had gotten pregnant while tricking for drugs in the same base house and had given birth to the baby boy she had brought in. He was only seven months old and Mike learned that she had just sold him to the drug dealing Dominican for an ounce of cocaine. She wanted her son to be raised in a home where he would be well off. Mike figured that it must have hurt her to sell her child, but imagined that she felt that it was the best thing to do, and there was no doubt that she wanted the drugs badly.
After becoming successful in distributing freebase to New Jersey on his own, Mike made up his mind to stop copping his cocaine from 158th Street and started copping from a spot on 163rd Street. He also decided to stop burglarizing homes as regularly as he had when he first started out. He felt it was no longer worth his time.
One day while he was in a stolen car, he drove down to the spot on 158th Street. When the people recognized him they instantly rushed to him all yelling at once.
"What's up, homeboy!" "Over here, Poppi!" "You remember me, my friend? My people got the best shit, me amigo!" "Yo! Yo! Over here!"
The coke block consisted of about thirty buildings and over half of them were drug houses. Running loose on the block were all of the cokeheads, baseheads, and small-time hustlers buying small amounts of coke and selling their stolen merchandise. There were also the women who were constantly searching for a dick to suck for money to buy drugs with. They even ate asses and sucked-off ten to fifteen guys in one session to get a rock to smoke.
Mike sold the stolen car, went into a base house to cop a quick hit off the pipe before he went back to his grandmother's house to smoke in peace. That's where he thought about what had just happened on 158th Street. He didn't see any of the people who rushed to him as friends, instead he saw them for what they truly were and how they really saw him. They had kissed his ass as if he was royalty but deep down inside, Mike knew that the words they really wanted to say were: "But your coke here, asshole!" "You stupid muthafuckin' basehead!" "I know who got the best shit to fuck your whole life up, me amigo!" This feeling is what made him stop copping from 158th Street to go to another spot to get his product. He wanted to feel better about himself while he did his business.
The animosity he had for the people on 158th Street made Mike feel that robbing them every chance he got was the proper thing to do. So rob them is just what he did.

CHAPTER TWELVE
MIKE AND CHAUNCEY GET INTO IT IN THE BASEMENT AND ITS KENNY TO THE RESCUE

Mike had dozed off while in deep thought on the couch in his grandmother's basement, but he was abruptly awakened by the sound of the basement door being banged on.
"Get the fuck up and open up this damn door!" came a deep male voice from the other side. Mike recognized that it was Chauncey. "Open up this door before I kick this muthafucka' down!"
Mike jumped up and went up the stairs to open the door. Chauncey barged through the door and continued to yell. He was furious.
"Where the hell have you been? The police have been coming by here all weekend looking for your ass!”
Mike waved him off and went back down the stairs. Chauncey followed, insisting, "You don't believe shit stinks, do you?"
"Man, I ain't tryin' to hear none of that. I'm tired."
"Tired! Well you fuckin' basehead, thief, we'll see how tired your little ass is come next week when you take your ass to court and get sent away from here! What the fuck made you and your dumb ass buddies rob all of those people's houses? White people at that! You think they're going to let you get away with some shit like that? What the fuck is your problem?"
Chauncey began to pace the floor as he spoke. "I swear I'm going to tell that judge to send your ass away and get you off of my hands! Me and momma tried hard to raise you and Kenny. He's doing what he should be doing, but you still keep on acting like a fuckin' fool. Runnin’ the streets with Ron, smokin' dope and stealing from people! Both of y’all are going to mess around and get your black asses killed!"
Mike stood silently staring into his father's eyes.
"I want you to get the fuck out of my Momma's house boy and clean up this goddamn basement!" Chauncey yelled.
Mike finally spoke. "Where the hell is my mother? Why won't you give me the information I need to find her? I want to see her."
"What?!" Chauncey yelled. "Your fuckin' mother don't want you! Nobody want you, you sorry little bastard! None of us want you! You are crazy! Your hoe ass momma is down in Miami living good, minding her business, and not giving a fuck about you! Now get the fuck out of this house!"
He raised his hand back motioning as if he was about to slap Mike.
Mike's entire attitude changed from agitated to pissed off. Stepping back before Chauncey could swing at him, Mike reached back and whipped out his gun pointing it at his father's forehead with a steady hand.
"Nigga!" he whispered angrily, with spit coming out of his mouth as he spoke. "I'll kill your muthafuckin' ass right here. I didn't ask to be brought into this fucked up world. Your black ass brought me here."
The two men stood there staring at each other. One was pissed off. The other was on the verge of pissing on himself. All was silent until noise began to come from the stairs.
It was Kenny. When he saw what was going on in the basement his eyes opened wide.
"Mike!" he yelled. "Come on man, don't do it! Please, don't do it!"
Mike stood there with his finger on the trigger. His heart was beating fast. "You heard what this no good piece of shit just said about Mommy?" he said. "Nobody talks about my mother like that. Plus he started this when he tried to hit me. I'm going to kill this nigga."
Kenny walked closer to Mike and stood right beside him. He believed that his father was going to die if he didn't convince Mike to put the gun away. "Remember our promise to each other that we would always be there for each other? Well you're about to do something stupid and I'm here for you now."
Kenny's words tore into Mike's chest like a knife. This was his brother. They had grown up together and even though they had chosen different lives, they were still brothers. Brothers who had made a pact.
"No matter what you think of Pops," Kenny said. "I love you and he's still our father. If our promise we made back in Jamaica meant anything to you, spare our father's life, man."
Mike stood there, still holding the gun on Chauncey, but stunned by Kenny's words. He reached his free arm over and draped it around Kenny's neck hugging him, but never dropping the gun. This one here is for you, Kenny." He lowered the gun. "I won't kill him because of you and the promise we made in Jamaica. I keep my word."
"Thank you, man," Kenny said to Mike. "Everything is going to be cool." There was half a smile on his face. "Everything is going to be cool." Mike looked over to his father. "Kenny just saved your life, you worthless piece of shit. I hate you, you fake-ass nigga." Then he tucked his gun back in his waistline and left the basement. He walked out to the cab. The cab driver was sitting there nearly asleep with the radio playing low when Mike got into the back of the cab.
"Take me home to the Bronx, yo!" he said loudly.
Chauncey and Kenny stood there in the basement. Kenny went over to him and hugged him tight. Chauncey hugged him back and began to weep loudly.
"Where did I go wrong?" He said through tears. "What kind of monster have I brought into this world? Oh my God, please forgive me."

CHAPTER THIRTEEN
THE CAB DRIVER AND THE NURSE SETTLING IN AT THE HERB GATES/ DERRICK, TAZ AND FLIP MAKE THEIR MOVE ON THE HERB GATES

Curtis Mayfield's "Freddy's Dead" was playing at nearly maximum volume in Mike's apartment in the old abandoned building. The nurse was there alone. She had awakened early and called home to check on her mother whom she hadn't seen in about a week. She hoped that the medication she had brought two days before she met Mike, had not run out. When there was no answer, the nurse called back, thinking she had mistakenly pushed the wrong number on the phone’s keypad. Still there was no answer. Knowing that her mother wouldn't normally be out of the house this early in the morning she began to worry.
To keep herself calm she mentally counseled herself and made herself busy by cleaning the apartment even though it didn't need it. "She's okay," she told herself but she didn't believe her own words. "She probably went out for a walk or something."
After going over the entire apartment, the nurse went into the kitchen and looked around to see what she could cook. She decided on potatoes and steak. Once the meal was done, she wrapped three plates in aluminum foil and let them sit in the oven to keep them hot. Then she poured herself a glass of Moet and drank some of it.
She was back in the living room where the stereo system was still playing, pacing back and forth when she heard someone knocking on the door. Going over to the door without saying a word just like she had seen Mike do, she looked through the peephole to see who was there. It was Mike and the cabdriver.
"Is everything okay out there?" she asked through the door without opening it. She remembered Mike saying to never open the door if somebody is behind him.
"Yeah it's cool. It's just us, let us in," Mike said. He couldn't help smiling. "She remembered," he thought privately. Stepping into the apartment, the two men were greeted with the enticing aroma of the meal that had been cooked. They were both starving. They hadn't eaten all day and the smell from the kitchen had both of their mouths watering.
"I cooked, baby,” the nurse said to Mike. "We can all eat now if you want."
"Hell yeah we can eat now!" Mike said with a laugh. "I’m starvin' like a runaway slave!"
"Me too," the cab driver stated, walking over to his usual place, the comfortable chair.
"Okay," the nurse said. "I'll be right back. You two just sit down." She went into the kitchen and came back with two plates. She handed one to Mike who was now sitting on the sofa and the other to the cabdriver. She left again and came back with two tall glasses of Pepsi. When she left once more and came back holding her own plate and glass of soda, she sat down on the sofa next to Mike. While they ate she looked over at Mike a few times trying to gauge how he was feeling. She wanted to reach out to him and tell him that she loved him, but decided against it. "I'll wait," she told herself.
After eating, Mike showered and then went into the back bedroom with the nurse to get his bandages changed. Afterwards he laid down on the bed to get some rest. The nurse put all of the extra bandages into her first aid kit box and slid it under the bed. She opened the window because even though it was springtime, it was stuffy and hot.
"Don't forget to close that window back before you go to sleep tonight," Mike said to the nurse.
"I'm going to just lay down with you for a while," she said. "Then I'll go clean up the kitchen before I go to sleep for the night."
"A'ight" Mike said, taking her by the hand and pulling her over to him. She laid there beside him, rubbing his chest with her slender fingers until he fell asleep. She kissed him on the forehead, turned off the lamp that was right beside the bed, and went to sleep herself.
_____________________________________________
Derrick was sitting down on the fire hydrant just across the street from the herb gates. He was talking with three other baseheads that he was cool with. They were all former hustlers who had traded their careers as drug dealing aspirations for addict hats. They were known to rob any and everybody who had something of value for them to take and trade for base to smoke.
He had been paying very little attention to his friends since the moment he happened to look up and see some woman inside of the herb gates opening the window. He kept his eyes pasted there wondering who she was and why she had opened the window.
"Ay, yo," he whispered loudly to get his crew's attention. "Y'all see that shit?"
"See what?" one of his boys asked.
"Up there," Derrick said and pointed to the opened window. "I saw some bitch open up the window and now she just turned off the fuckin' light."
"Oh yeah?" another one of his boys asked, looking very interested in what he had heard. "Y'all trying' to go up there?"
"Yeah," Derrick said. "Yo Rock! Me, Flip, and Taz are goin' up there. I want you to stay down here and watch things."
"Bet," Rock said, rubbing his hands together. "We finally caught this little muthafucka slippin'. He's probably up there gettin' some pussy. We got a surprise for his ass tonight."
"We'll be back. You know what to do if somebody comes through."
"Yeah, I got my end, y'all just go get his ass. I know his ass is up there."
Derrick, Taz, and Flip hurried across the street to the abandoned building and started to climb the fire escape. Taz was in the front so he stooped down to peek inside of the open window to see what was going on inside.
"Yo." He whispered when he looked in and pulled his head back away from the window. "These muthafuckas are in there sleep."
"A'ight," Derrick said. "Let's roll. Just be real quiet." Taz went to climb through the window and hit his head on the bottom of the open window, making a loud banging noise. When Mike heard the loud banging on the window, his eyes opened wide. His mind instantly alerted him that someone was coming into the apartment.
Taz was sitting on the windowsill dizzy and trying to shake away the stars he was seeing. "You damn fool!" Derrick whispered loudly up to Taz. "What the fuck you tryin'a do, wake 'em up?” Mike's heart began to race. Whoever it was thought he was asleep. He slowly reached his arm up to the pillow under his head, hoping that the nurse hadn't found the gun he kept there at all times just in case a situation like this occurred.
"Take your time, man. We got 'em." Flip said to Taz who was looking dazed as if he was going to fall off of the windowsill.
"I'm straight, I'm straight. I just banged my shit a bit, but I'm cool now. Y'all ready?" Taz answered back.
"Yeah," Derrick said, pulling out his gun from the front of his waistline. “Let's do this."
"A'ight." Taz hollered back.
Running his hand under the pillow, Mike was moving slowly trying not to startle the nurse who was still sleeping peacefully beside him. He smiled when his fingers brushed against the hard steel of the gun that he had hidden under the pillow. He eased the gun into his grip.
Taz got his mind completely back into focus and went to lift himself all the way through the window and into the apartment.
"Yo," he called back to Derrick and Flip. "I'm in. Come on, hurry up, we got 'em."
Derrick had been right behind Taz on the fire escape so he was the next to go up to the window. He kept his gun in his hand as he lifted one of his legs over the windowsill. Taz reached inside of his hooded sweater to get his gun out. Before he could bring his hand back out, Mike sprang into action. He whipped his gun from under the pillow and fired at the shadowy figure standing in his bedroom uninvited.
Taz never got the chance to scream. The hollow point rounds hit him in the chest and neck, killing him before he even hit the floor.
Derrick fell back through the window onto the fire escape as three bullets were let off in his direction, each missing him.
"Oh shit, dog!" Flip yelled back to Derrick. "Let's get the hell out of here!" He was running down the fire escape like a bat out of hell. Derrick had twisted his knee from the fall he had taken, but he got up and began to limp down the fire escape steps.
Mike jumped up from the bed and rushed to the window. He kneeled down out of sight while he looked out at the two men running down the fire escape. He shot four bullets at them and they quickly picked up their pace. Derrick turned around to see who it was shooting at them. That's when Mike recognized him.
Mike got up from the floor and went over to the bed where the nurse was sitting in complete shock. She couldn't speak. Her eyes were opened wide and staring at the dead man lying on the floor by the window. Her entire body was shaking.
She got up and ran to the bathroom to throw up.
Mike snatched two sheets from the bed and used them to wrap Taz's body up.
"Damn, son," Flip said to Derrick as they disappeared into the dark alley. "They killed Taz there."
Rock wasn't with the two men as they ran away from the scene. He had taken off down the street as soon as he heard the first shot fired.
"What the fuck happened in here?" the cab driver asked, entering Mike's bedroom. His eyes swept around the room and stopped on the dead body that was still leaking blood even though it was wrapped in sheets. His next words got caught in his throat. "These muthafuckas tried to rob me!” Mike said loudly. "They could've killed all our asses!”
The cabdriver never lifted his eyes from the dead man on the floor. "What are we supposed to do now? You know the police will be here at any time. What are you going to do with that?” He asked, pointing to the body.
"We are going to just throw this muthafucka over the fire escape." Mike said. "Now come over here and help me."
"Are you serious?" The cab driver asked surprised. He walked over to where Mike was kneeled down beside the bloody dead body.
"Hell yeah, I'm serious! When the police do decide to show up the most important thing on their minds will be gettin' the fuck away from here as soon as possible. They don't fuck around over here. They'll just peel his ass up off the street and get on about their business. Now help me do this shit."
Together they picked up the heavy dead body and threw it out on the fire escape. Then they climbed out of the window and pitched the body over the fire escape rail. The body fell hard to the ground, making a hard crunching sound as it hit the concrete.
They went back into the apartment. After Mike cleaned up Taz's blood from the floor and the wall everybody gathered in the living room. In minutes they could hear the sounds of police cars and ambulance sirens.
This neighborhood was infested with drugs and people who would do anything to get them. The police were called to this area on a regular basis and their fear of the people living in this neighborhood gave them all of the reasons they needed to believe that it was always best to spend as little time as possible in the area no matter what the situation was. This night was no different. It was actually better than usual.
"At least it's only one body," said one officer on the scene. "Now let's just hurry up and get out of this dungeon. We don't want to be here when all of these nosey losers start piling around trying to see what's going on. Just scrape his ass up and we'll handle the paperwork back at the station."
They were in and back out of the neighborhood in no more than eight minutes.
All of the lights were turned off in the apartment so that the police wouldn't even think to look inside. All they could see from the outside was the exterior of the abandoned building. The only lights on in the apartment were the small lights on the face of the stereo system and a candle that was burning in the corner of the living room.
"What the hell went on back there?" The cabdriver asked, wanting to know all of what had gone down.
"Ya'll got to understand the type of shit that goes on around here. Everybody here is scandalous so every chance they get, they will get what they have to get, the best way they can get it. All this shit out here (drugs, sex, violence) is like different ‘Gods’ to everybody and they have to serve these ‘Gods’."
You got pimps, hoes, dope dealers, dope addicts, paid niggas, and bums all piled up in one neighborhood. Ain't no harmony over here. I get a good profit here so anytime they catch me slippin', they gon’ try to come up here. Ya'll understand that?"
"Are you mad at me, baby?" the nurse asked pitifully.
"Naw, it's my fault. I should've told you about what would happen if you leave the window open. Just like I told y'all about the door."
"You did tell me to close the window, but I felt so good laying there beside you with that breeze coming in that I fell asleep right there. It won't happen again, baby,  I promise."
"Don't worry about it, we made it by," Mike said, looking back and forth from the nurse to the cabdriver. "I lost a few of my homeboys when I first started working' outta these apartments, the same way we almost got killed tonight. We just gotta be more careful around here." He paused and then continued, “Not too long ago one of my homeys let a Spanish girl in to sex her and she slit his throat while he was sleep and took everything she could carry. The reason I allow y'all to get this close to me is because I know where your mothers stay. When I went to take the money from my jacket from Samone’s house out in Jersey to Kim's house I told her that if anything shady ever happens to me up in here, she has to give the addresses to my big brother, Ron."
"What money?" the nurse asked openly, curious.
"Never mind that," Mike said. "I need to get some rest. We'll talk more in the mornin'."
"Yeah," the cab driver said, yawning. "I'm tired as shit. I'm just going to crash right here."
The nurse went over to the loveseat and laid down on it. She was worrying about her mother all over again.
Mike laid down on the sofa and plotted silently. "If it's the last thing I do I'ma get them muthafuckas who tried to rob me t'night. I saw that nigga Derrick's face. I know his whole crew. I will get mine.”

CHAPTER FOURTEEN
MIKE PREPARES TO GO TO COURT

The next morning Mike woke up feeling well rested. His wounds were practically healed and his walk wasn't as awkward as it had been when he first got back up on his feet. He got dressed and made himself some tea with honey and lemon juice. When he went into the living room, he put Anita Baker’s Rapture album into the stereo system and turned it on very low so that he wouldn't wake the nurse and the cabdriver.
He then sat down on the sofa and rolled up a bag of Chocolate Thai Stick marijuana inside of a Fanta tobacco leaf. He enjoyed the spliff while listening to Anita’s beautiful voice pouring softly through the system's oversized speakers.
Minutes later the nurse woke up. She yawned, greeted Mike, and then stumbled to the bathroom to take a shower. By the time she returned to the living room Mike had a hot cup of tea ready for her. He had also rolled her a spliff to smoke and set aside some coke inside of a rolled up dollar bill for her to sniff. She sat down next to Mike and began to indulge in the treats he had for her. Feeling the drugs taking over her body the nurse began to sip the hot tea and laid her head down on Mike's lap. He stroked the side of her face and her hair as they both relaxed, enjoying the morning. Neither of the two wanted to speak about what had happened the night before.
"Can I ask you something'?" she asked while looking up into Mike's eyes. "Do you ever think about getting out of this line of work and possibly getting a job?"
Mike thought it over for a minute. "No. I can't say that I have," he replied. "All I know is the streets and this shit here."
There was a short silence before he spoke again. "I didn't mention it before because I forgot myself, but I go to court Monday morning in New Jersey for some shit that happened out there.”
"What happened?" the nurse asked, sitting up on the sofa. "Is it serious? Whatever it is, don't you worry about it. I'll be here for you. Can I help?"
"No. Nobody can help me now. I don't really know how serious it's gon' be, but this is my problem. I chose this path so I gotta deal with everything that comes wit’ it. I can handle it. I don't wanna burden nobody wit’ my problems."
"What do you think will happen when you go to court?"
"I don't know, to be honest wit' you. Then again I don't really care. I'll deal with it no matter what it is."
"Well, can I go with you, baby?" "No! I told you I handle my own business." The cab driver had awakened. Neither him nor the nurse was ready for what Mike said next. "Matter of fact, I won't be needin' y'all anymore. I'm back on my feet and I appreciate y'all helpin' me out so I'ma look out for y'all before I go to court so that y'all can get somethin' out here."
"I don't want no damn money from you," the nurse said with tears rolling down her face.
Mike got up and went to the back bedroom. He came back holding a gym bag. He gave both of them ten thousand dollars each from the bag.
"That's for everything y'all did for me through the week. I don't know what's gon' happen when I go to court but if y'all ever need anything I'll make sure y'all know where I am. It's on y'all to get in touch wit' me though."
The nurse jumped up from the sofa and ran to Mike hugging him and crying.
"No, baby," she said pitifully. "I don't wanna leave. I love you. Don't you know that I love you?"
Mike pried the nurse off of him and held her at arm’s length. "You can't love’a nigga like me," he whispered coldly to her as the cabdriver looked on. "Now get your shit and step! Take the money and go. I got your bags packed over by the door. I hope everything works out fine with your mother."
"But, babe, I lost..." she said but got cut off by Mike.
"Don't speak, just do what I said, a'ight?" Then he looked over to the cabdriver.
“As far as that old dude at the liquor store who killed your father, whenever you get it in your heart to avenge your father's death come and see me and I'll go wit' you to fuck him up. I'll be your driver."
The cabdriver just shook his head. He knew that Mike meant every word he said. He and the nurse picked up their money from the coffee table and walked over to the front door. Mike gave them both hugs.
"I want y'all to know that I do appreciate everything." They shook their heads and left the apartment. Mike closed the door behind them and laid back down on the sofa feeling odd inside. He didn't know what the feeling was that he was experiencing.
He tried to make sense of what he had just done. "Shit, it's probably for the best. When I go to court Monday mornin' I might get locked up and those two would break out on me anyway. Fuck that and fuck them. I'm tired of everybody leavin' me stranded. My mother, father, grandparents and everybody else said that they loved me just like that nurse just did and I'll be damned if every time I turn around somebody ain't turnin' their back on me when I'm down. Fuck that shit."
"I got two kids coming' into this world and when they get here they will be all I have to call my own. I know my babies won't take advantage of me so I'll never leave them, if I can help it," he thought to himself. "Damn, I gotta call Ron so he can finally come over here tomorrow. I can count on him."
CHAPTER FIFTEEN
RON IS SUMMONED TO THE HERB GATES BY MIKE TO DISCUSS THE RUNNINGS HOW HE EXPECT THINGS TO BE RAN AFTER MIKE GOES TO COURT

The next morning Ron knocked on the big steel covered door. After peering through the peephole and seeing his big brother standing on the other side of the door, Mike smiled and opened the door letting Ron into the apartment.
Mike was ecstatic to see his brother and it showed when he hugged his surprised brother tightly. Ron had been the original owner of the herb gates, but left it up for grabs after a task force raid that would've landed him in prison if he didn’t escape as he did. He couldn't believe his eyes as they swept over the luxurious apartment. When he had left the herb gates it was in the same condition as the rest of the abandoned building– fucked up. He had decided to open up another "gates" (weed spot) in a safer neighborhood so that he would no longer have to worry about somebody taking his life or him taking somebody else's life everyday and every night. All in all this apartment that he had left behind looked a thousand times better than it did when he had seen it last. Now there was obviously expensive furniture and appliances filling up a space that once was filled with only milk crates, old couches, wooden foldout chairs, and a nineteen inch black and white television. Ron was shocked, impressed, and more than anything he was curious.
"God damn!" he exclaimed. Mike could see the surprise in his brother's eyes as he stood-by proudly. "What the fuck you been doin' here with this place, nigga? You got this spot flyer than my own fuckin' crib. I don't have half of the shit you got up in here in my own place. How the fuck did you get all this shit?"
"Sit down, yo," Mike said. "Let me fill you in on this shit."
Ron sat in the cabby’s favorite chair and Mike went back to the sofa where he could still see the faint impression of the nurse’s body.
"I did it just like you taught me when you were here runnin' the spot for the first year. I just kept reinup (replacing supply) and flippin’ my shit. I made sure the connect had his money and stacked it up⁸ all in the floor of the big closet in the back bedroom, the same spot you used to hold your money in."
"Fuck, how you hustled," Ron said. "Just tell me how the hell you got this place all furnished and equipped like this. That's the biggest TV out and I know I just saw that stereo system in the latest electronics magazine."
Mike looked down at the floor, then looked up to his brother, "I got something to tell you that I ain't too proud of."
"What the hell are you talkin' about, yo?" Ron asked impatiently. "Don't trip, but I've been freebasing'." Mike stared blankly ahead. Ron opened his mouth to speak but Mike raised his hand to silence him.
"Let me finish," he said to Ron. "I got all of the homies in Jersey robbin' and stealin' to support our habits. We hit up houses, stores, and we got females who steal credit cards runnin' up shit in the big department stores. When I first started off I was takin' all the stolen shit to the Dominicans on Broadway uptown, and sellin' it to them. Then I was like, 'Fuck that!' Why should I let them get all the money?" So I started buying' my own coke and selling' it to the homies in Jersey. I cook the shit for them and all that since they don't know how to do it. I blew-up and became a man. The Man, in fact. No doubt about it, big bro'."
Ron nodded his head, "Go on."
Mike continued, "I get everything brought to me from cars to soap to wash my ass."
"How did all this begin?" Ron asked, looking over the contents of the apartment.
"Remember when you sent me to Miami to pick up some ganja for you?" "Yeah?" Ron responded quizzically. "That was a while ago." "Well, that's when I got it in my mind to start this shit, runnin' freebase over to Jersey. That's also when I started freebasing myself. Juaney the connect showed me how to do that shit and I've been on it pretty hard since then, but as you can see yourself, I still hold it down and take care of my business."
"The last time the police raided this spot and I moved out, you tellin' me that you stayed here and continued to run the [spot] on your own?" Ron asked.
"Word!" Mike replied. "You taught me well, my nigga. But this gettin' high shit won't make it. You know what I mean? I just pulled off of that shit because it makes me feel like I'm hustlin' backwards. I didn't know where you went when you left. I thought you had moved to Jersey with Grandma and Chauncey. But I never saw you there."
"I was laying low with my wife and kids, you know? I got a small spot out in the suburbs but it ain't movin' half of what you've been movin' over here. I had heard a little while ago that this spot was booming but I never even thought to guess that it was actually you over here runnin’ shit."
"I've been at this for two years." Mike interjected coolly.
"Yeah, I know." Ron said. "But what are you going to do now? We got to get you off of that bullshit, I can't have my little brother bein' no basehead. You comin' off of it for real?"
"Hell yeah!" Mike said. "That's part of the reason I called you over here." He stood up and headed to the back bedroom.
"I got to show you some shit." He motioned for Ron to follow him.
Mike walked over to the big closet. When he stepped inside he slid a wooden panel from the far back wall that covered a large cast iron safe. He turned the dial on the safe and swung the safe's door open after the proper combination released the steel locking block in the safe.
"Come check this shit out." He called Ron and stepped away from the safe. Ron walked over and when he looked into the safe his jaw dropped in amazement. Half of the safe was packed tight with stacks of money. The other half was packed tight with thick packages.
"Where did you get all that money?" "Never mind that, Ron. Just listen."
Ron's eyes were still trained on the contents of the safe while his mouth remained on autopilot. He continued questioning his younger brother, "...and what the fuck is in those packages?"
Mike quickly took advantage of his big brother's brief pause. "There's almost two hundred thousand dollars and twenty kilos of coke. I got to go to court in Jersey Monday morning and I don't think they will be letting me out when I see the judge. I think they're going to book me up so I'm leavin' all this money and coke to you to keep shit rollin' until I get outta jail. I don't know how long I'll be gone. All I ask you to do is look out for Samone and Kim. I didn't tell you, but both of them are pregnant by me. Plus I want you to find Mommy and give her some money. Speaking' of Mommy, have you heard anything about where she is exactly?"
"No," Ron said. "Nobody knows exactly where she is, all we know is that she is somewhere down in Miami with our sisters. I heard they're with a nigga down there."

CHAPTER SIXTEEN
MIKE LEAVES HIS FORTUNE IN RONS HANDS & HE GETS SENTENCED

Mike and Ron spent the next couple of days together. It was now Sunday night and Mike had really enjoyed the time he'd gotten to spend with his big brother who had shown him the ropes and got him started hustling. He was especially glad that he knew his money would be safe. Ron was family so he could definitely be counted on.
Mike had told Ron all about his plans for the future for him, Samone, Kim and the children he had on the way. He planned to raise the child that he and Kim were having, with Samone. He said that he wanted to open up a grocery store and let Samone run it.
Ron had heard little of his younger brother's plans because Ron himself was lost in his own thoughts of how he would use Mike's money, drugs, and everything else in the Herb Gates to benefit himself. He planned to move his wife out of the rat infested ghetto where they lived barely managing to get by on the little money Ron made from his small marijuana spot, and send his kids to a private school to learn about being proud Rastas. His kids would love him for all the things he would buy them with the money. He would buy a matching pair of BMW's with his and hers license plates. This furniture would look good in his new home he planned to buy. Rhonda always wanted a big screen TV, and the stereo system would be good for their bedroom. They would take the vacation that they had always wanted but couldn't afford, finally they would get a honeymoon. He thought to himself, "I'm the one who got this little nigga started out. He owes me all of this."
"Yo, you listening to me?" Mike asked, looking at his big brother. "Uh, what?" Ron responded. "You said something?" "Yeah I said something, nigga! I've been saying something for about an hour. What the hell are you so deep in thought about?" "Aaaw, nothin' much. Just wondering what the kids are doing, you know?” Ron paused momentarily and then continued, "Well anyway, get some sleep. We have to get up early in the morning so we can get to Grandma's crib and that bastard Chauncey can take you to court. You know he's still down as your legal guardian and you can't go to court by yourself. Do you want me to come wit'cha when you see the judge?"
"Nah, I don't want nobody to be there when I see the judge. I can handle it.”
"You scared?"
"Hell nah, I ain't scared. I don't fear no man but God. Only God can judge me, for real."
After they were done talking, Mike laid back on the sofa and drifted off to sleep thinking about his mother. He wondered what she could be doing as well as how she was doing. He also wondered if she was thinking of him. "I love you Mommy. Wherever you are," he thought to himself just before he dozed off.

CHAPTER SEVENTEEN
RON MAKES HIS MOVE ON MIKES FORTUNE/ CHAUNCEY AND KENNY ACCOMPANY MIKE  TO COURT

The next morning Ron and Mike got up early. They had a very busy day ahead of them. Ron was anxious to get Mike to Jersey. He had hardly gotten any sleep at all because thoughts of all the ways he would use Mike's money continued to run around his mind keeping him awake.
He had taken his shower and was now sitting in the living room while Mike was taking a shower. He sat there overcome with anticipation and decided to call his wife.
He picked up the phone and dialed the number. Rhonda answered on the third ring. "Hello?" she said.
"Hey, baby," Ron answered. "I see you're up early.
"Yeah, I just helped the kids get ready for school. I'm not feeling too good; I'm going to the emergency room after I drop them off."
"What's wrong?"
"It's nothing too serious I don't think. I got cramps in my stomach and I've been throwing up all morning."
Ron thought about her words. "When is your period due?" he asked.
"I expected it about a week and a half ago. It hasn't come yet. I'll know what's up when I go get checked out. We may have something on the way."
"You think so?" he asked.
"Hell, I don't know, but I sure as hell feel like it. If we are having another baby, I don't know what we are going to do. We are having a hard time as it is taking care of Tracy and Raymond," she said.
Ron smiled, "It'll be alright, sweetheart. If we're having another child there won't be nothin' to worry about, I promise you that."
"How can you promise something like that? Have you forgotten where we live and how broke we are? We ain't got shit that we need and if it wasn't for the little bit of money coming from that dead ass spot of yours over in the suburbs we wouldn't have nothing at all. We barely get bills paid, baby, don't forget how fucked up it is over here."
"I ain't forgot nothin' about where we stayin' at right now–until next week. I just called to let you know that I have a surprise for you. I'll tell you all about it when I get home. I'll probably be there by the time you get back from the emergency room."
"What are you talkin' about, Ron?"
"I can't talk about it now but I want you to know it's somethin' big. Somethin' real big, baby, shit's going to change when I get home," he told her.
He heard the shower water in the bathroom shut off. "I gotta go, baby, I'll be home around six, okay?"
"Okay. I hope you're not into nothin' crazy," she said.
"Don't worry about nothin'. I'm cool, and we all gon' be okay when I get home,” he reassured her.
"Alright, I'll talk to you later." She finished.
“Okay, baby.” They both hung up. Ron wanted Mike to hurry up and get dressed so that they could be on their way. He didn't want to appear to be anxious so he just quietly sat and waited for his little brother to get ready to leave. When Mike finally came out ready to go, they left the apartment and headed out to New Jersey.
The brothers were quiet while traveling on Route 80 West making their way to New Jersey. Ron was thinking of the expression that would appear on Rhonda's face when he came home with all of the money Mike was leaving behind. He would rent a U-Haul to carry everything away from the apartment after he and Rhonda chose a new home. He had no plans on getting too involved with the coke. He still had a connect in the Bronx and he planned on selling it all to them for only thirty percent of what it's worth, just to get it off of his hands. He smiled to himself having no shame of the deception he had in his plans for his brother's property and trust.
Mike rested in the passenger seat of the car completely oblivious to the scandalous thoughts running through Ron's head. Mike himself was caught up in thoughts of what was going to happen when he went to court. "They'll probably ship me off to the bing. I'm glad I got my big brother to handle my business for me," he thought privately. "I know he'll take care of things out here."
He also thought about the nurse and the cabdriver. He decided to tell Ron all about them. Mike told Ron that they were good people who looked out for him when he needed a hand and he directed him to look out for them while he was gone.
"Give them my information to whatever jail they send me to," Mike said.
"Cool," Ron replied even though he had no intentions of doing any such thing. "They won't even know how to find me," he thought to himself. "Movin' my family the fuck up out of New York."
When they got to their grandmother's house, Mike got out of the car. Ron remained behind the wheel and didn't turn off the engine.
"Yo, you comin' in?" Mike asked him.  "Nah, man." Ron answered. "You know I aint tryin'a see Chauncey's punk-ass. That nigga always hated me because I ain't his and I can't stand his ass likewise. I don't feel like hearin' no bullshit from him about how Kenny is just so much better than us, so I'm just gon' get back to the city and check up on my wife. I ain't talked to her since I left home Friday."
"A'ight, homey." Mike said and reached into his pocket pulling out a key ring. He handed it to Ron.
"The smallest joint on the ring is for the 'herb gates,' the rest of 'em are just there." Mike said. "I don't know how long I'll be gone but I'll see you when I get back. Don't forget to do everything I told you."
"I got you, little bruh," Ron said. They gave each other a pound and Mike closed the car door. Ron drove away smiling as Mike entered their grandmother's house.
"Your dad's on his way right now to pick you up for court," Mike's grandmother said coldly.
"Yeah whatever," Mike responded sitting down on the sofa in the living room.
"Don't you use that tone of voice with me. That's why you are in some shit right now. You think you're grown. We're going to see how grown you are when they get you up in that courtroom," she said.
Just then there was the sound of a car horn just outside.
"That's him, now get out of here, boy." Mike's grandmother said while looking out the window facing the front yard.
Mike got up and walked out of the house to Chauncey's clean new Cadillac Coupe DeVille and got into the back seat. Kenny was sitting in the passenger seat up front.
"We got to be there at nine o'clock," Chauncey said to Mike. "I'll be right back, I gotta tell Momma something."
Chauncey left the car door open as he went up to the front of the house where his mother was standing with her arms folded across her chest. Chauncey hugged her and gave her a peck on the cheek. Then he whispered something in her ear, looking back to the car.
Mike watched this all without saying a word. Kenny turned to him, "Yo, what do you think will happen when you get to court?"
"I don't know, bro, and I don't really give a fuck. They can't tell me shit. God ain’t said what I do is wrong and until he does, I'ma keep on doin' whatever I gotta do to get by. He's the only one who decides what's right and what's wrong. I'll do as I damn well please until He shows me otherwise."
"No matter what you do you're my brother and you got my support," Kenny said, giving Mike a pound.
As Kenny turned around Chauncey was slipping into the driver's seat. He backed the car out of the driveway and headed off to the courthouse. Nobody said a word during the ride. Kenny thought about the situation at hand. He was glad that it wasn't him in Mike's position, on his way to be thrown in jail. On the same note he was unhappy that his brother had so many trials and tribulations to overcome in his life. Regardless of what anyone said about Mike, Kenny loved him and had his back. Nobody could say anything wrong about Mike to Kenny because it would all go into one ear and out of the other. To Kenny, Mike wasn't the criminal minded treacherous thug that people said him to be. To Kenny, Mike was a young man who was forced to take care of himself the best and only way he knew how. To Kenny, Mike was the only little brother that he had. He would love his brother no matter what happened and stand strongly beside him. Simply because of the fact that Mike had the strength and heart that Kenny only wished he had himself, Kenny felt proud of himself for being proud of Mike.
Kenny thought of what he wanted to do with his future. He’d been working at his father's carpentry company everyday before and after school receiving no payment for his labor. All the while he worked he was learning the ins and outs of running a business and he planned on starting a company of his own once he completed college. He loved his father although he strongly disliked the cruel words Chauncey spoke of Mike and Ron. Kenny felt that family was everything and that family should support each other under all circumstances.
"We're here," Chauncey said, parking the car in the parking lot of the courthouse.
They all got out of the car and walked into the huge stone courthouse.
"Can you tell me where the juvenile division is?" Chauncey asked the attractive dark skinned woman sitting at the receptionist desk.
"Take the elevator to the third floor and when you get off go to your right. You can't miss it," she answered.
They did as she said and found themselves at the entrance of the courtroom. Moments after they entered, the bailiff called Mike's name.
"Is there a Michael McCormick present?" "Yeah, I'm here," Mike said. "Well, we need you to go stand beside your lawyer with your guardian. We're a little behind here." "I don't have a lawyer," Mike said standing and walking up to the front. Chauncey followed. The Court has appointed an attorney to your case; he's the gentleman standing right there to your left. I need everyone to take their seats, the Judge is on his way."
