Mike and Chauncey sat at the attorney's table. Kenny sat directly behind them.
"All rise!" the bailiff said loudly. "Court is now in session. Judge Thomas Brown is now presiding over case number eighty-nine dash twenty-one thirteen."
Everyone in the courtroom stood up. Just then the door leading to the Judge's chambers opened and the Judge walked up to his place behind the large podium.
"All be seated," the Judge said, leafing through a stack of papers on the podium while everyone sat down in their seats. Judge Brown looked over the top of his glasses in Mike's direction.
"I need you to state your name before this Court for the Clerk's records. Please stand."
Mike and his attorney both stood up. "My name is Michael McCormick," Mike said with the corner of his mouth twisted.
"Alright, Mr. McCormick. I understand that you have been appointed an attorney who is here representing you now. I also understand that your legal guardian is also present. Is this all true?"
"Yeah, it's true," Mike responded arrogantly.
Your case number is eighty-nine dash twenty-one thirteen, and you are before the Court today being charged with breaking and entering and receiving stolen property. Do you understand the nature of the charges being brought against you?"
"Yeah." Mike replied flatly.
The Judge then looked at Mike's attorney, "Mr. Sanders, I believe it is?"
"Yes, Sir," the attorney answered.
"Okay," the Judge said. "How does your client wish to plead to the charges being brought against him?"
"Not guilty, your Honor." Sanders answered.
"For the record, the defendant Michael McCormick has entered a plea of not guilty in case eighty-nine dash twenty-one thirteen," the Judge stated. "Now the Court orders that Mr. McCormick be remanded to the custody of the Sheriff pending trial. Mr. McCormick your bond will be set at ten percent of twenty-five thousand dollars. Do you understand?"
"Yeah," Mike said with a disturbingly passive expression on his face. "Court is adjourned," the Judge said. "All rise!" the bailiff said loudly again. Everyone rose from their seats and waited for the Judge to leave to sit back down. Kenny had tears running down his face, as did Chauncey. Two deputy sheriffs came and put Mike in handcuffs. Mike looked over at Chauncey.
"Don't sit there fakin' in front of these people like you give a fuck about me, nigga," he whispered coldly. "You probably told the Judge to do this shit. Fuck you, nigga!" Then he looked at Kenny and dropped his head as the deputy sheriffs lead him away.
Kenny put his hand on his father's shoulder and gently squeezed it. Chauncey placed his hand on top of Kenny's. They both continued to cry.

CHAPTER EIGHTEEN
MIKE IS TAKEN INTO CUSTODY AND HAS A MAJOR RUN IN WITH ANOTHER INMATE

Mike was taken to his new home, which was an eight foot by ten foot cell. The Corrections Officer brought him a bedroll that contained a wool blanket, two white sheets that were worn thin, three towels, three washcloths, and a flat pillow. His cell was hollow and only had a metal bunk bed, a steel sink, and a toilet in it.
"All inmates on B Block report to the hall! All inmates on B Block report to the chow hall!" came a deep voice from the loudspeaker.
Mike set his bedroll on the empty bottom bunk and sat down on the edge of it. Before he was assigned to block B he had to receive a mandatory strip search and was rushed out afterward. He didn't have time to tie his shoes so when he walked on the block the laces of his new suede playboy shoes dangled along the floor. Now he had a chance to tie them. After he tied his shoelaces he walked out of the cell and began to follow the other inmates to the chow hall, speaking to no one on his way. From behind him he felt a tap on his shoulder. He figured it was an inmate passing by who had accidently brushed up against him. Then he felt another tap on his shoulder.
"Yo, little nigga," came a voice from behind him. "What's up with those shoes?"
Mike turned around to see who it was talking to him. There stood a tall muscular inmate with three others. All of them were looking down at Mike as he turned around.
"What the fuck you mean what's up with these shoes?" Mike asked. His heart was beating fast and he swept his eyes across the group of inmates preparing for any kind of violence that would occur. All of them were larger than Mike but none of this mattered to him. "If they bring some drama," he thought to himself, "They may get-out on me but each one of these niggas will know they been in some real shit."
"Nigga, you know the game," the tallest inmate who was evidently the leader of the bunch said. "Run them joints unless you want some heat."
"Nigga..." Mike began to speak but was cut short by a C.O. who was monitoring the hallway.
"Let's get a move on, gentlemen! Get to the chow hall now! If you bastards wanted to hold a fuckin' family reunion you should've kept your black asses on the streets. Now you're on my time so get going!"
The tallest inmate responded, "Yeah, yeah, whatever man, we movin'." He looked back to Mike. "We'll see you later, nigga." He said before leading his group to the chow hall. Mike followed a few seconds later and ate his skimpy meal with his mind on the inmates who had approached him. "Now I see I gotta watch these muthafuckas," he thought to himself. "If they want some shit, I'll give 'em some shit."
When Mike went back to the housing unit, he immediately went to the C.O.
"Excuse me, I need some cleaning supplies and a mop bucket please," he said to the C.O. who was sitting in the front office.
"Just a second," the officer said. He then reached under his desk and pulled out two bottles containing Lysol and disinfectant. When he gave them to Mike he walked him over to the cleaning equipment closet and unlocked the door so that Mike could get a bucket of water, mop, and a mop wringer.
Mike took the supplies to his cell and sat down on the edge of his bunk again plotting on what he should do. There was the sound of footsteps coming towards his cell. Mike could hear the voice of the tall inmate who had approached him in the hallway. He reached for the mop wringer, grabbing it by the handle, and moved to the back wall of his cell. Suddenly his door swung open and in stepped the tall inmate he had seen earlier, followed by his three partners.
"You comin' up off them shoes, dawg?" the tall muscular inmate said with an expression on his face that was supposed to be intimidating.
"You tryin'a take 'em up off me?" Mike replied, leaning back on the mop wringer he was holding behind his back.
The big young inmate balled up his fist and began to move in on Mike. "Hey y'all let's smash this little nigga, he talkin' real greasy to be a new jack," he said. The other three inmates followed close behind their leader. When the biggest inmate got within arm's reach of Mike he threw his fists up and prepared to swing at Mike's face. That's when Mike exposed the mop wringer into view. The tallest inmate didn't have a chance to block or dodge the blow that came to his head. The mop wringer hit him so hard in the chin that his body dropped instantly as blood rushed out of the deep slash that appeared across his now broken jaw. He was unconscious. One of the three inmates who came with him didn't see the other two who had run from the cell so he rushed at Mike expecting to have help, only to receive worse punishment than his leader had taken. Mike hit him across the nose with the mop wringer breaking it.
"You broke my fuckin nose, man!" the remaining inmate said as he dropped down to his knees.
"Fuck you, bitch-ass nigga!" Mike said as he hit him with the mop wringer again. "You niggas still want these shoes! Huh?! You want 'em, muthafucka?!" He began to kick the fallen inmates' bloody bodies.
"Stop that shit right now, you fuckin' nigger animal!" came the voice of the C.O. who had given Mike the cleaning supplies. He had pressed the panic switch on his walkie-talkie and within seconds the entrance of Mike's cell was packed with officers.
"Step back against the wall! Now!" One of the officers yelled to Mike. The officers rushed in on Mike when he refused to cease kicking the two inmates laying on his floor. Mike was hit with a stun gun and passed out.
When Mike regained consciousness he found himself surrounded by darkness. His entire body was in pain and as he felt his face he realized that his left eye was swollen shut. His wrist and ankles were also swollen. Mike stood up and winced in pain. "What the fuck," he said and heard his own voice echo back to him. Suddenly a dim light came on in the cell that was operated from the outside of the cell. There was a crackling sound coming from the intercom that was on the wall of the cell.
"Hey, you in there, get your ass up," came the voice of the lieutenant of the jail. "You hear me, boy? Get your black ass up!"
"Yo, fuck you! I'm up." Mike responded.
"Well listen here, boy," the lieutenant's voice continued. "Those two niggers you jumped on received over two hundred stitches apiece. You know what that means?"
"Nah, man," Mike said.
"That means that from this day until I feel like letting you out of the hole, your little black ass belongs to me. So make yourself at home, boy because that shit pit you're in is going to be your home for a while."
"Man, fuck you!"
"No man, fuck you, buddy. We'll see if you still have that attitude when you've been in darkness for a while. You see that light in your cell?" he asked Mike.
"Yeah."
"Well keep it in your mind because I only turn on lights when I like somebody. And I don't like you. Make yourself comfortable, nigger." Then there was a laugh before the lieutenant switched off of the intercom. The dim light was turned off leaving Mike in complete darkness once again. This solitary confinement was nothing more than a dungeon. There was no toilet, no bed, and no sink. Instead there was a hole in the floor made for the inmate inside to release their body's waste. There was only a cold concrete floor to sleep on. And there was a small pail of water by the door. This pail was only filled once per day so Mike had to use it carefully. The cell reeked of piss and shit from the inmate who had occupied the cell before Mike, and possibly from others before him. At first the smell alone was too much for Mike to take and he would find himself throwing up bile into the hole in the floor.
This cell reminded Mike of the base houses he used to visit regularly and the filthy project housing units and abandoned buildings he once lived in during his time of survival.

CHAPTER NINETEEN
TRINA VISITS MIKE IN THE COUNTY JAIL AND FINDS OUT WHAT MIKE IS UP TO IN THE STREETS/ MIKE FINDS OUT ALL HE HAS IS HIS MOTHER TRINA

Mike was awakened by three guards and told he had a visitor. Figuring that it had to be Ron coming to update him on what was happening with his money and drugs, and bond him out, Mike got right up and went with the guards down to the visitation room.
He was in shackles and handcuffs. He was ordered to sit down and informed that his visitor would be in the visitation room shortly. He felt terrible from being in the hole for two weeks but he looked far worse than he felt. He looked dirty, and sickening. He was just grateful that his brother was here to bond him out and he couldn't wait to leave this jail.
While he sat waiting for Ron to make it up to the visitation room, Mike tried to make himself as comfortable as he could with the shackles and handcuffs on. It was nearly impossible.
"Yo!" Mike called out to one of the guards on duty.
"Can you loosen up these cuffs some, they're cutting off my fuckin' circulation."
A guard came over to Mike to look over the iron around Mike's wrists and ankles. While he inspected the shackles and handcuffs a black woman entered the visitation room. Mike only saw the back of her head because when she came in she happened to look to her right searching for her loved one trapped behind the walls of the jail. Mike's look at the woman was nothing more than a quick glance. His attention was on the tight shackles around his wrists.
"These joints are fuckin' me up, yo," Mike said. "[I might] fuck around and pass out."
"Well," the guard said. "We heard about you, McCormick, and I'm tellin' you that I won't let you be bothered up here as long as you stay cool. Alright?"
"Yeah, man. I'm cool."
"Alright then, I'll loosen up the cuffs for you," the guard said and then reached to give Mike's wrists more space to move inside of the cuffs.
"God damn, these things are tight as hell!" The guard had an angry expression plastered on his face. "You know the guards name who put these on you?" he asked Mike.
"Nah, I don't know, man. They just woke me up."
"Well, I'm Captain Davis, the Captain of this jail. This type of shit causes unnecessary trouble and if it happens again I want to hear about it. Alright, son?"
Mike didn't get a chance to answer the Captain's question because his heart was beating faster than it had ever beaten before. His eyes were on the black woman who had entered the visitation room searching for the person she came there to see. He couldn't take his eyes off of her and as they made eye contact tears began to roll down his face.
“You alright, son?" the Captain asked and then followed Mike's gaze to the black woman.
"Oh," the Captain said, "your visitor's here. Well I'll get back to the front. Don't you forget what I told you, McCormick." Then he walked away.
The black woman walking toward Mike placed her face inside of her hands and began to cry uncontrollably. Mike was unable to find his voice as he stared in disbelief that his mother was standing before him.
"Oh my baby, my baby, I'm so sorry, baby," Trina cried. "Look at what time has done to you." She went around the small table where Mike was sitting and bent down to hug him. She stood there with her head on his shoulders crying for a few minutes and letting him do likewise. She had missed him dearly and hated herself for not being able to keep him with her. She had been told various times by Chauncey that Mike was okay and was better off staying close by him instead of moving down to Miami with her. Chauncey had grudgingly held back the truth about Mike's living because he knew that even though Mike was a young thug, his mother still loved him more than she loved anything, so he made her believe that he and Mike were getting along fine and that Mike never had any words or thoughts of her.
Chauncey had never really forgiven her for having babies outside of their marriage when he had gone to prison years before.
In fact it had been Chauncey's mother who had recently informed Trina of Mike's ways of living and the way he survived. Trina caught the next plane to New Jersey after she heard the truth from Chauncey's mother.
Now here she was with her youngest baby finally in her arms again.
"I never should've let him have you. I never should've let him have you, baby. I love you so much."
"I love you too, Mommy, and I've been tryin'a find you for a while now. Where were you?" Mike asked as Trina stood and went to her seat at the table.
"Baby, I've been in Florida for the past eight years. I was made to believe that I wasn't needed."
"Who made you believe that?" Mike asked. "I have needed you since the day you left."
"Baby, it's not important who made me believe it, all that's important is that I have you now and I'm never leaving you again, baby. You are mine and I love you."
She spent most of the time of the visit telling Mike where she lived and how she had been doing over the years. She told him that she thought of him every day and that before she caught the plane to New Jersey she had taken enough money from the man she lived with, to bond Mike out.
"Don't worry about a thing, baby," she said. "Mommy is here to help you." "Did you hear from Ron?" Mike asked her. "No, Sweetheart. I haven't heard from him. He knows I'm in town though. I already gave the bail bondsman the bail money for you to leave here so they should be doing the paperwork now. The man up front said that you should be out in less than an hour, baby. Why do they have those handcuffs on you, Mike?" she asked. "You're no animal."
"I'll tell you all about it when I get home, okay?" Mike replied.
"Okay, baby."
"Ma'am?" the Captain called out from behind his station. "I'm sorry but we have to close down now." That's when Trina got up and went to Mike helping him stand up. She hugged him tightly and kissed him on the forehead.
"I love you, baby, and I'll see you later okay?" she said.
"I love you too, Mommy, and I'm glad you came for me." Mike said and watched as she left the visitation room.
CHAPTER TWENTY
TRINA HIRES MIKE AN ATTORNEY AND FINDS OUT WHAT MIKE HAS BEEN UP TO IN NYC

Do I gotta stay up here until I bail out?" Mike asked the Captain after he had been sitting in the visiting room twenty minutes after his mother had gone.
"I don't know what’s going on but I got a call from the front desk telling me that they have some kind of mix up. Your mother and the bail bondsman she hired are supposed to be on their way back to let you know what's going on."
Mike sat back and tried to relax. He didn't have the slightest idea what was going on with his bond being paid. When he had been sitting there for about forty-five minutes the door leading out of the visitation room opened and Trina walked through it along with a white man whom Mike correctly assumed was the bail bondsman. Mike noticed that his mother looked stressed. There were fresh tears rolling down her face. When they got close to Mike, the white man reached his arm over to shake Mike's hand.
"My name is William Stover, your mother's hired me to oversee your bond payment. I'm here to let the both of you know that there is a problem."
"What kind of problem are you talking about?" Trina asked looking worried. "What are they trying to do to my baby? I gave them the money now what's going on here?"
William knew that little Mike here was far from being a "baby." He knew that a "baby" wouldn't and couldn't have a criminal record like the one he had just read with little Mikey's name on it. But people like Mike kept people like William in business.
"I have some bad news Mrs. McCormick. It appears that Mike has a detainer posted on him from New York City for at least two murders and robberies. Charges are also being pressed against your son for attempted murder on one of the inmates here and felonious assault on another. Last night he attacked them with a mop wringer or something and that's why he's in those chains. One of the assaulted inmates received nearly two hundred stitches in the face and head area. That young man is currently in a coma and expected to die. The doctors said that even if he lives he will probably suffer from brain damage."
Mike just looked down at the floor without saying a word. Hearing all of this being said in his mother's presence made Mike feel a little bit ashamed. Trina held his hand and rubbed the back of it to let him know that she was on his side no matter what was said or what was done.
William continued, "They have Mike locked up down in solitary confinement here in the jail and in the morning they will be transferring him to the adult jail and trying him as an adult. I'm very sorry Mrs. McCormick but at this moment there is nothing that I can do to help. If New York gives Mike a bail, I could assist you since my firm has nationwide offices including one in the City. I don't know how to advise you on how you should handle these charges he has in New Jersey but I will inform you that these charges are very serious. Mike is still young so if by chance he is to receive the minimum punishment for the murders he would be home before he turns forty-years-old,"
"What?" Trina asked shaking her head.
"Yes, Mrs. McCormick these charges are that serious and you have to keep that in mind when you're getting a lawyer to represent your son. I say this because if you have the right amount of money placed aside you can pay the right people and only have him serve a year or two. That's realistically speaking because after all, they are charging Mike as an adult. I bid both of you good luck and Mrs. McCormick, if you ever need me for anything you can call my office and I will have you taken care of the best way I can."

CHAPTER TWENTY-ONE
MIKE EXPLAINS TO TRINA WHAT HE HAS BEEN DOING TO SURVIVE WHILE SHE HAS BEEN GONE AND SHARES HIS CONCERNS WITH TRINA

Once Mr. Stover left the visitation room Mrs. McCormick stayed behind.
"Can I stay with my son for a few more minutes, please?" Trina asked the Captain who was still at his station.
"Yes, ma’am," the Captain said. "How about fifteen minutes? I would normally have the place closed down but it seems like you have something important going on and this young man hasn't caused any trouble so I don't mind you having some extra time. Actually he did the jail a big favor by straightening out those roughnecks last night."
"Thank you," Trina responded gratefully. "No problem." Trina turned to Mike and looked into his eyes, "Mike what have you gotten us into, baby?" she asked. "Mommy I had no choice in how I came up. I was forced to move out of Chauncey's and Grandma's house because all they did was talk bad about you, me and Ron. I got sick and tired of it. Chauncey wouldn't tell me where you were. He always called you names and told me and Ron that you cheated on him when he went to jail back in Jamaica before I was even born. That's how I found out that Ron had a different father. Then Chauncey threw Ron out of the house in New Jersey. When Ron left I decided to leave with him because I knew I couldn't take it without him, if I had to stay with Chauncey and Grandma. Chauncey always used to tell me that he doesn't think I'm his son. He says that you probably cheated on him and had me because I'm dark skinned while he's light skinned. So I said 'to hell with him' and left to go live with Ron. Chauncey always told us that you don't care about us so all we had was ourselves when we went to New York. We started hustlin' in one neighborhood and had our own spot that Ron ran. He left when the police came to bust it and got away. I told him that I was going to stay with Aunt Karen but I stayed at the spot alone. I told Aunt Karen that if Ron called to tell him I'm outside or at a friend's house. I promised to send her a hundred dollars a week so that she can get her some liquor. She sucks up more gin than the law should allow. In the meantime, I was running the spot all by myself thinkin' that Ron had moved back to New Jersey to be with Grandma and sorry-ass Chauncey. I even spent a lot'a nights there hopin' to see him so he could come back to our spot but I never saw him until right before I got here. All I had was his phone number but I didn't call because I didn't know how he would take what I was doin'."
"Baby, what were you doing out there in the streets?" Trina asked looking upset.
"Well, Mommy, to make it simple, plain, and short, I did just about everything I could do to survive out there," Mike said looking down at the table. "I started smoking freebase cocaine and that's what had me robbin' and breakin' into people's homes to support my drug habit. In time the police got on my trail but I got around them and kept on hustlin' and usin' drugs.
"You don't need no drugs in your life, son," Trina said to Mike. "I'll be here from now on, you hear me?"
"Yes, I hear you, Mommy." Mike replied. "I have something else to tell you, Mommy."
"What else is it, baby?" "I have two babies comin'." "Well, baby, if you can handle it, that's great news. I'll love them as much as I love you. So something good has come out of all of this. I want to get to know the girls who are having these babies, okay?"
"Okay, Mommy. I'll give you their phone numbers later. Enough about me, tell me what happened when you came to America."
Trina rubbed the back of Mike's hand again and spoke after a short time of silence. "It's a pretty long story Mike, but I'll try my best to give you as much as I can in the short version. First of all, when Chauncey and I came to America we were living in Brooklyn. Chauncey spent a lot of time looking for work and he started to treat me differently, but I stuck by him because I thought he really wanted to be a family and all. I was lost here in America because the only thing I knew how to do was take care of the house. I helped Chauncey fill out the paperwork to get his mother into this country because Kenny was living with her and I missed all of y'all so much that I was grateful to god for giving me the chance to see one of y'all at the time. A little while after Chauncey's mother got over here, they just up and left me all by myself. I didn't know anything about this country so it was pretty hard to get around. And since I didn't know where Chauncey and his mother had taken Kenny to live I didn't have anybody to turn to. I still tried to make it over here, but it was getting harder and harder the more I tried. In time I realized that Chauncey had planned the whole thing. He never forgave me for having Ron and Ronnette when he was in jail and after he got on his feet over here in America he left me high and dry. I was so broken from seeing Kenny in the little time I saw him before they took him and moved away, I was missing all of my babies.
"I thought about you and how good it used to feel when you would wrap your little hands around my neck and hug me. Being all alone was driving me crazy and I couldn't take it so I took the time to get enough money to go back to Jamaica. I checked around for Chauncey but Karen and everybody else just laughed at me and told me how stupid I was for thinking he would ever forgive me for cheating on him when he was locked up. I had nothing, baby, so I headed to Jamaica and the bus made a stop down in Miami and while I was waiting down there to catch a plane, I saw some advertisements for some good jobs that I could work even without any experience so I made up my mind to give it a try for a few months. That worked out well and in no time I had a nice apartment to stay in. I still wanted my babies though, so I kept on calling my mother letting her know not to let y'all go if Chauncey ever tried to come down there and steal y'all away from me. I met a real good man in Miami and I've been staying with him for almost the whole time I've been in this country, since I was only in New York for about six months.
"Anyway, I called my mother one day to surprise her and tell her that I could finally take care of all of y'all and that I was on my way to come and get all my babies. That's when I found out that you and Ron had already left. She told me that Ron had brought you over here and she was crying and everything because she hadn't heard anything from neither one of y'all, and she didn't know where y'all were at. I was worried sick and I knew that y'all were looking for Chauncey so I started looking for him myself. If it wasn't for Karen I would never have found him. She had found out that Chauncey started his own company in New Jersey. That's how I found out that he was living in New Jersey the whole time that he had left me. When y'all found him yourselves I was glad to know you two just weren't over here lost, and plus, Chauncey kept on telling me that y'all were okay up there, getting along good, and that y'all didn't need me or want to hear from me. All I ever wanted was for my babies to be happy so I didn't raise no hell. Not until now.
"My fiancé is a hustler too, and when I told him that I needed to go to New Jersey, he thought that I was trying to get back with Chauncey so he didn't give me the money I needed. So I took it as soon as he went to sleep. I know he'll understand once he meets you. I missed you like crazy all this time, baby, and I won't ever leave again. I don't have enough money to get a lawyer right now but I'll try my best to do what I can." "Don't worry about that," Mike said to his mother. "Ron has some money that I left out there with him. He will be taking care of you. I'll call him when I get the chance to let him know what Mr. Stover said and that we need some money as soon as possible."
The Captain made his way over to the table where Mike was talking to his mother; he had given them an extra half hour visit. "Sorry, ma’am," he said looking at his watch. "They're getting on my case downstairs, so I got to close down now. I'm going to do what I have to for Mike to be released from the hole since he's being transferred tomorrow. We'll put him down in intake so that he can at least use the phone. Is that okay?"
"I guess so," Trina said. Then she turned back to Mike and hugged him close to her. "Oh, baby. I'm so glad I found you." She began to cry all over again. "I promise I'll be with you no matter what happens, baby."
"Everything will be aight, Mommy. I will call Ron and we will all be aight, you just watch and see."
"Baby, I just want you to remember everything I told you when you were a little boy when I took you to the zoo before I came to America." Then she turned around and walked away from the visiting room once again.
"I remember, Mommy. " Mike said lowly. "I remember."

CHAPTER TWENTY-TWO
RON IGNORES MIKES CALLS AND SKIPS OUT ON HIM

Ron was busy getting everything ready and situated for his new home out of town. The day Mike had been taken to the county jail, Ron had taken the money and drugs from the herb gates to his own home in the ghetto where Rhonda was waiting for him. She had surprised him with the news that she was five weeks pregnant. She was worried about how they would get by feeding a new mouth when it was so hard to feed the mouths they already had. All of her worries were washed away when Ron opened up the large garbage bag he had brought in the house with him and poured two hundred thousand dollars on the floor in front of her.
"Oh shit, baby!" she said. "Where did all this Money come from? Is it all ours?"
"Yeah, sweetheart," Ron said, kissing his wife on the cheek. "It's all ours."
Then they began making plans on how they would use the money. It was hard for them to decide on exactly where they would live. Their choices were Florida, California, and Atlanta. After a long debate, they finally decided on Atlanta. Purchasing a home was simple and Rhonda even got their children registered into a good private school for the next semester.
Now they were back in New York City, removing all of Mike's furniture and appliances from the apartment he had lived in and nearly died trying to keep. The herb gates was now entirely empty. All of its contents had been loaded inside of two U-Haul trucks headed to Atlanta. The only item left in the apartment was the telephone. When Rhonda went over to unplug the phone it began to ring. She was startled and jumped away from it. She let it ring five times before she answered it as Ron looked on.
"Hello?" Rhonda spoke into the telephone. The operator answered. "You have a collect call from Mike." Rhonda looked over to her husband. "Baby, it's Mike." Ron put his hand to his long thick dreadlocks as if he had a hard decision to make. His dreadlocks were thick and hung far past his shoulders. They were so thick that the top of them were usually puffed up even when the front locks were pushed to the back. This caused some people to think that his locks were probably fake extensions. "Tell him I'm not here, I'll talk with him later when we get organized."
Rhonda hung the phone up and unplugged it from the wall. Ron hadn't told her that all the money and drugs were Mike's and she didn't press the issue. She was just glad that her family was now financially well off. Her husband had come through for her. "He's the best," she thought to herself following Ron out of the apartment and closing the door behind her. The herb gates was now officially closed down.
Mike figured that something was wrong with the jail telephone or that he had accidentally pressed the wrong button on the keypad so he tried again. When his call didn't go through for the second time, Mike began to worry that the herb gates had been raided. He worried for the entire day waiting for his chance to try again the next morning. When he finally called again the following morning, Mike was shocked even more. "The number you have dialed has been disconnected. Please hang up and try another telephone number."
"Something must be wrong," Mike said lowly, hanging the phone up and trying Kim's phone number. "Something has to be wrong, because I know Ron wouldn't shut the spot down." He knew that his big brother, whom he loved so much and trusted even more, wouldn't play him.
Kim's phone rang four times before it was picked up. "Hello?" Kim's voice came over the phone. "Who is it?" "It's me, Kim." Mike answered. "Oh." Kim said in a withdrawn tone of voice.
"Have you heard anything from Ron?" Mike asked.
"Yeah, he came over the day before yesterday. He told me to tell you that he was closing down the spot, he said he was moving out of town. He said that they found a big house to live in and raise the kids in, you know they havin’ another baby right now. He just said that he was sorry but he was retiring and moving away with his family. He was drivin' one of those new BMW 750's and Rhonda had the kids in a new Minivan."
"Oh yeah?" Mike said disgustedly. His thoughts were running through his head rapidly.
"Yeah," Kim said. "I don't know where he is moving to, all he said was that they were going down South. I don't know what's going on."
"I don't know either, but I do know that I'll see what's up," Mike said. "So, what's going on with you?"
“Not too much," she said. "But, umm, I got something to tell you." "What is it?" "Well, baby, I love you and you know that, but I see it all on the news that you are locked up in Jersey and that they might bring you back to New York for some murder charges. They said that you will be locked up in jail for at least twenty-five years. That's a long time, baby, so me and Mommy both decided that it's best for me to move on with my life and be with somebody that can help me-raise my baby. I know you don't want me to wait twenty-five years for you to come home, baby, that's too long. So what I'm sayin' is that I found somebody else. You remember Frankie from my building? Well, he told me he loved me and will be here to help me with my child. He goes to Lamaze class with me and everything. He plans on being there in the delivery room. I know you understand, baby."
None of what Kim had said surprised Mike, at all. "Oh," he said casually, "don't worry about me, just look out for yourself and for our child. That's all I ask. I hope you stay happy, 'cause that will keep the baby happy. It's all good as long as no nigga don't be up in there puttin' his hands on my child and as long as you don't have my child callin' some nigga, "Daddy," like I ain't nothin'. And when I come home and come to see my child I don't want no bullshit comin' up."
"I know," Kim said. "Let me worry about that. You can write and call to check on the baby when he gets here if you want."
"Yeah," Mike replied. "A'ight, I'll holla at you later."
"Okay, baby." Kim responded before finishing with, "I love you," and then hanging the phone up gingerly.
Mike hung up and and then made his way back to his cell. "Damn!" he thought privately. "I can't send my mother over there to meet Kim. Ms. Johnson hates my ass."
He went and laid down on his new bed in the adult section of the county jail where he was transported after he had been charged as an adult at the age of thirteen. This was common when a youth was charged with offenses similar to those Mike was being charged with.
He couldn't get his mind off of Ron. Ron had closed down the spot without telling him and moved away with everything Mike had. He didn't give Mike a single dime to help out with his case, nor did he give their mother anything. It was obvious that Ron didn't give a damn about what happened to him. Knowing this made Mike highly upset and kept him tossing and turning all through the night.

CHAPTER TWENTY-THREE
RON RUNS INTO HAVIC TRYING TO MAKE MOVIES BEHIND MIKES BACK

Ron was on his way to sell the remainder of the drugs Mike had left behind.
Pulling up to the dilapidated building on 137th Street between 7th and 8th Avenue with an old friend that he trusted, Ron looked around to see if the buyer was there yet. His friend's name was Tarique and he was also a Rastafarian.
They both got out of the car and went inside of the record store at the bottom of the building. There was a man standing at the back looking over the cassettes. There was a leather briefcase in his hand. Ron approached him.
"You got the stuff?" the man asked after Ron came beside him and spoke their chosen code word.
"Yeah," Ron answered in an exaggerated accent, "Me got it, Star." He opened the bag showing the man that he had brought the drugs with him. When he looked back up he was staring down the barrel of a nickel-plated .357 magnum. There was another Rasta standing close by who had seen the man pull the gun out. He immediately dove to the floor and placed his hands behind his head. "Oh shit, mon, don't shoot me! Don’t shoot me!" he begged.
The gunman looked sharply over to Tarique who was standing over by a rack of old records scared half to death. "You two muthafuckas get down just like this 'bloodclot' and keep your mouths closed."
Both men dropped down to their knees and lowered themselves down to their stomachs and palms. The gunman then proceeded to check all three men for weapons. When he found none he smiled big. This shit was easier than he thought it would be. He snatched the white plastic shopping bag that Ron had brought into the store and began to walk out of the record store. Before he reached the exit, Ron had reached under his hat and retrieved the 22. Caliber handgun that he kept entwined in his thick locks. This is why his hair was puffed on the top. When he had the gun in his hand he turned his body around and fired three shots in the back of the man who had tried to rob him.
"Oh shit!” the man screamed as he fell down to his knees and tumbled over on his face where he died. The bullets had bounced around in his body destroying most of his vital organs. Blood was pouring profusely from the holes in his body.
Ron got up quickly and snatched up the bag of cocaine and the briefcase. "Tarique!" he yelled, not caring who heard his comrade’s name. "Come on, Son, we got to get the fuck away from here!"
They ran out of the store and jumped into the car they had arrived in. Rubber from their tires was tatted to the asphalt as Ron quickly sped away from the scene.
When they reached Ron's apartment, which was now empty, Ron told Tarique about his plans to move down South with his family. He had no more plans of running the streets hustling. He had only wanted to sell the drugs at one third of their actual price so that he would be rid of them quickly.
He looked inside of the briefcase and saw that the money was there. It was over one hundred thousand dollars. He closed the briefcase and handed the bag of Coke to Tarique.
"Yo, the old spot we used to work out of is empty now," he said to Tarique. "You should be able to get shit back pumping with that. You got eight kilos here homie, that's more than enough to get the door opened."
"Yeah, mon! Good lookin' out." Tarique replied.
"I ain’t got no time for the game no more." Ron said. He told Tarique all about how he came into the money in the first place. Tarique knew that Ron was scandalous but he never had it anywhere in his mind that Ron would actually betray his own blood just to gain a few dollars. He had seen the news and heard that Mike was going to be covered on "America's Most Wanted" for the murders he was being charged with. Knowing what was going on in Mike's life with the courts, Tarique knew that Mike needed all the help he could get. This caused him to look at Ron even more skeptically. "If he'll play his own brother like that, I know he'll fuck me over in a hot second," Tarique thought to himself.
After they smoked the last of the ganja spliff they had fired up, they left the apartment. Tarique got into his car and left towards inner city Brooklyn. Ron slid into his new BMW and drove out towards the expressway where he headed down to Atlanta to start his new life. Rhonda was expecting him home the next day.

CHAPTER TWENTY-FOUR
MIKE IS SENTENCED IN NEW JERSEY

Mike and his mother sat on the bench in the front of the courtroom with a public defender. They were listening to the Judge as he proceeded in sentencing Mike.
"Mr. McCormick," the Judge bellowed, "This Court sentences you to twenty-five years to Life for your crimes here in New Jersey. This sentence will run concurrently with the sentence you receive in New York City for the homicides you were charged with there."
Mike remained silent. He had agreed to make a guilty plea in an attempt to avoid any harsher punishment later when or if any of his other crimes would be brought to light. He figured that if he pled out to the charges he had now, the District Attorney would be satisfied that he was off the streets and would drop any future investigations against him. He planned to make enough money on his own to get a lawyer to appeal his case since he didn't waive his appeal rights. Things didn't look good, but mentally Mike remained in control. His mother told him that as long as he knew what he was doing, everything would work out right. She would be there for him regardless. She had told him that she was just glad that he was alive and that she would do everything she could to help him with his case.
Now she sat there on the bench and put her head in her hands and began to cry. Her youngest son was being escorted out of the courtroom in chains like an animal headed to the slaughterhouse.
Mike looked back to his mother and mouthed the words, "I love you," through his tears. Trina whispered, "I love you too, baby."

CHAPTER TWENTY-FIVE
MIKE ENTERS YARDSVILLE YOUTH RECEPTION CENTER/ THE OFFICIAL BRAKE DOWN OF THE AMERICAN DREAM

The next morning,  Mike was on his way to Yardville Reception Center down the turnpike in New Jersey, sitting on the bus in handcuffs and shackles. He was sitting beside a muscular bald headed black man who had an old scar going down the side of his face. Mike wasn't in the mood to talk so he turned his attention to the window to observe the scenery around him.
He remembered that he hadn't gotten the chance to call Samone and he made a mental note to do so as soon as he got situated in the prison where he was now en route.
The gray prison bus pulled up to a big set of double doors attached to a huge cement wall that was connected at all four corners. As the doors opened and the bus rolled slowly to the drop-off area, Mike noticed that there were four gun towers around the prison yard. All of the towers were occupied with white men carrying high-powered rifles.
When Mike and the rest of the prisoners were led off of the bus, they were taken to the intake area of the prison. They were then unchained and ordered to strip all of their clothing off. All of them received prison clothes and were assigned to the units that they would be living in.
The whole ordeal made Mike think of a movie he had watched with Samone. It was called "Roots".
He looked at all of the other prisoners around him and wasn't very surprised to see that most of them were black. He guessed that most of them were more than likely in prison for the same reason as him, stepping outside of the boundaries of society's laws to acquire cash money, the "American Dream." Financial comfort, respect, and clout.
The so-called American Dream had been constantly flashed before their faces, and once they chose to veer down the path of crime to achieve everything they saw in all of the advertisements spread throughout their neighborhoods; obstacles would pop up to either slow them down, or shut them down.
Mike and his fellow prisoners, who looked like older versions of him, were all products of their environment. This environment is known as the ghetto. They had adopted a life of hustling not because they were "bad" people who sought out trouble, but because hustling was all they knew how to do in order to get by.
People from the ghetto go through harsh and negative experiences in areas of life that people who are well off take for granted. It was nothing big to walk through the ghetto and see a small child playing in a dirt yard wearing nothing but the same soiled pamper that he had on the day before. Families often went through times where their lights, water, gas, or electricity would be turned off by the companies because all of the bills couldn't be paid at once. Father figures were scarce, and many of the children were left to fend for themselves for the most part. The ghetto was filled with people whose level of survival depended solely on what they had the heart and ability to take from others. Everybody wanted a way out. And every time they stepped outside of their homes and saw the many billboard advertisements displaying cars, homes, and family experiences that they would likely never have, their way out seemed more and more justified. Every time a television set was turned on they would see a commercial that made them feel that their way out was absolutely worth the risk. Every time the hustlers who had made their way out of the ghetto returned to the ghetto to pay homage, those who remained struggling in the ghetto saw how they themselves could be living if they could only find their way out. This encouraged them even more to fight their way out of the slums by any means available, despite the legality.
While the more fortunate citizens of society lived lavishly in their suburban neighborhoods, they continuously slapped the ghetto dwellers in the face by advertising all of the finer things, knowing the ghetto dwellers couldn't afford these fine things.
Society always told the poor that an honest living is the best living. But as the poor learned from years of experience, "honest living" among their kind commonly consisted of growing old washing cars around their ghettos, flipping hamburger meat at McDonald's, working at a gas station, or being a janitor somewhere. Even though there were those few who made it out of the ghetto with "honest work," these exceptions were very rare.
Stress and desperation had continuously been loaded on the shoulders of the ghetto's occupants and then suddenly, out of nowhere, came drugs that could take away the stress if only for a little while.
Those in the ghetto who sold these drugs that were manufactured by society were then punished with imprisonment by that same "society." Coincidentally, the punishment for dealing drugs could be more sever than any punishment for any other crime short of capitol murder. None of this mattered to most of the poor because in their filthy world of heartache, survival itself was all that counted.
High-class, beautiful women, expensive jewelry, cars, and clothes were constantly broadcasted as status symbols, and drugs made the acquiring of these status symbols easier than any other means available to the average person in the ghetto. So they sold drugs to other people in the ghetto. Those ghetto people died from the drugs they bought from people in the ghetto. And those people in the ghetto were punished more harshly than anyone else who committed drug offenses.
So if these drugs had been intentionally placed in the ghetto for the purpose of destroying its inhabitants, whoever devised this plan was definitely getting what they planned for. Poor people dead and poor people imprisoned. Yes, the plan is working beautifully. The temptation began at young ages in the ghetto, which is why it wasn't uncommon to see a twelve-year-old boy from the ghetto on a bicycle with a pocket full of drugs. They all wanted what Mike had wanted. They wanted all of the things the older hustlers before them had obtained –fast wealth and a ticket to a better way of life.
But everything must go as planned, which is why most of these hustlers either found themselves dead or in Mike's current position, trapped behind the walls of one of the many hells commonly known as prison.
Those who were lucky enough to keep their lives were shipped off to penitentiaries to do physical labor as so many that were brought to America were meant to do in the first place. They were chained up and showcased so that the bidding could begin for their lives and liberty. Then society continued to flash and advertise the "good life" to the poor youngsters who were next in line to fall into the trap.
Fashion trends became more expensive on a regular basis. The greatest amount of posters and billboards showcasing the items of these trends were posted up in the ghetto. And the drugs were plentiful. So the traps stayed full.
Things still remain the same. When a person from the ghetto goes to trial he is typically found guilty by a jury of "his peers" in a court of law. The odd thing about the jury of his peers is the fact that none of them are actually his peers at all. He is lucky if even one person on the jury is black, let alone from an environment such as his.
This so-called fair trial is proven a joke when you tell a young black man from the ghetto that he is being judged by a jury of his peers only to look in the jury box and see that everyone inside of it is over fifty years old, from higher tax brackets, and predominantly white... very, very white... old and white... and angry and white.
His case is over before it even begins.
Once prisoners are sentenced, their entire file is sent to the Department of Corrections so that they can see who has what skill or attitude, which helps them in deciding where they should send individuals in the prison system. They observe the most aggressive prisoners and send them to maximum-security prisons in an attempt to let the prisoners know that they will have hell to pay if they try to control or overrun their precious prison system. Mike was aware of the rules in the game of hustling before he had taken it upon himself to play it. He chose his path and now he was receiving his consequences. His punishment wasn't a surprise to him. The only thing that surprised him was the fact that even with the trap known, the poor couldn't turn it around and make it backfire on the criminals that set it. Modern day slavery is in full operation. It didn't begin with Mike and it wouldn't end with Mike. Survival was the key, and survival was the only thing on Mike's mind as he walked down the hallway of his new plantation. Or should I use the nicer term, prison.

CHAPTER TWENTY-SIX
MIKE'S BIG PRISON SHOWER INCIDENT/ MIKE MEETS OMAR THE MULSLEM JAILHOUSE LAWYER/ MIKES GIVING A NEW NAME BY OMAR

The first few months of Mike's prison sentence went by smoothly. The prisoners who kept abreast of the large prison compound's gossip grapevine knew what Mike was about before he had arrived. They all expected him to be a loud oversized teenager looking for trouble but they were surprised when they saw the way he carried himself. They admired the way he walked with his head up and stayed to himself, minding his own business. He was a hustler and he spent each day and night plotting on ways to grab the next dollar. Most of the prisoners tried to take Mike under their wings but he trusted nobody so he never attached himself to any group, gang, or organization on the compound.
But with prison comes drama–it's unavoidable. Even though Mike was a hard teenager who could hold his own ground, some prisoners couldn't see past the fact that he was just a teenager. Those were the prisoners who had spent nearly half of their lives barely surviving in the prison system. They somehow felt degraded by the prison administration that had placed a child in their world as if they were children themselves.
Mike noticed their stares, whispers, and snickers when he was around some of them, but he never acknowledged those few prisoners.
One day while Mike was sitting at a table playing spades with three 'old-heads' he noticed a huge white prisoner with long hair and tattoos all over his arms, staring at him. He ignored the "big cracker" and continued his card game. When he was ready to leave he told his older playing mates that he was going to take his shower and lay back in his cell for the rest of the night. One of the elders who had noticed the white prisoner eyeballing Mike advised Mike to take his chair to the shower with him to place his cosmetics on. Mike didn't take his advice. He went into the shower and stripped down after he laid his cosmetics on the ledge of the window so that the Correctional Officers on duty could glance into the shower and make sure that no inmates were getting beaten, stabbed, or raped. On this night the C.O. was busy talking on the telephone and paid no attention to the shower room.
When Mike turned on the lukewarm water he saw the same white man walk into the shower room who had been staring at him earlier. Mike just proceeded to take his shower paying no mind to the white man showering three stalls down from his own. When Mike was ready to wash his face he turned back towards the window to retrieve his bar of Neutrogena face soap. That's when he noticed that his chair was there in the shower and his cosmetics were on top of it. Looking out the window he could see the old-head that had told him to take the chair with him, walking away from the shower. He had placed Mike's chair in the shower room for him and as Mike saw the big white man sneaking peeks at his body, he understood what the situation was all about. He grew tense and decided to see if his bad feelings were warranted. He reached down on the steel-folding chair and picked up his Pert Plus shampoo. He lathered his hands with it and placed it in the back of his head and leaned under the streaming water, pretending to make a chore out of washing his hair. He closed his eyes down to thin slits and glanced over at the white prisoner. The white man was looking around as if to make sure that no one was watching. He thought Mike's eyes were closed. He walked over to the second stall away from Mike. As he got closer Mike moved in a flash to the window where his chair was. Mike grabbed the steel chair and swung it wildly as the big white man moved in on him. Mike missed the first swing, but the backswing smashed against the predator's left temple and cut him down to his skull. There was a loud scream and blood poured from the side of the prisoner’s head as Mike continued to hit him with the steel chair.
Mike had felt a hard object hit him on the back of the head, and he fell to the floor. Looking up he saw three C.O.s standing over him. He tried to stand back up and was hit again. He fell back to the floor and passed out.
When he had regained consciousness he was in the solitary confinement unit of the prison, known as “the hole”.
"Hey Youngblood," came a voice from the next cell over.
"We heard about what you did in the unit to that crazy white cat who tried to rape you. It's about time somebody split him open.
I want to show you somethin', son. Here, check this out, and I'll talk to you tomorrow." Then the prisoner next door to Mike stuck his hand through the bars. He was holding a thick dark green book. Mike reached through the bars of his own cell and grabbed the book.
"Yeah, thanks," Mike said.
"Just check it out, son, it can help you find yourself. By the way, I'm Omar, everybody knows me around here. I'm the best lawyer on the yard, but we'll talk about all of that in the morning, alright, Mike?"
"Yeah, but how do you know my name?" "I read the newspapers everyday, son. I'll talk to you in the morning." "A'ight." Mike responded coolly. When Mike went to his bunk, boredom caused him to open the book that Omar had given him. It was the Holy Qu'ran. Mike began to read it and found himself totally interested in what he was reading.
Mike woke up late the next morning and began to read more of the Quran. He wanted to ask Omar some questions about what he had already read, but he didn't want to wake him up so he sat back and read more. While he read he was startled by the sound of a foreign song coming from Omar's cell. Mike moved over to the bars and sat down on the concrete floor to listen to what Omar was singing even though he didn't understand a word of it. When Omar stopped chanting he began to speak the same language he had been singing. Mike was curious as hell but remained quiet and just listened until Omar finished, and then he went back to his bunk because the C.O.s were coming to pass out chow. When they left Mike went back to the bars.
"Yo, Omar, you up over there?” he called out.
"Yeah, Youngblood. I'm up. How you keeping' up over there?"
"I'm cool, you know I was reading the book you gave me and I like it. I want to ask you some stuff about it."
"So you dig it, huh?" Omar asked, obviously pleased that the Quran had struck a chord with the youngster. "Yeah." "That's good to hear. Man, you need that book there. I'll answer any questions the best I can, later but right now I got somethin' else I want to rap with you about."
"What's that?" Mike asked curiously.
"It's about you bein' here. I can help you get off of that twenty-five to life sentence but you have to trust me."
"No offense to you, Omar, but man I don't trust nobody. Nobody at all."
Omar let out a laugh that could be heard all over the solitary confinement unit. "I like you, son. You remind me of myself twenty years ago when I was about twenty-two. You ain't like any thirteen year old I ever seen before. I know you, son, I've been down the road you're travelin' and I want to help you get back out there. This ain't no place for no teenager or nobody period, for that matter."
"And just how do you think you can help me out if you can't help yourself get out of here?"
"That's just it, son." Omar said with a chuckle. When I came in I had three life sentences and forty years on top of that.
But I kept my mind on them books in the law library and fought my case myself. I worked my time down to eighteen years and I got twelve in on that so I think it's pretty safe to say that I did help myself just a bit. Hell, I worked twenty-three other convicts out of this system, these white folks don't like me here. They say I'm defeating their purpose, but hell, I didn't write the law.
What's right is right and what's wrong is the fact that they got you sittin' up here in Yardsville Penitentiary instead of a juvenile camp."
Mike was interested now. "Yo, you tellin' me I ain't even supposed to be here?"
"Hell no, you ain't got no business up in here! They got so many laws against that, it's pathetic. I'm tellin' you, son, they know what they're doin'. They figure that you ain't nothin' but a kid so you won't think to double check their bullshit."
"So I really can get back out of here?" Mike asked, wanting Omar to expound on what he was saying.
"Yeah, you will get out of here, all you gotta do is put a little bit of trust in me, and my work will speak for itself. You got arrested when you were only twelve years old and when you turned thirteen they sentenced you as an adult. That's an illegal sentence because you are on docket for committing the murders when you were twelve so they were supposed to sentence you as if you were twelve when you went to court. When you do the shit they got you in for at the age of thirteen, then they can legally sentence you as an adult, but that's the limit. You can assassinate the Pope at age twelve and they can't do shit but send you to a juvenile camp until you turn eighteen. I know my shit and I know that I can have you out of here, son. All you gotta do is say the word and I'll do what I'm known around here for doing."
Mike spent a few seconds thinking it over and realized that he had nothing to lose.
"A'ight," he said to Omar. "I want you to do my case and try to get me out of here."
They were both let out of the hole on the same day and they picked up where they had left off. Omar had answered all of Mike's questions about the Holy Quran that he could and told him all about himself and how the Qu'ran changed his entire life. He now lived and loved Islam. One night while they were still in the hole they stayed up all night long talking. Omar had told Mike that his name didn't fit him and that he had a good one for him. When Mike laughed and asked Omar what he had in mind, Omar told him "Wahid."
"What does that mean?" Mike asked.
"It means Unique," Omar replied. "So whenever somebody calls Wahid, you'll know that they are telling you that you are one of a kind. Really, we can just get right to the point and call you Unique. How's that?"
Mike laughed more. "Yeah, man, I guess that's pretty cool." At that moment Mike became Unique and walked proudly understanding its meaning.
Unique spent most of his time talking with Omar, reading different books, and talking on the phone with his mother, Samone, and Kenny. He had heard nothing at all from the nurse or the cab driver he had hooked up with and looked out for. He also heard nothing from Ron. His mother was the number one person in his corner and she accepted every call he made to her down in Florida. And from a distance, she had been a good grandmother to his son with Samone; they named him Maleek. The son he had with Kim had been named Raheem. Trina would call Kim's mother's home to check on Unique's son, only to be cursed out and hung up on repeatedly.
Omar had put together Unique's appeal stating all of the laws against his sentence along with a lawsuit against the Department of Corrections for allowing the sentence to occur. In order to avoid the multi-million dollar lawsuit against them, the Department of Corrections had agreed to lower Unique's twenty-five to life sentence to the appropriate sentence of a juvenile, but they made stipulations in the agreement to keep Unique in Yardsville for the rest of his time. He would be released at the age of eighteen.
The Department of Corrections had hoped Unique would get himself into more trouble while he was in Yardsville so that they could keep him there forever, but once he won his appeal, Unique laid low and concentrated on what he would do once he was let out. He continued to keep Omar as his company on the compound and showed his thanks by doing the right thing for the rest of his time. They read books, played chess and had many discussions about life in general.
Time went by and Unique was now seventeen years old. He would be eighteen next week.
Unique laid back on his bunk thinking about the years that had passed him by and the instances that had occurred along the way. He was only thirteen when he had come to Yardsville, now he was eighteen years old and close to being released. He was satisfied knowing that he had used his time to his advantage. He was mentally together and physically healthy. He also was still a hustler.
While in prison he had suffered his losses like a man and planned to bounce back instead of using his losses as an excuse to fall into a psychological slump. His own brother had played him and left him with nothing. Kim had left him and began to hate him when she found out that he had a baby with Samone. Unique still remained in control. When he won his case he informed his mother that no one was to be told, so all of those who knew Unique from the beginning continued to think that he would be in prison forever.
That night he called Samone's house to let her know that he would be home within a week. The phone was answered by her little brother, John. "Hello?" he said. "Who is it?"
Unique didn't recognize John's voice right off. "This Unique, who is this?" he asked.
"This is John, man. What's up?" John answered laughing happily. "Samone is downstairs, I'll go get her in a minute."
Mike hadn't talked to John in a while and he was glad to hear him sounding happy that he called.
"What's been happening John, damn man, it’s been a while since we hooked up. Is everything cool out there?"
"Yeah, it's alright out here." "You know I'll be out next week," Unique said. John got silent and waited for Unique to speak again. "I'll be home this Friday." "Oh yeah?" John asked. "Yeah," Unique replied. "Damn, man," John said. "I got somethin' to tell you." "What's up?" "You know you always been like a brother to me and Samone is my sister but man I don't want you to trip when you come home and see what's goin’ on. Samone got another man and they been living’ here for a minute."
"What do you mean a minute?"
"Hold on, here comes Samone, she'll tell you about everything." There was the rattling sound of the phone being transported from one hand to another.
"Hello?" Samone said into the phone. "Unique?"
"Yeah, it's me," Unique said coldly. "I know you wasn't expecting' my next call to be this early, but I wanted to let you know that I'll be home Friday, but I see we have some talkin' to do."
"Oh baby, for real?" Samone said, nearly dropping the telephone. "What do you mean we have to talk?"
"Come on now, Samone, I thought you were keepin' it real wit' me. Don't act like you don't know what's up all of a sudden. Tell me about your man."
"Oh shit, baby, look. I wasn't tryin'a keep that from you or nothin' like that. You have to understand that you had a long time to do and I couldn't wait that long. I stayed by your side even though I have Billy here with me. I had to protect myself from being hurt knowin' that you may never be comin' home. I just want you to understand that I love you still but I'm not in love with you anymore, Unique."
"And just what the fuck does that mean?" Unique asked with ice and sarcasm in his tone.
"Unique, don't make this harder for me than it already is. Baby, I still got your back with' anything you're tryin'a do because I want our son to know that both of his parents get along well."
"Well, I'll let you know now that I'm still on my ass in love with you and when I come home I'll give you two months to decide who you want to be with. Either way there won’t be any hard feelings, Samone, a'ight?"
"Yeah, Unique, I hear you, baby." "I'ight then, Samone, I'll see you when I see you. I gotta go now."
CHAPTER TWENTY-SEVEN
MIKE IS RELEASED FROM PRISON AND IS PICKED UP BY CHAUNCEY AND KENNY

That Friday morning Unique was escorted to the intake department to be processed out of the prison. He had given the majority of his belongings to Omar so when he was being let out all he had with him was a small canvas bag. He was given three hundred and sixty dollars and was told that he was free to go. Unique exited the prison and stepped out into the free world. He deeply inhaled the fresh air letting it flush out the stale wind from his lungs. It didn't really register with him that he was actually free until he saw Chauncey and Kenny standing in the parking lot beside Kenny's truck. Kenny smiled big and embraced his brother tightly.
"Damn, man, it's good to have you back, Unique. We got to get you some food man, you're thin as hell. But we got you with us again. Welcome back, bro," Kenny said happily.
"Thanks, man. It's just good to be back out here. I still can't believe this hell is over," Unique said letting go of Kenny.
Chauncey looked Unique in his eyes as he walked over to shake his hand. "Are you sure it's over?" he asked his youngest son with genuine concern. Unique thought about the question for a brief moment and found that he had no answer.
"We'll see," Unique said, accepting his father's handshake.
"What are you planning to do with yourself?" Chauncey asked as all three men got into Kenny's Ford Explorer.
"Right now I'm just gon’ lay back and see where I should be. I got a few things to take care of before I can honestly say what I'll be doin' in the future."
"Well, you're a man now and I trust that your experience has changed some things. They call you Unique now and with a name like that comes certain actions. I hope you'll do the right thing out here."
"Yeah," Unique said from the backseat. "I'll be aight."
After a few minutes of silence Unique spoke again, "Yo, Kenny, you can drop me off at the bus station? I can get to New York City from there."
"Don't you want to go check out grandma?" Kenny asked.
"Grandma knew where I was for five whole years and she never came to check me out. She'll see me when I'm ready to see her."
The remainder of the trip to the Greyhound station was quiet. When the truck pulled in front of the station, Kenny stopped to let Unique out. Unique went into the station and caught the next bus heading to New York City.

CHAPTER TWENTY-EIGHT
UNIQUE MAKES HIS WAY TO RONS MOTHER-IN-LAW HOUSE TO CONFRONT RON

Unique made his first stop in the projects to buy a gun. When he got there he noticed how much things had changed while he was incarcerated. The community had changed a lot, and all for the worse. Unique also noticed that some of the people who were strung-out on drugs when he was out before were now cleaned up and making money. He saw that some of those who were once on top of the world, having money, cars, homes, respect, and everything else on the list of the finer things in life now were smoked-out and strung-out on drugs.
When he went inside of the apartment where he used to buy guns for quick jobs, everybody embraced him. They were surprised that he was out. Some thought that he had escaped from Yardsvillle penitentiary until he told everybody about how he had won his case in the appeals court in New Jersey.
After he copped a gun, Unique called a cab and left the projects. He had a hunch and decided to play on it. He knew that Rhonda loved her mother so he thought that if he went to her home, he would be able to get the address to where Ron and Rhonda lived down in Atlanta.
When he got to Ms. Arshema Simmon's home he found that he had made out better than he expected when Ms. Simmons opened the door and saw Unique her mouth dropped open in shock.
"Hey, baby!" she said, hugging him. "When did you come home?"
"I just got out today Ms. Simmons. How are you?" Unique said, stepping into the front hallway. "I'm fine, Mike. Welcome home," she said, walking him to the guest closet to hang his jacket.
