“Not much, Sandra.” Dee-Dee responded. “I’m having a good time.”
Sandra looked at Unique noticing he and Dee-Dee were dressed alike. “So girl,” she said to her friend. “Who is this?”
“Oh, this is Unique, we just met.” Dee-Dee responded. Then she turned to Unique and said, “This is my friend Sandra.”
“Nice to meet you,” he said acknowledging Sandra.
“You too,” Sandra responded. “Well, girl, let me get back over there with Felix before I have to kick some bitch’s ass t’night, you know they’re on him. I just wanted to come over and holler at you and see what’s up.”
“Okay, girl,” Dee-Dee said. Then Sandra moved closer to her to whisper in her ear. “He’s a keeper, girl, but tone that smile down a bit before you break your fuckin’ face.” “Whatever,” Dee-Dee said, waving Sandra off while her smile spread further across her pretty face.
“Bye, Unique.” Sandra waved, making her way back the way she had come.
Eric B. & Rakim continued to perform, rapping, “Microphone Fiend,” “I Ain’t No Joke,” and “Paid In Full,” among other popular hits they were quickly becoming famous for.
After the show, Unique escorted Dee-Dee from the building, steering her away from a section close to an exit where a fight was breaking out between two small groups of guys. He drove up 8th Avenue (Frederick Douglas Boulevard) and made a left onto 145th Street. Unique and Dee-Dee were both feeling good, her because she was having such a good time with her new friend and him because he had new business to look forward to the next afternoon. Unique sped up the steep streets headed back to Dee-Dee’s building. He pulled up and parked.
“You have a good time?” he asked her. “Yeah, I had a good time, Unique. Thanks for taking me out.” Dee-Dee answered. “When will I see you again?”
“Here’s my beeper number,” he said while writing on a jagged piece of paper. “Hit me whenever.” She wanted to ask him why he was leaving so early because it was their first date, but she decided against it. She noticed that Unique kept looking at his watch. “He probably has a girl he got to get home to,” she thought to herself. “Oh well.”
She reached over and kissed him on the cheek and let herself out of the car. “Thanks again.” She said before heading up to her building hoping that she would see Unique again soon. She thought he was strange, but a good strange. She planned on calling him later. “I like him,” she said to herself while entering her building.

Chapter thirty five
UNIQUE TAKES SIMONE & HER NEW MAN BILLE TO THE S&S AFTER HOUR SPOT

Unique pulled up in front of Samone’s mother’s house at exactly 10:35 p.m., Samone was standing out front with Billie.
They both walked over to the car and got inside. Billie sat in the front passenger seat and Samone, who was slightly uncomfortable with the night’s arrangement, sat in the back seat.
“What’s up, y’all?” Unique asked his passengers with a sly smile that neither of them noticed.
“Not too much, homey,” Billie answered excitedly. Samone followed up, “We’re doing okay, Unique, how are you?”
“I’m cool,” he said. “So, where are we headed?”
Billie spoke up. “Let’s go to the S&S after hours spot.”
“That’s a bet.” Unique said while pulling away from the curb.
His radio was playing the newest mixtape from a popular DJ named Kid Capri. On the way to their destination Unique opened up the conversation with Billie. They talked about everything from the goings-on of New York, to sports, and all things in-between. In the back seat Samone was trying hard to hear over the music to listen to what and who the men were talking about. She wanted to figure out Unique’s intentions. She wondered what he was up to taking her and the older man who took her from him out for a night on the town. She hoped nothing crazy would come about. She had told Billie about her not really wanting to go out with Unique like this, but Billie being Billie told her she had nothing to worry about. He couldn’t fathom the idea of an eighteen year old who had been in jail since he was thirteen causing him any problems. Samone was still uncomfortable. Billie didn’t know what type of person Unique was and she hoped he wouldn’t learn the hard way.
Entering the S&S Club with Samone and Billie in trail, Unique made his way to his usual section in the club. Samone and Billie looked on as he was being greeted by name by everyone from the men guarding the doors to several prominent partygoers. Most were old partners he had dealt with in the days before his incarceration. When the club’s owner heard that Unique was in the building he went over to his section, greeted him and handed over a chilled bottle of champagne –on the house, of course. Getting comfortable in his booth with his two guests, Unique was busy giving nod greetings and waves to several people in the club who began looking in his direction when DJ Starchild (RIP) gave him a shout-out through the huge club speakers.
Billie was impressed but began to feel a bit insecure and inferior in Unique’s presence. He glanced over at Samone who was sitting beside him. He wanted to see her reaction to the treatment their host was receiving. Her expression was passive being that she wasn’t brand new to seeing Unique receive this kind of attention in his area or any other area where he was known.
“Damn,” Billie thought to himself. “This nigga only been out a few months and he got juice.”
Samone wondered how Unique was going strong since she knew that Ron had ran off with everything Unique had before he went to jail. She hoped for their son’s sake that Unique didn’t go back to robbing coke spots, because Maleek loved Unique and she knew that he would be crushed if anything ever happened to him.
Vibing off of the club's music and atmosphere, Unique enjoyed himself as he planned to get to know more about the man Samone had living around his son. “You want some coke?” Unique asked Billie in a tone low enough for only Billie to hear.
“What?” Billie asked, even though he had heard the question clearly.
“I said, do you want some coke?” Unique repeated.
Billie used coke periodically whenever he had enough money to get it without taking from Samone. She hated it and let him know it every time she caught him sniffing. “What you got?” He asked Unique.
Unique glimpsed over at Samone who was staring at him. Then he pulled out a hundred-dollar bill, filled  with glistening, flakey cocaine so she could see it, and handed it to Billie who took it and began immediately to sniff as much of the powder as he could.
Samone watched the scene and held back her anger the best way she could.
Billie offered the bill back to Unique with some coke still in it.
“Nah, man,” Unique said, waving Billie off with a smile. “I don’t sniff that shit.” “All of this is for me?” Billie responded simultaneously, surprised and pleased.
Moments passed as the three sat in the booth. Unique was chilling out. Billie was high as a kite in Chicago. Samone was infuriated and ready to leave.
“Come on, y’all, let’s go to the back,” Unique said, getting up out of his seat. The back of the club is where the hustlers gathered to gamble. Samone didn’t want to say a word in protest even though she was heated. She got up and reluctantly followed the two men to the back room.
Billie and Samone followed closely as Unique made his way over to the cee-low table.
“You know somethin’ about this here?” Unique asked Billie.
“Nah, homey,” Billie responded, obviously stoned. “Seen dudes playin’ but I never played myself. I don’t even understand that shit.”
“I’ll explain it,” Unique said. Then he began to explain the rules of the game.
“You roll three dice. When two of the dice show the same number, the number on the remaining dice is your point. The next person rolling has to try to roll a higher number on his odd dice. If the number on his odd dice is equal to yours, nobody wins. If it’s higher he wins. If it’s lower, he loses. Whoever rolls the dice and the numbers come out as four, five and six automatically wins. That roll is cee-low, the name of the game. Six on the odd dice is also an automatic win because you can’t get any higher.
After explaining those and the rest of the rules to Billie, Unique gave him a one hundred dollar bill to gamble with. He lost it immediately. Then Unique got into the game and shot for himself and his hand was hot. He got into the game with five grand and won an additional ten grand. After giving twenty-five hundred back in bad rolls he quit and bought everybody at the cee-low table drinks. After picking up his winnings he walked out of the club with his guest in tow.
Samone wanted to go home, so Unique made his way up 8th Avenue, jumped on the Harlem River Drive and headed towards the George Washington Bridge en route to New Jersey.
When they got back to Samone’s mother’s home, Unique pulled into the driveway. Before getting out of the car Billie asked Unique for more coke.
“All I got is ten bucks.” Billie said and pulled it out of his pocket.
Unique looked into the rearview mirror at Samone who was in the back seat steaming with anger at Billie.
To Billie he said. “Give me another bill.”
Billie handed him a single and the ten-dollar bill. Unique pocketed the ten spot and poured twenty dollars’ worth of coke into the single, and then he handed the impregnated bill back to Billie.
“Damn!” Billie exclaimed loudly, looking at his glistening gift. “If I give you a twenty will you double that?”
“For you, I think I’ll do that homey,” Unique replied slyly. Billie turned to Samone and asked her for twenty dollars.
“For that shit?” she asked, furious at her man. “You got that shit backwards Billie for real. I don’t know what your problem is tonight.” Samone protested. She gave him the money anyway.
After Billie and Unique made another exchange, Unique gave the twenty dollars back to Samone and all the money he had won gambling at the club.
Holding the money Samone boiled over with rage at the situation.
“Fuck this bullshit!” she snapped and threw the money hard towards the front window. It hit the windshield and then scattered all over the front of the car. Unique could hardly hold in his laughter.
Billie began scrambling to pick up all the money. That’s when Unique yanked out a gun and aimed it at Billie’s head. Billie’s eyes bulged and he began to sweat and shiver.
“Nigga, don’t make me kill your ass in here tonight,” Unique growled. “Put that shit down, and get the fuck outta here. Now!”
Billie followed the orders and got out of the car. Samone was so upset that she couldn’t move. She just sat there in the back seat staring at Unique with angry tears running down her face.
“So,” Unique said to her after putting his gun back under his seat. “This is what you left me for? An old ass cokehead who’s broke as hell, livin’ wit’cha momma, and old enough to be your father?” He shook his head and laughed arrogantly.
Samone didn’t respond. Instead she hung her head in shame and let herself out of the car.
Listening to Unique’s laughter grow faint as he drove away, she made her way up to the doorway of her mother’s home to be in the company of her cokehead who was actually old enough to be her father, and who had made a fool of himself and her in the worst way. A lot of her love for Billie withered away that night.

Chapter thirty six
UNIQUE PUTS DOWN THE TAKE OVER PLAN

At exactly twelve o’clock the next day Unique was sitting in Tony’s coke spot waiting to hear the “good news” that Tony had spoken of the day before.
“Demilo, mi amigo,” Tony said entering the front room to greet Unique.
“Yo, what’s up, homey?”
“I hope I didn’t keep you waiting for too long.”
“Nah,” Unique responded. “I was just sittin’ here thinkin’.”
“Well, let me fill you in on why you’re here. As you already know, everybody down the hill comes up the hill to cop, then all the runners take them to a number of different spots up here to cop and make their pickups. It’s been that way for years now. Down the hill you have the blacks in Harlem while you got the Dominicans up here in Washington Heights.
“The blacks live in a world of their own just as we Dominicans up the hill live in our own world. Dominicans can’t go and haven’t been down the hill to hustle, but the blacks are free to come up the hill to cop their weight. Are you following me?”
Unique had been listening to every word to catch Tony’s angle. He wanted to hear the rest since he knew there was more. “Yeah, I’m hearin’ you,” He answered.
“Okay, my friend,” Tony said. “That’s the reason you’re here today. I want to set up shop down the hill so that it will be easier for the blacks to cop my weight down there without having to come all the way up the hill and spread all this money and information around to all these bosses up here.
“That will also keep the heat down and lessen all of our chances of being fucked with by the feds. This is where you come in.
“I want you to move my weight directly to the brothers in Harlem to cut shit short and keep the money from going through so many hands. You will control the drugs down the hill while I supply you the weight from up here. I see how you move and I know there is nobody down the hill better for this job than you, which is why I will be supplying you all the weight you need on consignment.”
Inside Unique was amped but he knew that a businessman never puts his enthusiasm on display, so he kept his poker face on and began to speak.
“I think that’s a good idea and I know it’ll work, but you know, you are talkin’ about a shit load of fuckin’ kilos. The block I’m hustlin’ on alone has four buildings moving the better part of three keys a day, that’s each building. That means they have to supply at least twelve kilos a day just to my block alone. Then you have all the coke spots from 110th Street all the way up to 155th Street on 8th Ave. That’s over a hundred coke spots Tony, so that’s a lot of kilos to supply. What you’re describing is a power-move that has endless possibilities. I’m definitely up for it as long as we can keep it poppin’ and supply the demand for the weight.”
“Don’t worry about that part of it, Unique. I guarantee that everything you need to move, you will have. I will be seeing to it.”
“There’s one thing I want to know. Unique stated, leaning closer to Tony who was sitting at the opposite end of the table. “I can tell you put a lot of thought into this plan, so tell me, why did you choose me to be your right hand in this move?”
“Well,” Tony said, “I knew you would ask that and it’s simple and plain, you got this shit in your blood, homey. You are a true hustler so you know the game. You play by your own rules and kill only when need be. You were holding your shit down tight at thirteen and now that you’re back on your feet a little, I know it’ll only be a matter of time before you become a boss, around here, and I have the best plan for making it happen so that your becoming a boss in this game won’t have a bad effect on my moves and at the same time I’m giving you an opportunity that you won’t have by just being a boss. Being a boss in this game is now only as big as its title since this crack is so easy for just anybody to cop if they got the money. Together, me and you can lock down a major part of this game and make everybody happy at the same time.”
Unique smiled now, seeing the reality in Tony’s words. Unique knew that the position offered to him was better than any position he would be in no matter how far he blew up on his own. Tony had the connection to get the weight they would need to make this plan happen. “A'ight, I’m wit’ that!” Unique stated confidently.
 “But let’s take it slow and crawl through this thing. I want to start with my block first, so that we can get the feel of this shit. That should be fifty-keys I will need to start, which should hold down my block for a few days, probably almost a week. I got my own building and it’s covered tight so I got plenty of space to store the weight down there.
“Now let’s talk about how much you will be charging me for each kilo. You know the street price up the hill for a kilo is twelve grand. So I have to sell it for less than that down the hill so the peeps down there won’t want to come up the hill for a better price.”
“I feel you,” Tony said. “I’ll give each kilo to you for seven grand and you can sell them for whatever you want down there. How’s that?”
“I can work wit’ that,” Unique replied.
“When do you want to start?”
“Shit! Right now if you have no other plans,” Unique said energetically.
“Let’s roll.”
Tony led the way to the back room and pointed to the sidewall that had sixty neatly wrapped packages, the size of cookie boxes, lined against it.
“Take all of those except ten. Come back tonight and I’ll have somebody here help you move ‘em down the hill.”
The two men shook hands to finalize their new power-move. Tony was loyal to the game and all of those on his team. He’d been hustling ever since he moved with his mother to America. Tony had seen the way Unique raised himself in the game when he was just a shorty and knew that even then Unique was loyal to those he dealt with. Tony knew about how Ron had crossed Unique years before and he was impressed when he learned that Unique hadn’t killed Ron as he had done many others for much less. To Tony not killing his older brother showed another level of character in Unique. Even though Ron stiffed him for everything, Unique wouldn’t murder his own blood. “I know. I made the right decision,” Tony thought to himself, lighting a Cuban cigar and heading to the back of the apartment to begin putting Unique’s packages aside for him to pick up later that evening. “It’s on now.” He said aloud, smiling. He was elated that he had brainstormed such a powerful move and gotten the best right-hand man in New York City as his partner.

Chapter thirty seven
UNIQUE CALLS A MEETING WITH ALL THE 8th AVENUE HUSTLERS/ UNIQUE MOVES HIS 1st, 50 KEYS

The following morning Unique called a meeting with the three other bosses on the block. The four men gathered in the park one street down from where their businesses were operating. Unique was dressed in army fatigue pants with a matching jacket. He wore beige Timberland boots firmly laced and a pair of designer glasses that were tinted just enough so that his eyes couldn’t be seen. His recently cut fade was covered with a black skullcap. Inside of his jacket he wore a shoulder gun holster that held his 9-millimeter pistol.
When all the men seated themselves at a picnic table in the park Unique could sense the tension in the men around him. He was also cautious himself. He placed both of his hands on the table and addressed the group. “I know we’ve had our differences over the past few weeks, battling for control of the block, but I have a proposal for future business that all of us will benefit from. That way, we can all have an understanding.”
Unique looked around the group to see how they were taking his words so far. All of their facial expressions were passive but curious. He proceeded.
“How much are y’all payin’ for kilos?”
“Thirteen grand,” they answered back in unison.
“Well,” Unique scanned and locked eyes with each of them before continuing,
“I will knock that price down to ten grand as long as we have an understanding amongst each other.” He looked at them all again. “Are y’all down with that?”
“Shit yeah, I’m down for it!” Dave answered. He had been in shootouts with Unique and hated the way Unique had stepped on his toes in the past, and stalled his business with his superior product. The others at the table had experienced similar issues with Unique. Now that Unique was making an offer to chop down the price of kilos for them, Dave had to accept. He was a hustler and the purpose of hustling was getting money. It was all about the money. The other men followed Dave with their own agreements to purchase kilos from Unique at ten grand apiece.
 “Is this ten grand for a kilo of powder or a kilo of rocks?” Ted asked. He was seated at Dave’s right, while Junior was on Dave’s other side.
“Powder,” Unique answered sharply.
“How much will you charge to rock it up the way you was rockin’ up yo shit?”
Unique thought the question over for, a very brief period before answering. “That will run you eleven grand a key if I have to cook it up.”
“It’s a deal.” Dave said, smiling and glancing at his Rolex watch.
“Same here,” Ted said.
“It’s a bet,” was Junior’s reply.
What Unique was promising them was at least a three thousand dollar discount on their packages, which he would cook into his “unique” brew of crack cocaine, rocked up the way he did his own. None of them had any problem accepting the business offer. When the men left the park, and in their minds, the past problems they had over control of the block were gone. The beef was done, it was time for steak.
Everything went smoothly with Unique’s first delivery, and after three days, what he hadn’t retailed himself of the fifty kilos he sold to Dave, Ted, and Junior. The block was jumping once again and all the bosses were getting their packages off without conflict because they all could see that there were more than enough customers for everybody. They pushed a lot of their work to the heads but they also sold some of their kilos to some of their homeboys on other blocks for a little less than they were already paying.
Unique pulled up to Tony’s building with a van he had rented and parked next to the curb. When he went upstairs, Tony let him into the apartment, greeting him with a smile.
“Is everything alright, my friend?” Tony asked.
“Just perfect.” Unique smiled. “Everything is sold and I got three hundred and fifty grand down in the van.”
Tony was impressed. “That’s good to hear, homey.”
“I’ma lea’da van wit’chu,” Unique said. “That way you can have somebody reload it wit’ fitty mo’ joints and roll it down to me.”
“I’ll tell you what,” Tony said, “I’ll have them load up one hundred for you instead of fifty; that way you can really have a week’s worth of work this time. You’re really puttin’ it together down there, and shit is lookin’ good.” He gave Unique a pound and continued to smile as Unique exited the apartment. Business was off to a good start.
Unique had a strong weekend and by that Sunday, he had sold the hundred bricks and took Tony seven hundred thousand in cash. The next day when Unique went to Tony’s spot, Tony complimented him on his firm and consistent ways of getting business handled. Then he informed Unique that after the work he had done so far, he should get a little rest.
“I’ll see you whenever you come back to see me.” Tony had said. “I know this shit is tiring. We’re ahead right now, so we both can afford a break.”
To Unique a rest sounded like a good idea. After all, he was tired as hell from all the work he had done moving about and cooking up all those kilos for his customers. When he left Tony’s spot he had plans of getting as much rest as he could and taking advantage of his break.
He and Dee-Dee had talked on the phone a few times starting the same night he had taken her out. Unique took her as a good girl just trying to get by. He wasn’t up for any serious relationship even though he enjoyed her conversation and company.
He debated briefly on whether or not he should hook back up with her while he was relaxing. He wasn’t sure.

Chapter thitry eight
UNIQUE’S DELEMA WITH CATS, RATS & DEATH/ UNIQUE COPS HIS FIRST BMW/ CONFRONTS DEE DEE’S BOYFRIEND

Unique woke up late that night. He was awakened from his rest by the noise of a rat in his building. The rat was vigorously eating the remains of the Chinese food Unique had left out before he went to sleep.
After Unique threw a shoe in the direction of the rat, it fled from the room.
The building had a lot of rats and Unique hated them. He also hated cats but had gone to a pet store months before and purchased a kitten. He kept a distance from the cat as it grew and the cat served its purpose as best as it could in keeping as many rats away as possible.
Unique had a hatred and somewhat fear of cats that carried on through the years beginning back in Jamaica when he was just a child.
_____________________________________________
At five years old, while living in Jamaica with his grandmother from his mother’s side, Unique had been extremely fond of their neighbor who was a very old man. Unique loved to visit the old man to hear stories of how Jamaica had been in the old days. The old man had no family and his only constant companion was his black cat, which he had owned and loved for many years. The cat’s name was Smokey. Smokey never made much noise when the old man was around, but at times, when the old man would leave the room, Smokey would look at Unique hissing and showing his teeth. One day Unique fled from the old man’s little house after Smokey had attacked and clawed him leaving bloody scratches on his neck and arms. Later that day, Unique went back to visit the old man only to be cut short by Smokey in his path looking ready to attack. Little Unique began to back up slowly ready to run. Then Smokey lunged towards him and jumped on him, clawing and making high-pitched noises from his throat. Unique yelled out in fear and tried to shake the cat off of him. When this didn’t work, Unique reached for Smokey’s tail. He yanked the cat off of him and swung it around by the tail and threw it forcefully into the big oak tree in the old man’s yard. Smokey hit the tree headfirst and fell to the ground dead with a bashed head and a broken neck. Seeing the dead cat enhanced Unique’s fear and he ran back home where he remained until the next day.
That next day, Unique went to the old man’s house to see that Smokey was gone. When he knocked on the old man’s door there was no answer but the door was unlocked so he went in the house. The old man appeared to be asleep, but Unique couldn’t wake him up even though he tried hard, so he went home and told his grandmother that the old man wouldn’t wake up. She went over to check it out and saw that the old man was dead. Walking around the bed she saw the old man’s dead cat lying on the floor. The old man had died in his sleep.
Unique had wanted to go to the funeral but his grandmother told him he should never go to any funerals at all so he stayed behind. All of the grownups who knew the old man knew how much he loved Smokey, so some of them said that losing the cat is what made the old man lose his will to live anymore, because the cat was all he had. Hearing things like this made Unique blame himself for the old man’s death, and caused him to have an undying fear of cats. He saw them as the devil roaming the earth in the form of flesh (covered in fur, of course).
_____________________________________________
Moving out of the building was heavy on Unique’s mind as he got into a cab and headed to 125th Street between Park Avenue and Madison Avenue to Dapper Dan’s. He had taken his car there to have the interior re-done along with other things. There was a custom made MCM cover on the tail of the car with a matching cover on the front grill. On the tail there were gold letters spelling “Peace God.” On the front grill, the same style of lettering spelled “Unique.” The interior of the car had been done in the same custom MCM leather as the grill and tail on the outside. There was also a ragtop on the car matching all the other leather. He had BBS rims on Perelli tires put on the car to replace the original wheels.
Taking the keys to his car, Unique paid for the work and received the leather outfit he had ordered to be made to match his car. All of it cost nine thousand dollars, which Unique promptly paid.
Heading out to Dee-Dee’s building, Unique rocked his head proudly to the music playing from his tape deck. It was Eric B. & Rakim’s “Paid In Full.” Unique pulled up to Dee-Dee’s building feeling good about his little car all decked out and shining until Dee-Dee came to the car. When she got in the car and closed the door, Unique could see that some nigga was following her and talking shit to her with a smirk on his face. It was Damond.
“Damn, baby, you bounced on me to be with a nigga drivin’ a fuckin’ Mitsubishi?” Damond said, and laughed while balancing a toothpick between his teeth. “This shit is played-out and wack as fuck. What type of broke-ass nigga you got up in there pushin’ some bullshit like this? It’s not even a BMW.” He continued, showing off for the small crowd that had gathered. They were all laughing at Damond’s cracks on Unique’s ride.
Dee-Dee was embarrassed for Unique who seemed to not even be paying any attention to the fool standing outside of his car. “Fuck you, Damond!” Dee-Dee said loud enough for everyone to hear. “You ain’t ever had a car since I knew your silly, broke ass, so get one and then run your mouth.” She looked over to Unique who was smiling for some reason. “Come on baby, let’s go. Don’t pay this fool no mind, ‘cause he ain’t about shit,” she said and gave Damond the middle finger as Unique pulled away with that odd smile still plastered to his face.
After going to lunch together, Unique and Dee-Dee went to get a room. When they got to their room, Unique told her to stay put for a little while and left without telling her where he was going.
All afternoon Unique had been preoccupied thinking about the bullshit Damond had said about his little ride. When he left Dee-Dee at the hotel, he went to his building and took several stacks of money from his fat stash. He didn’t even go to Tony’s to pick up a load of coke as he had earlier planned.
When he returned to the hotel, Dee-Dee was lying across the bed with nothing on but her panties and bra. After talking for a while, they ended up fucking for nearly three hours.
When he dropped her off at her building, he could see that she had a nice time by the way she looked at him all glassy eyed. They agreed to hook back up the next day and Unique pulled off.
When Unique got to Jersey, he went to see his sister Ronnette to give her some money and see what was up with his parole officer. He learned that he was on good grounds with his parole officer who just wanted to be satisfied knowing that he wasn’t in any trouble with the law. He stayed the night at Ronnette’s home and left early the next morning, after hugging her and letting her know to get with him any time she needed something.
He drove to a local BMW dealership in Jersey, and laid out sixty-two thousand in cash for a new black BMW M5. It had shiny chrome trimming, and its interior was soft black leather. The dashboard was filled with digital contraptions that Unique would have to learn about. He had fallen in love with the car on sight.
He took the Mitsubishi to Ronnette and left her the keys and caught a cab back to the dealership to pick up his BMW. After making other small runs, he headed back to New York to resume business and pick up Dee Dee. They were going out to another concert.
When he pulled up to Dee-Dee’s block, he could see heads turning to look at the new BMW. He could see they were wondering who was driving it and who they were coming to see on the block.
When he got in front of Dee-Dee’s building, he pressed the car horn two quick times and waited for her to come out. There was the same crowd gathered that was there the day before. They were all gawking at the new BMW that nobody else in New York had yet. Damond was in the crowd as wide eyed as the others, not knowing who was in the car or who they were waiting for.
When Dee-Dee came out of the building she looked at the new BMW and glanced up and down the street for Unique’s car. He had called to say he was on his way but she didn’t see him anywhere.
Unique saw Dee-Dee’s confused look and smiled, loving the moment. Then he honked the horn once more to get her attention, meanwhile the crowd’s stares were getting more intense. Dee-Dee looked at the BMW again and her confusion turned to pride and surprise when she saw Unique standing up on the edge of the car’s floor with the driver’s side door hanging open. He waved for her to get in.
Walking past the crowd to go to Unique’s new car, Dee-Dee saw Damond who was so shocked all he could do was stand there staring. She couldn’t help herself.
“Hey, Damond!” She called him. Everyone was watching her, including Unique. “Is that the BMW you were talking  about yesterday?” Then she laughed in his face. The crowd followed up laughing at Damond who was speechless. “I guess you opened your mouth a little too quick, huh?” Dee-Dee added and got inside the brand new foreign machine.
Unique was still standing there with the driver side door open. “Hey, yo, homeboy!” he called to Damond.
“What’s up, dog?” Damond answered, thinking maybe he had a chance to be down.
“Check this out and take it how you want it,” Unique said antagonistically. “If you hustled half as strong as you ran ya mouth, maybe you would be rollin’ in some ‘bullshit’ like this,” he continued, patting the roof of his rare M-Series. Then he laughed at Damond setting the crowd off with cracks and laughter at their homey. He and Dee-Dee could hear the crowd riding down on Damond as they headed away. As the car got a short distance away, Unique set the crowd on edge by peeling out, leaving a huge cloud of smoke from the tires burning against the evenly paved street.

Chapter Thirty nine
UNIQUE AND TONY EXECUTES THEIR PLAN OF THE BIG TAKE OVER OF 8th AVENUE

Unique was back on the block hustling and doing his thing when Tony stopped by to check him out about two weeks after the incident with Dee-Dee’s old boyfriend.
“Yo, what’s up, homey?” Unique said, greeting his business partner.
“Not much, Unique,” Tony answered, entering the apartment. “I just stopped by to see how everything is coming along down here. I see you got shit going smoothly.”
“Everything is fly, son. No doubt. Let me show you around the spot, homey.”
After Unique gave Tony a tour of certain parts of the apartment building, Tony was ready to leave.
“I got to step back up the hill. Come and see me later with whatever money you got from the hundred keys I gave you.”
“What’s up, Tony,” Unique asked concerned, seeing the withdrawn expression on his partner’s face, “Everything is tight, ain’t it?”
“Yeah,” Tony said, rubbing his temple with one of his hands. “I just got to finish off the three-hundred keys I got up the hill. Shit is hot up there so everything up there is moving too slow.”
“I’ll tell you what. Just chill here for a minute. I’ll be back through in about an hour.” Unique said. “Just sit tight and make yourself at home. Lock the door behind me, a'ight?”
Then he left quickly.
Fifty minutes later Unique returned and handed Tony the keys to a rent-a-van he had parked outside.
“This is what I want chu to do.” Unique said. “Take the van up the hill ‘cause I just moved the hundred keys and your seven-hundred grand cut is in the van. It’s all there. I’ll keep your Benz down here until you get back. It’ll be parked where you left it.”
Tony was slightly confused. “You tryin’ to tell me that you just pushed a hundred keys in less than an hour?”
“Yeah,” Unique answered, “I got all of it off. Matter of fact, niggas was sick because I ain’t have enough to cover what everybody wanted.”
“Straight up?” Tony asked in disbelief.
“Word is bond!” Unique shot back proudly.
“Well look, since shit is movin’ like that down here, and you’re getting it off so fast, why don’t you take the three-hundred I got up the hill and move them down here so I can pay these people their fuckin’ money. This slow shit up the hill got them worryin’ some and I don’t want them to lose faith in our moves.”
“That’s all good,” Unique responded enthusiastically. “Take the van up the hill, and move your loot out, then fill it up with as many joints as you can fit in it, and bring it back. I’ll make a few phone calls while you’re gone to let niggas know I re’d-up.”
“Cool,” Tony smiled. “I’ll be back in a few.”
Unique saw that when Tony returned with the rent-a-van, his own personal van was behind him. Tony had to pack both vans to get all of the kilos to Unique. He had given the job of driving his own van to his young cousin, Jake, who was just coming up in the game on his own.
Tony took the keys to the van from Jake and handed Unique the keys to both vans, then he was on his way back out of the apartment when Unique stopped him.
“Yo, where you goin’? Just chill up in here. I got enough niggas to serve to get both vans empty before the end of the night. Y’all can sit here and chill while I go take care of business. That way we can save an unnecessary trip later.”
When he left and came back four hours later, Tony and Jake were chilling comfortably on the sofa watching a boxing match on Unique’s big screen television.
Unique handed Tony the keys to his van and kept the rent-a-van keys. Then he sat down on his large leather chair and exhaled heavily.
“Your money from the three hundred keys is all in the van. I already took my cut out of it. I moved all that shit. Niggas is dumped up!” Unique exclaimed with a smile, before continuing. “They got all they wanted and paid off the top so don’t nobody owe us nothin’, you know how that go.”
Tony didn’t show how elated he was over how fast Unique had gotten rid of the kilos, even though, just hours before he was at a virtual standstill up the hill.
When he got back to his building, Tony gave Jake fifty-grand for his assistance moving the weight. Then he went inside to piece together his next moves considering how quick Unique was spreading out the work down in Harlem. After realizing that his partner had gotten rid of nearly three million dollar’s worth of material in one day, he also recognized that his partner was a gold mine. He planned to have a talk with Unique to let him know that he would be stepping up the pace. He decided that he would do it later that night and, at the same time, also let Unique know that since he had moved about two weeks’ worth of weight, he would have to rest and relax until things slowed back down, down in Harlem so order can be maintained. Nurture demand and provide supply. Tony was pumped up about the work he had witnessed.

Chapter fourty
UNIQUE VISITS KENNY IN NEW JERSEY

After sleeping for nearly an entire day, Unique woke up, and with nothing else to do, he began his daily non-working day routine, beginning with a hot shower. While in the shower, his mind drifted to his mother whom had stood by him during his incarceration.
She lived in Miami and Unique hadn’t seen her since he was released from prison.
Drying himself -off and wrapping himself with one of his Ralph Lauren bathrobes, he smiled to himself. His thoughts of his mother had given him an idea. “I’ll go check her out.”
He thought. “As a matter of fact, I can check Kenny out before I go down to Miami.” His smile grew wider with the thought of seeing his brother and mother.
A little after one o’clock he called Ronnette and told her that he was coming to Jersey, and he asked her to call Kenny. After talking to his sister for a few more minutes, he hung up the telephone. Unique got dressed, and then went into his hidden safe and removed twenty-grand from it. He placed the money inside of an empty shoebox and went to catch a cab to the parking garage where he kept his new BMW.
Three hours later, Unique pulled up in front of Ronnette’s house in New Jersey. When he entered the house, he was briefly shocked when he saw his brother and how much he had changed. After a brief but meaningful embrace, the two brothers sat down and began to talk. Ronnette looked at her two little brothers, and she was happy to see them both in her home.
“So what’s been goin’ on?” Unique asked Kenny. “I see you got the company truck outside. How is everything goin’ wit’ the business?”
With that, Kenny dropped his head, not really wanting to say what was really on his mind about their father’s furniture company that Kenny himself practically ran.
“Well, all is okay, I guess.” Kenny finally replied. “It’s not seeming to be paying well, but business is going perfectly other than that.”
That’s all Unique needed to hear to know that their father was taking advantage of Kenny. Unique always had a disliking towards their father, Chauncey, for a multitude of reasons. What he had just heard from Kenny only enhanced his negative feelings.
“Oh, I see what it is,” Unique said. “But don’t sweat it, bro. I got a little something to help you out.” Unique looked directly into Kenny’s eyes as a way of letting him know that he sincerely understood the situation that he was in with their father’s company. “Come ride with me right quick. I got a trip to take, but first I want to get right. You know I can’t leave town without any gear.”
“A’ight,” Kenny said meekly. “Let’s go.”
Kenny needed all the help he could get at the moment since his father wasn’t paying him his just due for the work he did every day running the business.
“Where y’all going?” Ronnette asked, watching the two of them get up from their seats to leave.
“Nowhere but the mall.” Unique answered, reaching into his pocket. He pulled out a roll of money and gave it to his sister. She smiled and put the money into her own pocket.
_____________________________________________
 “Can you show us where the men’s department is?” Unique said to a sales girl who was in the store where he planned to do some of his shopping.
“It’s straight to the back, sir, you can’t miss it.” The girl replied in passing.
“Thanks.” Unique said already on his way to the back of the store with Kenny in tow.
Kenny had never shopped in this store because he couldn’t afford the expensive clothing they sold. He never kept up with the hot new street fashions for the same reason so he just planned on watching his brother shop, then follow suit purchasing similar clothes for himself, since he now had the money to splurge a little. Before they entered the mall Unique had taken some money from the shoebox and given Kenny the remainder. It was about fifteen-grand. More money than Kenny had ever had at one time before.
When Unique caught sight of another sales girl, he told her he was taking a trip to Miami and needed clothes for the trip. He wanted her to help him pick out a few things. Within twenty minutes, they had both chosen the clothes they liked and were ready to leave. Unique instructed their sales girl to also pick out a few items for herself, as well as the young woman who rang up their purchases did. After paying for their new gear, Unique had an extra five hundred dollars left from the money he had set aside for the small spree. He started to put the money in his pocket then stopped and looked at the pretty sales girl.
“So, what’s your name?” he asked her.
“It’s Jane,” the sales girl responded with a smile while pointing to the nametag on her shirt.
“You have any kids?” Unique asked.
The question caught Jane off guard since she had never had too many men ask that question as part of their introduction. “Yeah, I got a little girl. She’s three.” Jane answered.
Unique placed the five hundred dollars on the counter and pushed it towards her.
“Well, take that and buy her something nice.” Unique said. “Oh, thank you, sir.” Jane said smiling gratefully.
“Just call me, Unique.”
“Okay, Unique. Thank you, and please come again.” Jane said, smiling even bigger.
“Will you be here if I come again?” Unique asked Jane. “I sure will try,” she responded.
“In that case, I will try to come again,” he told her. Then he and Kenny left the mall carrying their bags. Back in the BMW, heading towards Ronnette’s house to drop off Kenny, Unique asked Kenny to give him the run-down on everything with their father’s furniture company.
Kenny told him in full detail about how he himself ran the company but received practically no pay for his work, even after he complained to his father about it many times. He told Unique about his girlfriend Pat and their intentions of moving to another state together all of which Chauncey disagreed with, because he wanted Kenny to stay in New Jersey to continue running the company even though he received no pay for it. Kenny related to Unique about how he secretly had to go out and find a part time job as a janitor in a high school just to save up for him and Pat’s move, which would probably never come with the low payments the high school gave him every two weeks.
“Yo, check this out,” Unique interrupted after he had heard enough. “The money I just gave you, I want you to keep that shit and do what’chu gotta do wit’ it. You and Pat sound serious and you’re my brother, so I hate to see our own sorry-ass dad taking advantage of you and keeping you from doin what you need to do wit’cha girl. Don’t even tell him about the money, just take it, scoop your girl up and bounce to wherever it is y’all want to be. That ain’t shit but fifteen grand, but it’s more than enough to start off with and I’ll be hittin’ you off on the regular as long as you need it and you’re down to get paid. You’ve been workin’ yo ass off from day one for that bastard and now you see the light, so bounce. Now you see why I never liked that fuckin’ bum. He ain’t no father of mine. That’s on the real!”
When they got back to Ronnette’s house, Unique wrote down his cell phone number and his beeper number with a personal code for Kenny. He gave Kenny the paper and let him know to contact him whenever he needed him. “I’m goin’ down to see Mommy,” he said to Kenny. “Let me know what’s going on up here when I get back or I’ll even call you when I get to Miami. Make sure that nigga don’t see a dime of yo money, bro’. Just vanish away from this bullshit without looking back.”
That’s just what Kenny did the very next day. He picked his girlfriend up and they went straight to the airport carrying nothing but their new money.

Chapter forty one
UNIQUE GOES TO THE BRONX FOR A SURPRISE VISIT TO HANDLE HIS BUSINESS

Back at his building in the City, Unique called his mother in Miami through the number Ronnette had given him. His mother was surprised to hear his voice and ecstatic to know that he was coming to Miami to see her. When he got off the phone, Unique went to his stash and filled a small gym bag with a hundred thousand dollars for the trip.
He had purchased his clothes for the trip three days earlier and left them in the BMW, so when he left the building all he had in his hands were keys and a gym bag.
Unique bought an open round trip ticket to Miami at the Pan Am Airlines window. Minutes later he boarded the plane and made himself comfortable for the flight.
Nearly dozing off early in the flight, Unique began thinking of how it was best for him to leave New York for a while because of some business he was forced to handle the night before...
_____________________________________________
That night he had veered off into his old neighborhood in the Bronx where he had been involved in the robbery of Tony’s old boss.
While back in the neighborhood, Unique caught sight of an old-time petty hustler named Derrick. Derrick and some of his boys had tried to rob Unique during the time when he was healing up from his gunshot wounds.
Back then Unique had vowed to get revenge against Derrick and his nickel and dime partners.
Now, seeing Derrick, and his crew sitting on the hood of a new Mercedes Benz 500 SEL all of his old feelings of disgust and need for revenge came over him so thick and intensely that he nearly blacked out from anxiety. He would later find out that Derrick’s success in the game had come when he took over the herb gates after Ron had moved out and ran off with Unique’s money. Derrick had quit smoking coke  shortly after he began selling crack out of Unique’s old weed spot. His success came swiftly and continued throughout the five years that Unique was in prison.
Unique continued driving around the corner and parked his BMW by the curb. When he got out of the car, he pulled out two 9-millimeter pistols he had tucked in the front of his waistline. He approached the unsuspecting men from behind and whipped one of his guns up pressing the barrel against Derrick’s head while the other two men looked on surprised and scared nearly to death.
Wasting no time at all, Unique emptied the clip of his other gun into the heads and bodies of Derrick’s boys and looked into Derrick’s terrified eyes as his partners fell dead to the ground.
“Remember me, muthafucka?” Unique asked Derrick. Adrenaline was surging through him rapidly, his eyes were widened with rage, and spittle flew from his mouth into Derrick’s face as he spoke.
“You think I forgot about the bullshit you pulled, nigga? Huh?!”
“Look, man,” Derrick began pleadingly, shaking in fear. “I was fucked up back then off coke. I always had respect for your hustle, Mike, man. I mean that shit. I was just fucked up. Don’t kill me, Mike. I’m on some different shit now I’m gettin’ money, man, and I’ll break you off, homey. Just let me live. Please.”
“You know the rules nigga… you know the rules.” With those final words, Unique let two rounds fly through Derrick’s head, at point blank range. Then he calmly fled the scene.
_____________________________________________
 “Please fasten your seat belts. The plane will be landing in fifteen minutes.” The Captain’s voice came loudly over the plane’s P.A. system. This snapped Unique out of his sleep.
He looked around him to get a good look at his surroundings and remember where he was.
When the plane landed, everyone got off and retrieved their luggage. Unique grabbed his own and proceeded out of the airport to one of the many cabs that were lined up along the curb. Getting into the cab, Unique handed the driver a piece of paper with his mother’s address on it. The driver pulled off and headed in the direction of Trina McCormick’s neighborhood.

Chapter forty two
UNIQUE DECIDES TO UPGRADE HIS MOTHER, TRINA'S HOUSE

Pulling up to a small one-story home, Unique got out of the cab and knocked firmly on the wooden front door. A short, thin older Cuban woman answered as she opened the door.
“Hello there, you must be looking for Trina. She lives in the back of the house.”
“Yes, I’m looking for her, but how did ya’ know that, ma’am?”
The Cuban woman chuckled and rubbed her hand from the front of her long hair to the back where her hair was tied into a long ponytail.
“Well,” she said, “If you don’t look just like your mother, I don’t know who does. I’ve heard a whole lot about you, Unique. Isn’t that your name?”
Unique chuckled to himself, and felt good knowing that this little Cuban woman knew so much about him because his mother loved him enough to talk so much about him. “Yes, that’s me,” he responded.
The Cuban woman extended her hand to him. “My name is Maria,” she said as Unique shook her hand. “Come on in, Unique, I’m more than sure that your mother will be pleasantly surprised to see you.”
“Thank you,” Unique replied and entered the home with his bags in tow and headed to the back to find his mother whom he hadn’t seen in months.
Trina had heard the cab pull up and by the time Unique made it to the back door Trina came running to him saying his name and smiling big. She threw herself into his arms. “My baby!” She said holding on to him.
Unique smiled and said playfully, “Come on, Mom, don’t start that mushy shit.”
Trina paid this protest no mind as she squeezed him tighter. She never had any problems showing her emotions. Before she finally tore herself away from her son, he planted a soft kiss on her forehead.
Walking around the house, Unique looked around at his mother’s meager belongings and felt sorry for her that she had been living in such a small house with almost nothing inside of it. All of these feelings vanished quickly and were replaced with the inevitable. “I’ll fix this shit,” he thought to himself. He looked at his mother and said, “Damn, Ma, you could’a told me you was crammed up in this little place.”
“Boy, you know me. As long as my house is clean, and I got a roof over my head, I’m just fine.”
“I know, Mom, but you know I want the best for my mother.”
“Yes, I know, baby,” she said, beaming at her son’s love for her. “But this is all your mother can afford.”
“I’ll take care of this thing here, ‘cause I ain’t goin’ for my mother livin’ up in no house that’s so small you can barely fit a deck of cards in it. Don’t worry, Ma, I got this handled.”
Trina smiled. “Boy, stop talking about my little house and go wash up. The bathroom is over there.” She said pointing.
“Shit,” Unique said smiling, “As little as this place is, I can see where the bathroom is, and where everything else is, from right here.” Then he went into the bathroom to prepare for bed. He had a busy day ahead of him.
Trina and Unique stayed up all night talking about the days to come.
“Yo, Ma, if you won the lottery what type of crib would you want and what type of furniture would you put into it?”
Trina thought about that question for a brief second then reached under her bed and pulled out two opened magazines. She handed them to Unique. The first magazine was an updated issue of a weekly Miami real estate magazine and the second was actually a catalogue from one of Miami’s prestigious furniture companies. Trina had spent a little bit of her time going through these articles and she had circled what she liked best out of both of them even though she knew she would never experience living in such an expensive atmosphere. She had her dreams, and these articles allowed her to see them.
“These books are nice,” Unique said, keeping his eyes down in the catalogue so that his mother wouldn’t detect the anxiety he was going through. “Can I have them?”
Trina smiled. “Yes, baby, you can have them. I can get more tomorrow.”
“Cool,” Unique replied. He turned out the lights in the small bedroom and kissed his mother on the cheek. “I love you, Mommy,” he said before taking his place on the floor beside her little bed where he drifted off to sleep.
As he breathed evenly in his sleep, Trina laid in her bed feeling better than she had felt in years. “I may not have much,” she thought to herself, “but laying down there beside me in this bedroom is the best thing I could ever have, my son!” She kissed his forehead and went to sleep.
When daylight came Trina got up, showered, and left for work at the flowerpot factory where she was employed.
Unique woke up and found his mother gone and relaxed for a minute before preparing for his day. After showering he got dressed and began making telephone calls.
First he called the furniture company and made arrangements to get all of the furniture and accessories Trina had circled put aside to be delivered to an address he would call later to have everything shipped to. Then he called the owner of the home for sale that Trina had circled. He was told all about the home and had no question that he was going to buy it. He wanted it for his mother.
“So, how much are you asking for the house?” he asked.
The owner on the other line responded, “We’re asking for two hundred and twenty thousand to be paid over thirty years on a set mortgage plan.”
 “Would you take a hun’ed-and-fifty thousand in cash?”
“We most certainly will,” the owner answered with a chuckle.
“Okay,” Unique said. “I’ll give you fifty-thousand right now and the rest when you move out after our lawyers complete the necessary paperwork. Is that a'ight?”
“Sure, what time should we expect you?”
“Within the next two hours. I want to check the place out to see how I’ll arrange it.”
“Well, you’re in luck because me and my wife had everything moved out just yesterday. We’ve already moved. You caught me on my way out to have the phone number transferred to our new place. I’ll stay and wait for you. Did you say your name was Unique?”
“Yeah, that’s me.”
“Okay, Unique, I’ll see you in a few.”
After retaining an attorney to take care of the paperwork, Unique called the furniture company and gave them the address to his mother’s new home.
“That’s 138 West Exchange Drive?” the delivery Supervisor asked.
“Yes,” Unique replied.
“Everything will be there within the hour, Sir.”
“Okay, thanks,” Unique said.
“No, Sir,” the delivery supervisor responded. “Thank you. I get extra pay on commission.” The home was huge. There were three large bedrooms and a very large master bedroom, and each bedroom had its own bathroom. There was also a bathroom downstairs. The sunlight from outside shined into the windows of the spacious living room. The kitchen was completely modernized with contraptions Unique doubted he would ever learn to operate. There were operations manuals on the grand-sized kitchen counter for all of the kitchen appliances. The top-of-the-line central air conditioning system gave the entire home a perfectly cooled atmosphere to rival the muggy Miami heat. With the exception of the kitchen, the entire home was covered in wall-to-wall plush carpeting that matched the cream colored walls.
There was a big yard in front of the property where the “For Sale” sign had previously been placed. There was a broad three-car garage connected to the left side of the house. In the back, there was a large swimming pool and a Jacuzzi with a large barbecue grill in the corner. Unique observed everything as the owner gave him the tour of the home. He fell in love with the place the second he walked into it and as he gave the fifty grand to the owner he told him so. The owner gave him two sets of keys that were once his and his wife’s.
“We can have the rest of the payment arranged for next week. I’ll have my attorney contact yours and everything will be done. As of now, the place is all yours.”
“Thanks,” Unique answered. “I’ll be movin’ in tonight if it’s not a problem.”
“It’s all yours, you can move in any time you wish. I’ll be leaving now. You have my phone number and my attorney’s, and I have yours. Been nice doing business with you.” Then the gentleman turned and left Unique standing alone in the living room of Trina’s new home.
After the furniture company arrived, Unique showed them exactly where he wanted everything to be placed. Once the home was completely furnished six hours later, Unique called a cab and went to the nearest gift shop. Then he had the cab driver take him to a car rental company where he rented an all black, convertible Chrysler LeBaron, and from there he went to a pawn shop that was right down the street from the rental car company. He paid the clerk there seven hundred dollars for a gun that only cost four hundred and fifty dollars, just so he wouldn’t have to give any I.D. or fill out any paperwork. He also bought five boxes of bullets.
He wanted to go to the Projects but didn’t know the directions yet. He wasn’t far from Trina’s little home, so he drove back there where his mother would arrive at any minute from work.
He stretched out on the old tattered couch in her small living room until he heard her old Buick pull up outside. Then he got up to meet her at the front door. On the way, he reached into the pocket of his navy blue Nike sweats and pulled out a small giftwrapped box with a red bow.
When Trina opened the front door, Unique stopped her. “Get a good look at this mouse hole, Mommy, this is the last time you're going to see it.”
Trina was confused. “Baby, what are you talking about? And whose car is that in front of the house?”
“Oh, that’s just a rental car I got a little while ago,” Unique said, smiling. He handed her the small box. “Go ahead, open it.”
Trina opened up the box and the first thing she saw was a cutout of the home she had circled in her magazine. The home she had daydreamed about living in.
She took the cutout from the box and was both shocked and confused to see two sets of keys in the bottom of the box. She looked up into her son’s eyes and began to cry.
“Wait until you see it, it’s so much bigger than that piece of paper.” Unique said laughingly, then he reached out and held his mother.
“Oh, baby,” Trina said. “I won’t believe it until I see it.”
“Well, lady,” Unique said, “You ain’t said nothin’, let’s go. It’s close by so you can follow me to your place. Don’t bring anything but your beautiful self.” With that he headed to his rental car and pulled out into the street smiling as he saw his mother’s old Buick right behind him. Trina followed her son’s car and said a silent prayer for his safety. She knew his lifestyle and loved him in spite of it. She was so proud of him that she couldn’t even think straight as she pulled her old Buick in the driveway of the home she had once fantasized about. The home her son had given to her out of love. Her new home.
She laughed and cried simultaneously while running up to her home to get a look inside of it. Unique had made her dreams a reality. She nearly fainted looking around at all of the furniture she had dreamed of owning and felt she would never have. Now she had it all.
“Thank you God,” Trina said out loud.

Chapter Forty three
UNIQUE MEETS MERCEDES, PORSCHE AND LEXUS

After spending a few hours with his mother, in her new home, Unique hopped back into the convertible and headed back to his mother’s old home to close it up once and for all.
On his way back to her new home, Unique stopped at a red light at an intersection where a Jeep Wrangler pulled up next to him, its stereo blasting a bass-filled Luke Skywalker song. The Jeep was filled with four attractive women. The driver caught Unique glancing in their direction and reached to turn down her music.
“Hey, baby, what’s up?” she said loudly.
Unique took this as an opportunity to recruit someone to show him how to get around Miami.
“Not much of nothing,” Unique answered.
The girl responded back in a sweet, southern accent, “You ain’t goin over to the block party in the projects?”
“You inviting me?”
Just then the red light turned green, and the girl motioned for Unique to pull over into the supermarket parking lot. “Follow me,” she said. Unique followed.
Once in the parking lot the driver got out of the Jeep so Unique got out to get a good look at her. He wanted to see if there was any truth to what he had heard about the bodies of young southern gals. The girl had a set of tits, hips, and ass that were so thick and in place that Unique had to wonder if she really knew what she was working with.
“What’s your name?” she asked as she leaned back and her firm behind pushed against the Jeep while her passengers looked on.
“Unique.”
“Unique, huh?” she asked with a cute laugh. “Well, Unique, you must not be from around here. My name is Shantel, and there go my sisters up there. That’s Mercedes, Porsche and Lexus.”
Unique chuckles. “Those are some pretty nice and um...expensive names.”
This brought laughter from all four women.
“So,” Shantel started again, “where are you from?”
“New York.”
“Well that’s cool. We got a block party jumpin’ off in the projects and yeah, I’m inviting you, Mr. Unique. All you gotta do is follow the Jeep.”
“Cool with me,” Unique replied, and they were all on their way.
Unique walked behind his four hosts and observed his surroundings, taking in the southern flavor of the get-together. “Now, that’s what I’m talkin’ ‘bout!” he thought to himself, looking at the huge mountains of concert style speakers stacked together beside the section where the party’s DJ was posted up. There was a thick ocean of mostly smiling and laughing black faces. There were masses of people standing around smoking weed, dancing or sitting down on car hoods chatting. Everyone appeared to know everyone else and all were having a good time, including Unique.
The females were all scantily clad in revealing outfits but it was the very short cut-off jean shorts that caught Unique’s attention.
“What type of shorts are those?” Unique asked, leaning close to Shantel’s ear so she could hear him over the party noise. He was pointing at a thick female who was lost in a zone shaking her sexy-plump ass to the rhythm of the music.
“Those are Daisy Dukes,” Shantel responded laughing. “They don’t wear them up in New York?”
“Nah…and what’s that dance she’s doin’? Damn!”
“That’s the DooDoo Brown.”
“What about her?” Unique asked, pointing to another thick female.
